# Reference agent: plan

A test agent for creating characters and running them against an Agent Realm world. It is also the seed of the public [reference agent](https://agentrealm.gg/) the site quick start calls for: Python first, an example rather than an SDK.

It is outside the formal backlog. It is built interactively and changes as the API does.

## Boundaries

- **An ordinary API client.** HTTP only. It imports nothing from the Go code and never touches Postgres, Redis, or NATS. If it needs something the API does not give, that is a server gap, written down below, not a side door.
- **Python 3.11+, standard library only.** No install step beyond Python. The AI planner (M4, A35) is the one place a dependency may enter (the `anthropic` SDK), imported only when the planner runs, so `--no-planner` and `make test` stay standard library only.
- **Obeys the invariants a client can see.** At most one intent per tick, no standing orders, and when there is no decision it sends nothing.

## Architecture: AI plans, state machine executes

The agent is a fast algorithmic layer under a slower planner (A61, the redirect of 2026-10-06). The planner decides *what* to do; the state machine decides *how*, tick by tick. No state picks its own goal.

- **The plan stack** comes from the AI planner (the strategist, A35), on in every live run; directives `goals` (manual steering) outrank it. With the planner on nothing else fills the stack. Only in the `--no-planner` test mode does the built-in planner turn `policy.goals` into ops (`plan.builtin_goals`). Its top op is the one thing being done.
- **Reflexes** act on what is happening now, whatever the plan says: Sync, Downed, Escape, Ashore (under attack on water with only a fording supply in hand, make for the nearest land, A133), Retreat, Heal, Fight, Flee, Pickup (a supply underfoot or adjacent), Recover, Detour (a valuable in view worth the steps it adds to the walk, A71, A73) Greet (a one-tick hello to a likely helper in sight not yet spoken to, sent only at a decision window, never by the held-queue probe, A65) and UseItem (a bigger chest held is used on yourself, with no hostile in range and never by the held-queue probe; also the `use_item` op's executor, A108).
- **Executors** run only to carry out the top op (`plan.OP_STATE`): Equip, Loot (`fetch_item`), Shop (`buy`), Investigate (`read`, `say`), Break (`break_block`), Solve (`use_block`, `compose`), Gather (`gather_gems`), Travel (`travel`), Boss (`fight_boss`), Level (`enter_level`), Wait (`wait`) and Explore (`explore_area`). Each guard asks `my_op`: is the top op mine?
- **The safe default.** With no op, Explore explores safe ground: off hazards, away from hostiles, and when hurt inside safe zones first. Once hurt-safe ground is all explored it pushes the boundary: the nearest frontier outside, still off hazards and away from hostiles, with the survival reflexes above it (live Walk run: "look around" took 18% of decisions with unexplored ground 150 blocks east). With no reachable frontier at all, the ground in reach is all seen and the way on is the planner's call: it never steps in place and never parks because a hostile stands next to it; it raises one `no_way_on` trigger for the cell and sends nothing, and the planner reads State `map` (the ground round the character, cell by cell) and the clues and names the block to break, the hostile to fight or the place to go (A114). It never stands in for an executor (A120): under any op but `explore_area` it holds, and the op's own executor keeps the walk (Travel's through unseen ground too) and clears the way, as the next bullet says. Hurt, with a known hostile within the planner's danger radius (6, `explore.CAUTION_RADIUS`), it explores nothing: it steps to the neighbour that gets furthest from it, safe-ish ground first, or holds when none gets further, only while that hostile is closing in (`survival.approaching`) and for at most 10 s, before it explores again (`explore.keep_away`; free-play run 6 started hurt beside a gristlewick and walked into it before the first plan landed; run 8 stood off one that kept its distance for 177 s, A82). A non-forced decision always has a productive intent. The only waits are forced ones (downed, asleep or unplaced, server-imposed), the safe default holding for the planner when nothing is left to explore (A114), an executor holding for the planner with its way shut or nothing to work on (A120), and the planner's `wait` op, which must say `why` and lasts at most 30 s.
- **An obstructed op clears its own way (A120).** Evan's rule: "if a goal or target is obstructed we need to try to unobstruct it, not get distracted". Every executor with a target works one ladder (`states/way.py`): its walk routes round (blocks, hostiles' reach, through unseen ground too); with no way round it opens the first block on the cheapest way that a held item is known to open (`way.open_the_way`: `break_toward`, the opener table; once the walk has no step only a way round over known ground counts, with unexplored ground beyond the block at fog cost, A129), or, on a walk on the stuck ladder (Travel, Shop's walk to a seller, Level, Boss, A15), Break opens the nominated block at step 2; a hostile on the way is the engagement's to fight (Fight acts above every executor) or the planner's with a `fight` op (A114), never the executor's own; only then does it raise one `no_way_on` saying why, what blocks the way (`blocked_by`: hostiles, the first block and whether a held item opens it) and what it tried (`tried`: this op's own searches, `Use`s and breaks recorded since it reached the top, never what is merely held, A129), and hold (`ask.ask_the_planner`, `way.clear_the_way`). An op with nothing to work on (no such item or NPC in sight, nowhere to gather, an item or fragment missing, a boss op's conditions not met) asks the same way. The survival reflexes (Retreat, Heal, Fight, Flee) act above every hold; unanswered, the op stalls and is moved below (A104). A `fight` op's ask holds nothing, so Flee below it stays free. With `--no-planner` the built-in plan answers every such ask by exploring round the cell (`plan.builtin_way_on`).
- **Exactly one thing picks the movement target** at any moment: a reflex, the top op's executor, or the safe default. States never take turns moving the character, so the A58 runs 3–7 pacing pairs cannot form; the oscillation guard (A15) stays as a safety net that should not fire.
- **Commit to the target and the goal it serves (A71, `targets.py`).** A target, once picked, is kept until it is reached (or gone), proven impossible (no path at all, or stuck detection's give-up), or the op it serves changes, or its walk balloons: its price rose since last priced and it costs more than the cheapest other candidate by a clear margin (`targets.outpriced`, A109); never because another candidate came nearer on its own, and not when another state walked in between. A prerequisite (a key before a locked entrance) is a stop inserted before the target, which stays; a valuable in view worth the steps it adds to the way is a detour (A73), after which the walk resumes toward the same target. A new head op from the planner waits for the action under way to end. So no decision undoes the previous one without new information.
- **Stall clock.** An op whose executor makes no progress for 30 s is moved just below the next op on the stack that can make progress, and dropped when none can (`goal_stalled` or `goal_failed` to the planner), so the stack never pins the agent. Its target and why are remembered, and it is not put back on top until something gives a reason to retry: a new item held, a new clue, a new way known (A104).

## What the API gives today

| Call | Use |
|---|---|
| `POST /worlds/{code}/characters` `{name, avatar, model_agent}` | Create. 201 returns self. |
| `GET /characters/{id}/self` | Lives, health, perception range, movement speed, alive, placed. |
| `GET /characters/{id}/position` | `{map_id, x, y}`, with no `level` (that is on the minimap). 409 `not_on_map` until placed. |
| `GET /characters/{id}/world` | Tick rate, sandbox flag, status. |
| `GET /characters/{id}/terrain-tiles?map_id&x0&y0&width&height` | Block types inside perception, plus revealed ground, as a grid: `rows` of `legend` symbols, `?` for clouds. |
| `GET /characters/{id}/entity-tiles?…` | Characters, NPCs, supplies inside perception. |
| `GET /characters/{id}/zone?map_id&x&y` | Zone at a revealed cell: `safe`, `brightness`, and a hunting ground's `strength_ceiling`. |
| `GET /characters/{id}/minimap` | Every revealed map's size, its `level` (positive inside a level), and level entrance marks (`entrances`: `{x, y}` per map). |
| `POST /characters/{id}/tick` `{"intents": [{...}], "snapshot_version": N}` | Replaces the character's queue with an ordered list (`[]` clears it; no `intents` leaves it running). Optional `snapshot_version` is the observation version last applied; the server answers with a delta when it still matches. Returns `queue_id`, `queue` while it is held, `finished_queue` once it ran to its end, `intent_results` since the last call (none for an applied `Wait`, B133), events by tick, dropped count, the observation, and the clock. |

Auth is `Authorization: Bearer <key>`.

A `Use` target `{"kind": "npc", "npc_id": N}` names the block that NPC stands on when the `Use` runs ([API rules § Use](https://agentrealm.gg/docs/api)). It ships in server release 1.11 (saims B126); **Fight**'s NPC swings depend on it (A45).

**One request per character per tick, with a burst of 3, on every character route.** Reads count. The limiter is a token bucket refilled on the front's tick interval ([Manual §7.4](https://agentrealm.gg/docs/manual#74-rate-limits)). So the agent runs a call budget: each tick it spends its call on the read it most needs or on the tick submit. The round trip carries the observation (B15). This agent reads ground chest contents and its own `health` / `max_health` from it; **Retreat** (A9) uses health with the threat table and directive params, and **Heal** (A10) uses it when hurt and out of combat. Each `Damaged` event also updates a threat table, after the same response's observation is applied: max damage per hit per hostile type, keyed by the source's type code, with an unmeasured default until something is measured (A6). A hit whose source is not among the entities perceived before or after that response, or has no type code, is not recorded. Trap and `occupy` damage is recorded under its own keys but never raises the default for an unmeasured hostile. **Fight** (A23) will add the full win estimate and margins. Terrain and entity reads still compete with intents for the same budget.

Every request on a character route spends from one client-side copy of that bucket (`pacer.py`, A98): `Client._call` takes a token before it sends, whoever the caller is (a decision, a held-queue poll, a startup or wake read, an error retry, the park's clear), so no path can leave the budget. The copy refills one token per wall-clock window (`epoch / tick interval`), at most 3, and about one a second while the character sleeps; the world read sets the window, and before it the pacer assumes 1 s. A 429 empties it and holds off for its `Retry-After`. The runner's loop waits out the same pacer's windows and decides one call per window, so the burst is only ever spent by startup reads and catching up after a slow round trip.

## Real time

Worlds run at 10 ticks per second by default. The agent's shape already fits: the planner is the slow loop and writes plans off the tick, and the reflexes are the fast executor. The intent queue (B98) lets one request carry up to four seconds of intents, run one per tick; the agent sends movement as a paced `Step`/`Wait` queue, `Use` and `Say`/`Broadcast` through the attack and speech pacers, and most other intents as a one-intent queue (M6). A reflex that fires while a queue runs replaces it (see **Reflexes**). When a read or event makes the rest of a running path wrong, position is re-read and the next poll replaces the queue with a walk replanned from there, or with an empty queue when no route is left. Only a cell that became blocked after the queue was sent counts, so a blocker the plan could not avoid does not cost a resend every poll (A43). `run` does not send `Sleep` (B45) when it stops, so a stopped character stays standing until auto-sleep takes it off the map. See [Real-time play](https://agentrealm.gg/docs/guides/create-a-character-agent#real-time-play).

## Architecture

```
            ┌──────────── one call per window ────────────┐
 scheduler ─┤ read position | read terrain | read entities | POST tick(intent) │
            └──────────────────────┬──────────────────────┘
                                   ▼
                              world model
                   (per map: tiles, occupants, age of each read;
                    self; recent events; last intent + result)
                                   ▼
   planner  ──sets──►  goal + settings  ──►  plan (path)  ──►  reflexes  ──►  intent or none
 (scripted / llm,                              A* over known                (every window,
  slow, off-tick)                              walkable tiles                no model)
```

### Scheduler: which call this window

Checked top to bottom:

1. Position unknown, or a door or death may have moved us → read position. Not while off the map (A5, A58 run 9): after `Died`, or when the last self read says `placed: false`, position only answers 409 `not_on_map`. Poll `tick` instead, which still carries events; `Respawned` re-reads self, then position. While the last self read says alive but not placed, also re-read self every 10 windows, in case placement brings no event (`GetSelf` `placed`: GAME_NOTES.md Death, respawn, sleep).
2. Terrain around us is stale (map changed, or we moved more than half the perception range since the last terrain read) → read terrain.
3. Entities are older than the character's `entity_refresh` ticks, or a `Damaged`/`Attacked` event just arrived → read entities. A tick delta that carries entities counts as fresh, but once we have moved, a real read also comes due `entity_refresh` ticks after the last one: deltas alone let a walk run into hostiles it had not read (A16 Walk run 4, `WorldModel.entity_read_due`).
4. Otherwise → `POST tick` with the chosen intent, or with none, when the cadence below says it is due; else a pending `get_zone` on a revealed cell around a respawn anchor or along the path (A7), or send nothing this window.

`POST tick` runs on two cadences (M6). Urgent, meaning a hostile within 3 blocks, a `Damaged`/`Attacked` not yet re-read, or `Damaged` in the last round trip: every window. Calm: every 4–10 ticks, never later than the intents still queued run out. A paced movement queue opens the gap up to its length; a one-intent queue still brings the next poll a tick later. A window the gap skips sends nothing unless step 4 has a zone probe ready. The reads above outrank it, so a calm gap's spare windows go to stale terrain first, then stale entities, then safe-tile discovery. Each window counts as one tick, so a skipped window still brings the next poll and `entity_refresh` due.

Self is re-read after `Died`, and every 60 windows otherwise.

Movement goes as a paced `Step`, `Wait`×n, … queue along the path, cut at the world's horizon, with no trailing `Wait`s (M6). The waits per step are the ticks per move at `movement_speed`, rounded up. The next queue opens with the `Wait`s still owed since the last applied `Step`, counted to the latest tick a server response reported, not the windows counted locally since, so it never draws `movement_cooldown`. Position follows each `Step` result as it arrives, not the submit. A result counts only if it names the `queue_id` our own submit was answered with; a late result for an earlier queue is ignored, never adopted, even when the submit's response named no queue. An applied `Wait` has no result (B133), so results are never counted against the intents sent: progress is the highest result index and `queue.next_index`, and the queue is over only when a `finished_queue` names our `queue_id` or one of its results is rejected. A `finished_queue` naming any other queue is ignored. A lone intent (a `Take`, an unpaced `Use` or `Say`) is held the same way until its result or `finished_queue`, so it is never decided and sent again before it has run (A63 run 3: 20 of 22 `Take`s went twice). While that queue is still running, the window sends nothing unless a reflex below fires or a read or event makes the rest of the path wrong (`BlockChanged`, an entity on the path, a terrain read that disagrees, or a known hostile whose `hostile_range` now reaches a cell still to walk: A63 run 4, Heal walked a 10-`Step` queue into a known pack). Hostiles are counted, not cells (`brain.path_threats`). Three kinds never make a queue stale: the fight's group and the last hitter, which follow the walk anyway; any hostile the walk was planned without, which is every hostile for a losing Retreat (`Memory.path_skip`); and a hostile already in reach when the queue was sent, which the replan could not avoid. So a hostile moving while in reach is no new threat, and the queue is resent only for a hostile not yet counted. A cell already blocked when the queue was sent does not count either. In those cases the next poll replaces the queue with a replanned walk. The poll that looks for a firing reflex (`dispatch.probe_held_queue`, run by the runner) runs only the states that can answer with one (`dispatch.PROBE_STATES`: the reflexes but Greet, then Boss once a boss fight is engaged; in the park phase, `PARK_STATES`), never an executor's search, and when none fires the state that sent the queue stays the active one, so a poll stays quick while a queue runs (A64). The probe leaves no trace in memory: `Memory` is snapshot before it and put back whole when its answer is not sent (its corridor searches aside: they cache search work, not decisions, and copying them would cost more than the probe; `memory.SEARCH_CACHES`), so a pick, a try, a commitment or a re-arm made for an answer that never went out never happened (free-play run 5: probes counted Heal's drink tries for drinks never sent, and both potions were written off); a reflex that is sent keeps what it decided, but not the navigation learnings, stuck attempts, boss fight or Greet's tries (`dispatch.PROBE_NEVER_KEEPS`). The queue is dropped when a reflex fires (its intent replaces the queue, and since the dropped queue's later results are no longer read, position is re-read and the path forgotten), with two guards because a held `Step` may have run before its result came back: a reflex that walks to the very cell the held queue's next `Step` enters, or asks for exactly the intents the held queue has still to run (`Wait`s aside; A63 run 4: **Pickup** re-sent the held `Take`, 11 of 24 `Take`s wasted), leaves the queue running, and a replacement whose `Step`s, aimed from the tracked position, would land on a blocked, hazard or unknown cell from any cell the unconfirmed `Step`s could have reached in the ticks since the queue was sent sends a `Wait` instead and re-reads position (A63 run 3: a Flee step re-aimed from a stale position walked into known lava), when a `Step` is rejected (a rejection discards the rest server-side), when a death clears it, or when neither `queue` nor `finished_queue` has named it a couple of ticks past its length (the handoff store lost it; the character must not stall, and we may have walked unseen, so that drop also re-reads position and forgets the path and the last `Step` tick). A `Step` onto a door replaces the rest of the queue with `[]` on the very next call, before anything else is read, because the `Step`s behind it were planned from the wrong place. Once a rejection or a door has dropped the queue, a server `queue` echoed on that same response does not bring the hold back. A finished queue, a rejection, a door, a death, a firing reflex, or a lost queue send us back to step 1.

`Attacked`, `Damaged`, and `Died` on a queue are always ours: they carry no `subject_id` there.

### Reflexes: every round trip, no model

The first rule that matches picks the intent. They run on every `POST tick`, also while a movement queue is in flight: rules 2–4b drop that queue and send their intent in its place; rules 4c and 5 leave a live queue running.

1. Previous intent rejected → clear the path. A rejected `Step` teaches the map by its code (A14, `navigation/rejection.py`), every lesson keyed by map and cell:

   | Code | What the agent records |
   |---|---|
   | `not_traversable` | Impassable until a `BlockChanged` on that map and cell. |
   | `block_occupied` | Kept off for the next decision, then costs 100 extra for 30 ticks. |
   | `conflict_lost` | Nothing; the next move may retry. |
   | `door_locked` | Impassable, and stored as a locked door under the map's `doors` in the knowledge base, which every character of the world then keeps off. |
   | `over_strength_ceiling` | Impassable until the loadout (armed or worn) changes, and stored under the map's `hunting` in the knowledge base with the zone's ceiling for A27's strength bracket. |
   | `would_strand` | Treated as anything else below. The server refuses the move that would strand us (GAME_NOTES: unequipping the supply that keeps us on water), so the agent is never left stuck; it just tries something else next decision. |
   | anything else | Kept off for the next decision only. |
2. Standing on a block in `avoid_blocks` → step to the nearest safe neighbour.
3. Hostile in range: `on_hostile = "flee"` → the **Flee** state walks a committed multi-step escape: the best single step away first (standing still when cornered), then a route to a known safe tile or a run away from the hostiles (A9). `"fight"` → the **Fight** state (A23), not a reflex here.
4. `pickup = true` and a worthwhile supply underfoot, adjacent, or in a ground chest within reach → the **Pickup** reflex: `Take`, or `WithdrawFromChest` with that one `supply_id`. With the carried chest full it never drops: what to give up is the planner's call (`drop` on a `buy` or `fetch_item`, A102), so it skips the pickup (A20). Walking to a supply further away is **Loot**'s, only for a plan `fetch_item` op.
4b. Our last death dropped a chest on this map, `pickup = true`, and a tile on or next to it is known safe (A7) → the **Recover** state (A11) walks there; on or next to it, `WithdrawFromChest` with only its `chest_id`, which takes everything that fits, until the snapshot shows it empty or gone. `Died` names the chest and where it landed; tick POSTs carry the last applied observation version when we have one, so most round trips get deltas and the chest's `contents` stay in the model without a full snapshot every time.
4b2. `pickup = true`, a walk under way, and a free gem, a life, or food while hurt in view, where going by it adds no more steps than it is worth: the walk over known ground from where the character stands to the find and on to the best cell of the route still ahead (the queued steps, then the path), less the steps the walk takes to that cell anyway (`detour.extra_steps`, `pathing.route_ahead`; free-play run 4 priced finds from the end of the queue and walked past gems beside the route). There is no fixed reach, but a find near in a straight line and far on foot (beside a later stretch of a route that winds round a wall) is left until the walk comes by it, so a detour stays a short insert; a gem pile is worth 8 steps and each gem pile next to it 4 more (up to 16), a life 16, food while hurt 4 (`detour.allowance`, A73: run 4 walked past a gem triple 5 cells off that cost about 7), on ground Gather may work (clear of every known hostile's bar, remembered hostiles included, A22) → the **Detour** reflex (A71) walks to it by a path that prices every known hostile's reach as costly and must stay clear of it (`gather_safe.route_clear`; a find with no such path is skipped for the run), Pickup takes it once in reach, and the walk resumes toward its committed target. A `gather_gems` op with `fight` on top lifts the remembered and route tests. Once taken, a detour is kept until its find is taken or a concrete reason drops it, never for the time it takes (A125): a detour whose walk has no step, or stands no shorter over its window (`pathing.bounded_step`), is given up and that find is skipped for the run; one whose find is gone, or whose ground or path turns out held (a hit on the way, say), is ended (free-play run 5).
4c. On safe ground, or off it while `policy.hostile` does not name NPCs, a likely helper in sight not yet spoken to, with no hostile hit in the last 30 ticks, no known hostile within 3 blocks, the speech cooldown done, and the top op not a `say` → the **Greet** reflex (A65): one `Say` "hello" to the nearest one. The API does not mark helpers (see **Server gaps**), so a likely helper is an NPC that has stood on one cell for 50 ticks in view (helpers stay put, GAME_NOTES NPCs; `WorldModel.npc_still`) and is not known hostile: a boss, the last hitter, or a type that has swung at us, hit us or died in view, this run or an earlier one (`survival.known_hostile`, the same test `is_hostile` applies to NPCs). A monster that has not hit us yet can pass that test only by standing still, and off safe ground no NPC is greeted while `policy.hostile` names NPCs, at any range. Greet never walks (`Say` reaches 25 blocks, further than sight). It fires only at a decision window: it is not a reflex for the runner's held-queue probe (`reflex_while_held`), which leaves its tries as they were, so a walk queue already running is never dropped for it, and it costs the walk one tick. An NPC is greeted once: the applied hello lands it in `greeted_npcs`, Greet's own record in the knowledge base; one whose result never comes back is retried after 30 ticks, at most 3 times. A hello never counts as speech for the planner's `say` ops: it does not enter `spoken_npcs`, a refused one spends none of a `say` op's 3 refusals, and a later `say` op to a greeted NPC still says its own text (the runner tags a `Say` as the hello when it submits one that Greet decided, `Decision.state`, never by its text, and files its result by that tag). An NPC already spoken to by a `say` op is not greeted.
5. Otherwise the top plan op's executor, or the safe default, picks the move (**Architecture: AI plans, state machine executes**); a path is walked as a paced `Step` queue (see **Scheduler**).

"Hostile" and "in range" read from settings: the API serves no hostile's reach and no other character's health.

### Plan: goal and path

A goal plus a path over the M7 cost grid (A12–A13, `navigation/planner.py`). Step costs: known walkable 1; fog 2, so unseen ground is assumed open and paths may run through it; fire and lava 1 plus their `occupy_damage` from terrain reads, or plus 100 when no read has named it; an NPC or character standing there plus 50 (high but finite: they move); a cell a `block_occupied` rejection named in the last 30 ticks plus 100; each hostile in `policy.hostile` plus 30 minus 5 per block of distance, out to 6 blocks. Known blocked tiles, void, tiles a rejection made impassable (reflex 1), and breakables are impassable except at break time (A28, below), and a door is entered only as the goal, since stepping onto one warps. A* runs over the known extent plus start and goal with a one-tile fog ring, so an unreachable goal returns no path instead of searching fog forever. It expands at most 400 cells per replan (A13); when it finds the goal or proves it unreachable, that is the plan. When the budget runs out first, the replan plans only inside the perception window, with the same budget: a goal in sight gets the path to it, or the best partial path toward it when the window does not connect. The cell the agent stands on competes on the same score, so a window where no reachable cell beats it is no path: a dead end beside a goal that cannot be reached is reported as stuck, never a step away and back (A58 run 7). A goal out of sight also gets a coarse corridor search over 16×16 cache tiles, at most 48 tiles expanded per replan. It runs backward from the goal and is kept per plan (`goto` on its own map, the death chest) in `Memory.corridors` across replans until the goal or map changes or a step is rejected, so each replan carries on where the last stopped. A cache tile costs 16 times its mean passable step over the passable share, priced by the same grid (fog, known blocks, hazards, avoided, rejected and costly cells, occupants, hostiles); a finished corridor is re-priced every replan and searched again when a tile on it got dearer or impassable. The window search keeps to the corridor's tiles and those beside them, and ends on the cell with the best cost so far plus twice the estimated distance left along the corridor search's tree (from a cell, to the next tile toward the goal in that tree, its centre or the goal itself in the goal's tile, then along the tree), which is the same whatever tile we stand in; a corridor that gives no cell better than where we stand is no path, which stuck detection escalates, never a straight-line retry (free-play run 4, `planner._toward`); until the corridor is finished it heads straight for the goal. The executor walks the known prefix and replans when it runs out. `explore`, `doors`, and the door-graph floods of a cross-map `goto` (A26) pick among known tiles with the unbudgeted search of A12. Picking the nearest of many targets (`nearest_target`) is one Dijkstra from where we stand that stops at the cheapest target (ties go to the one nearer in a straight line, then the smaller cell), then one A* for its path; so unreachable targets cost at most one flood between them, not one each. Each search prices a cell once, and hostile danger is laid out once per search instead of summed per cell, and the map's frontier is kept up to date as tiles change instead of found afresh each ask. On a 30k-cell map a whole-map explore decision takes well under 100 ms (A23 Run 2 blocked 5–17 s; `tests/test_navigation_speed.py` guards it). The next step must be a seen walkable or door tile, and the queued walk stops before the first cell that is occupied or otherwise not open now, so it never Steps onto an NPC the grid priced at 50. A path whose first step is on an unseen tile does not count; the op's executor has no step this decision and the safe default moves (under a `travel`, Travel asks the planner and holds instead, A120). `avoid_blocks` are impassable except when standing on one with no safe step off: then they cost 100 extra per step, so the plan crosses as few as it can. Movement is Chebyshev: diagonals cost the same as straight steps.

`policy.goals` are input to the built-in planner, which turns each into one op; no state reads them.

| Goal | Op | Target |
|---|---|---|
| `explore` | `explore_area` anywhere | Nearest frontier: a known walkable tile next to an unknown one. |
| `doors` | `travel` to `entrance` at `0, 0` | Nearest known door (`framed_door`, `rock_entry`) whose warp is not yet recorded, else the nearest door. Stepping onto one warps. |
| `goto` | `travel` to `point` | A fixed `(x, y)`, on `policy.goto_map` when set (A26), else the current map. A goto already stood on gets no op (A16). |

Re-plan when a step is rejected, when the map changes, or when a terrain read disagrees with the path.

**Door graph (A26, `navigation/door_graph.py`).** A `goto` on another map, or one not reachable on this map, is a Dijkstra search over the door graph: nodes are the start, the goal, every known door and every warp landing; walking edges come from one cost-grid flood per node, and a door with a recorded warp has a zero-cost edge to its landing. A door reached on foot is left only through its warp, so one whose warp is unknown is a dead end; the start walks out even when it stands on a door. The agent walks the first leg; after the warp, it replans from the landing. With no known route the goal yields like any unreachable one. Warps are learned by observation: a `Step` onto a door sets `warp_from`, and the next position read records where it landed, unless it still stands on the door (no self-loops). A refused Step, a death, or a not-on-map error drops the pending warp. Knowledge base shape, under `maps["<map_id>"]` (`knowledge_maps.py`): `terrain` is `{"x,y": block_type}`, merged per terrain read's window and once more from the world model at exit; `doors` is a list of `{x, y, block_type}` sorted by cell, plus `to_map_id`, `to_x`, `to_y` once a warp is recorded, and `locked: true` once a Step onto it was refused `door_locked` (A14).

**Stuck detection (A15, `navigation/stuck.py`).** Every path to a target on the current map is an attempt keyed by goal, map and target cell; Explore (the plan's `explore_area` op and the safe default's `explore`), Travel (`travel:<to>`, every plan `travel` op, including the built-in planner's `goto` and `doors`), Recover (the death chest) and Level (`level:door`, `level:frontier`) all run through it. So do the short walks to something in sight, Heal to food (`heal_food`) and Loot to a pickup (`loot`), through `pathing.bounded_step`; those skip the ladder below and go straight to step 5 on no path or the first failed window, since nothing in sight is worth a broken block or a reveal walk. Heal's walks price a block on the straight way that something held opens (a bush and a blade) at its break, and open it Break's way once it is in reach when the cheapest way crosses it (`states/heal.py` `_walk_toward`, A89; A16 Walk run 4). Making an attempt active restarts its time window, since only time spent pursuing it counts; so only a walk that sends a step makes its attempt active, and one whose first step is taken or unseen waits without touching the active walk's window. It is given up after 300 ticks of waiting with no step in between, timed from when the wait began. A bounded walk's progress window and its wait both hold only while the walk is pursued (tried, step or not) on consecutive decisions, counted by dispatch (`NavStuckMemory.decision`); a walk that was not pursued on the previous decision (Heal left it on health back, food eaten on the way, or a higher state's turn) starts both afresh when it is taken up again (`nav_stuck.resume`). So a food or pickup is never given up on time it was not being walked to or waited on, and an owed goto held below Heal or Loot still escalates. A Heal or Loot walk's attempt is also finished when its food or pickup is in reach to `Take`. A give-up drops the current path only when it is the given-up attempt's own. These give-ups queue a `stuck` signal like any other, on purpose: the trace and the M7 gate report give-ups on other goals from them, and a strategist that sees food or a pickup it cannot reach can plan around it. They cannot flood the 16 kept signals, since each target backs off for 300 ticks, doubling on each give-up. Progress is the remaining length of the planned path plus the straight-line rest to the target, read when the state plans or keeps a path; an applied Step only bumps counters, so no search runs per move. The attempt is stuck when that measure has not fallen in 20 applied moves or 300 ticks, when its last 8 cells hold 3 or fewer distinct ones, when 3 of its Steps in a row were rejected (an applied Step resets the run; another goal's Steps do not count), or when the planner finds no path. A route whose first step is taken by an occupant is not "no path": the attempt sends nothing and waits out its window. Escalation follows PLAYABLE_AGENT_PLAN Navigation §4, each level with a fresh window and entered only when the one before failed: step 1 replans with fog at 8 instead of 2 (one that finds no path fails at once); step 2 (**Break**, A28) arms a held capability and `Use`s the first breakable on the straight route to the target when break memory has no failed `(block, capability)` pair for it (an `applied_no_effect`, or a refused `Arm` or `Use` other than `attack_cooldown`, marks the pair failed; with no pair left, step 3 starts); Recover and Investigate walks at step 2 yield the round so dispatch reaches Break; step 3 walks up to 40 moves toward the reachable frontier cell nearest the target, found by a breadth-first search over at most 400 seen open cells, which keeps the route along the obstacle (it stands in for the spec's left-hand wall follow and cannot circle); a plan shorter than any seen before ends the reveal and is walked, and failing again tries step 4; step 4 replans only through the door graph (`alt_route_path`, A26/A28), not another same-map A* to the same cell; a break that opens its block, or a step-4 route, walks again from step 0 on probation (`stuck.walk_again`): if that walk fails before standing 5 cells (`RESET_PROGRESS_CELLS`) nearer the target than the attempt ever had, the ladder resumes after the level that reset it, so grass cut beside a route that fails the same way cannot loop the ladder and the target is still given up (A16 Walk run 3); step 5 backs the target off for 300 ticks doubled on each give-up, drops its path, and appends a `stuck` signal (goal, reason it first got stuck, why each level failed, explored outline, blocking block types) to `Memory.nav_stuck.stuck_signals`, which keeps the last 16; the strategist (A35) drains them as its `stuck` trigger. A given-up frontier cell is skipped by `explore`, `explore_area` and Level: every walk to a frontier cell is registered as `frontier` in `nav_stuck.SHARED_KEYS`, so a cell one gives up on is skipped by all, and a given-up door by `doors` and Level, until its backoff ends; a given-up door, Travel destination or chest yields the round, and the safe default moves (a given-up Travel destination asks the planner and holds instead, A120). Break attempts live on `KnowledgeBase.breaks` keyed `"<map_id>,<x>,<y>,<capability>"`; only at step 2, or while **Break** walks to the cell it opens, are the breakables on the straight route to the target passable in the cost grid, at weapon cooldown plus one, plus the tool's gem price when the Supplies reference does not list the supply as kept on a break (`used_up_on_break` false: swords, knives, mallets); after a break opens its block, Break re-arms the weapon armed before it (A28). A supply's capabilities come only from the Manual's Supplies reference (A54) or from a break that subtype already opened (A46); only held or armed supplies count, since worn gear cannot be armed. **Escape** never breaks blocks; it only steps off hazards. Plan op `break_block` is handled by **Break** (A28). A cross-map `goto`, Travel or plan `travel` op tracks the first leg toward the door on the current map the same way, backing off the ultimate destination on give-up; after the warp it tracks the destination on that map. A cross-map destination with no known route yields without backoff: no route is not stuck. Every give-up, whatever its goal, is one record in one memory, `NavStuckMemory.given_up` (`stuck.GiveUp` by goal, map and cell), aged by time alone: it holds until its backoff ends, and its power is kept for the next give-up there (A90). The `stuck` signal carries `retry_at_tick`; whether to drop the goal behind it is the planner's call.

### Planner: sets goals and settings

- **scripted**: goals and settings come straight from the character file.
- **llm** (M4, superseded by the strategist in [`docs/PLAYABLE_AGENT_PLAN.md`](docs/PLAYABLE_AGENT_PLAN.md), shipped as `strategist.py`, A35): the AI planner, on in every live run. On every event (op done or dropped, stuck, death, hurt, new clue, new map, a discovery, A71, A72) and, with no event, 15 s after the last reply (A83) it sends the model a summary of the world model and gets back the goal stack and settings, within a per-minute budget. It runs off the tick loop. The loop keeps the old answer until a new one lands, so a slow model never costs a tick. `run --no-planner` falls back to **scripted** goals from the character file, as a test mode.

## Character file

One TOML file per **behavior profile** (`python/characters/<profile>.toml`). The file stem is the profile name. `tomllib` is in the standard library. Name, avatar and model agent belong on `create`, not in the profile (A59).

```toml
world = "sandbox"

[policy]
kind = "scripted"                   # idle | wander | scripted (the AI planner, A35, sets the goal stack on top)
goals = ["explore", "doors"]
on_hostile = "flee"                 # flee | fight | ignore
hostile = ["npc"]                   # npc, character; an NPC counts once its type shows hostile (A9)
hostile_range = 2
pickup = true
avoid_blocks = ["fire", "lava"]
entity_refresh = 5                  # ticks between entity reads when calm
```

At run time, pass `--character-id` / `AGENTREALM_CHARACTER_ID`, or `--character-name` to pick an existing character in the profile's world. Each run appends a JSONL trace at `python/.state/<profile>.<character_id>.trace.jsonl` (gitignored).

A separate runtime file, `characters/<profile>.directives.toml`, is re-read whenever it changes (A8). It carries survival `params` (defaults and ranges in [`docs/PLAYABLE_AGENT_PLAN.md`](docs/PLAYABLE_AGENT_PLAN.md)), a hard `never_attack` list enforced in the reflexes and on tick submit, and optional strategist fields (`goals`, `instructions`). On reload, changed `goals` rebuild the goal stack from the top and drop the current path, so the new top op replans at once; unchanged goals keep the stack's progress and the current path, and only reset its params to the file's values. Path ownership follows the plan head: **Explore** keeps walking a path only while it was set for the stack's current top op (same op and target), so a head change, even to another op of the same kind such as a second `travel` point, replans. With no valid `goals`, the AI planner fills the stack (A35); only in the `--no-planner` test mode does the built-in plan mirror `policy.goals` one for one (A34, `plan.py`). A `goals` list where every entry is invalid logs that and is treated as no `goals`. An op no state runs (`hunt`, `avoid`) is dropped with a log line when it reaches the top of the stack, and an op whose executor makes no progress for 30 seconds is dropped too (a `travel` to a `hunting_ground` with none known searches for one instead, bounded, A27); meanwhile `policy.goals` get the move, and a path one of them sets is kept until it goes stale instead of being replanned every window. A `travel` op of any `to` that resolves to a cell stuck detection gave up on (`entrance` at `0, 0` aside) leaves the stack for good and is unpinned (A16). Directives `travel:*` entries are not stack ops: **Travel** (A27) reads them straight from `goals` and keeps its own queue, so the stack's `travel` ops come only from the built-in plan or the strategist. **Gather** (A22) reads a `gather_gems` goal straight from directives `goals`, so dropping it from the stack does not stop it. `wait` seconds are converted at the world's `tick_rate_hz`. `never_attack` and the survival `params` **Retreat** reads (`retreat_hits`, `risk`, `lives_floor`, A9) change behavior today; `fight_margin` feeds only the win estimate **Fight** (A23) will gate on. The states read the plan's params (`Plan.params`): the file's values, tightened by a stack `set_param` op or by the strategist (A35) within the param limits; a reload resets them to the file's values. A file that fails to read or parse keeps the last good directives, or the defaults on first load; deleting the file restores the defaults.

## CLI

```
python -m agentrealm_agent create characters/wren.toml [--name Pat --avatar default --model-agent agentrealm-reference/scripted]
python -m agentrealm_agent run characters/wren.toml --character-id ID [--park-seconds 60]
python -m agentrealm_agent status characters/wren.toml --character-name Pat
python -m agentrealm_agent metrics characters/wren.toml --character-id ID
python -m agentrealm_agent metrics path/to/wren.42.trace.jsonl
python -m agentrealm_agent compare-metrics baseline.trace.jsonl candidate.trace.jsonl
```

Environment: `AGENTREALM_API_KEY` (from the account page on agentrealm.gg), optional `AGENTREALM_CHARACTER_ID` (used only when neither `--character-id` nor `--character-name` is passed), and `AGENTREALM_BASE_URL` (default `https://api.agentrealm.gg`, the public API; lives there are permanent).

One profile per invocation; several characters means several commands. `create` prints the new character id and writes no local file. `run` drives one character until interrupted (Ctrl-C or SIGTERM), then parks it on safe ground for up to `--park-seconds` (default 60; 0 = off) and clears its intent queue before it exits (A66); a second signal stops the park and exits within 5 s, clearing the queue only if that call finishes in time. It logs one line per window to stdout (tick, position, call made, intent, the result of the last one, events) and a JSONL trace to `.state/<profile>.<character_id>.trace.jsonl`, so a death can be read back as a decision. `metrics` takes a profile (`.toml`, any case) plus `--character-id` (or `AGENTREALM_CHARACTER_ID`) to build the keyed path, or any other path as a trace or metrics snapshot, and reads the last run from that trace (the trace is appended to; each run starts with a `world` record) and prints levels cleared, deaths, kills, gems, time per level, and how many lines it could not parse. Time per level runs from leaving the overworld (the town's map) to the level's `level_clear_ceremony`, across every map of the level; a run that starts inside a level has no time for it (A41). `compare-metrics` subtracts a baseline from a candidate and prints the deltas, so a regression shows up as a number (A42). Each side is a trace (`.jsonl`) or a metrics snapshot (`.json`). A delta is `null` when either side has no value: `gems` never seen, or a level timed in only one run (cleared in one and not the other, or entered mid-level), which would otherwise read as a slowdown or speedup of the whole level time.

## Server gaps that limit the agent

| Gap | Effect on the agent | Where it lands |
|---|---|---|
| No hostile's reach is served | `hostile_range` is a guess in the character file, and **Retreat** takes a hostile one cell away as in reach (`survival.HOSTILE_REACH`). | None |
| Which NPC types are hostile is not served | An entity read names an NPC's type, never whether it is a monster or a townsperson. The agent learns it from play (`survival.is_hostile`, `WorldModel.hostile_types`): an NPC is a threat once its type has swung at us, hit us or died in view (`NPCDied` names only hostiles), or it is a boss or the one hitting us. Until then it is not, so a new monster type gets its first swing in (A23 survive-a-fight run 2). What it learns is kept in the world knowledge base for later runs (`knowledge_base.hostiles`; free-play run 6 started beside the type that killed run 5 and did not know it). | None |
| A character's attack power is not served | `get_self` has no attack power, and the owner sheet's `strength` lumps it with weapon damage and armor defense on the viewing bucket. The win estimate uses the world's published base, 2 in Olympuff, hardcoded as `survival.BASE_ATTACK_POWER` (A81), so a permanent gain above the base goes uncounted. Read it from the API instead if it is ever served. | None |
| No NPC's health or damage is served, except a boss's health | Hostile health and damage per type are learned from `NPCDamaged`, `NPCDied` and `Damaged`, with conservative defaults until measured ([`docs/PLAYABLE_AGENT_PLAN.md`](docs/PLAYABLE_AGENT_PLAN.md) Combat). | None |
| No supply is labeled as a scroll on entity reads | **Investigate** learns scroll `supply_subtype_code` values by probing (A56): every code seen is logged, one carried or in-sight supply of each unseen code is `Read` once, and a code that does not answer `nothing_to_read` is kept as a scroll (`seen_supply_codes`, `probed_supply_codes`, `scroll_subtype_codes` in `kb.extra`). Until a code is probed, its scrolls go unread. | None |
| `Use` on an NPC by id ships in server release 1.11 | **Fight** aims every NPC swing by id (A45), so against a server older than 1.11 it cannot land a hit on an NPC. | B126 |
| No NPC is labeled helper or hostile on any read | **Greet** (A65) takes an NPC for a helper only when it stands still in view and has not hit us, and greets none off safe ground while `policy.hostile` names NPCs; the planner's State shows `hostile` (known hostile only) and `stays_put`, not a served kind. A helper that walks, or a monster that idles on safe ground, is misjudged. | None |
| No carry capacity, and no carried chest's subtype, is served | The snapshot's `inventory`, `GetSelf` and the owner sheet give neither, and history omits `SupplyUsed`. The agent derives capacity from the chest its own `Use` applied, kept per character in the world knowledge base (A115), bounded by `carry_capacity_full` and never below the slots carried. Every death resets it (A157): a `Died` clears it, and a death the agent did not see (another client, or while it was not running) shows as fewer lives at the first read than were saved with the record, or as a served `death_chest` the agent never knew of. A death not seen while a heart made up the life, with its chest already emptied, still leaves the record stale until a `carry_capacity_full` refusal shows the pack is smaller: a `Take` (or a `WithdrawFromChest` naming no supplies) proves no room for one more, a `WithdrawFromChest` naming `n` supplies only that `n` more did not fit (`loot.full_bound`), and the record is forgotten only when that bound is below its chest's size. Read it from the API instead if it is ever served. | None |
| Our own strength is served only on the owner watch sheet, not on a character route | The agent brackets its strength from `over_strength_ceiling` rejections and does not read the watch sheet, which sits outside the character call budget. | None |

Supplies reference (A54): the agent's seed item tables now come from the Manual's Supplies reference, served as JSON at `https://agentrealm.gg/docs/supplies.json` (saims B132) and read through `supplies.py`: each row's `capabilities` (saims B138; `use_effects` and a field per stat before it, still read, A123): food (`heal` and `eaten_on_pickup`) and potions (`heal` in `use_effects`) for Heal, Detour, Loot and Equip; break capabilities (the cut, chop, smash, burn and blast in `use_effects`), weapon class, and `used_up_on_break` for Break, Gather and Equip; each weapon's `damage` and attack cooldown for the win estimate's swing, the weapon ranking and the pacing of every `Use` (`supplies.weapon_damage`, A81; `supplies.weapon_cooldown_ms`, A123); each armor piece's `defense`, counted only worn, for the hostile's swing (`supplies.worn_armor_defense`, A97); and each chest's `chest_capacity` for Loot's carry capacity (`supplies.chest_capacity`, A97). Each run loads the table once before its first call (the cached copy under `python/.state/supplies.json` while it is under an hour old and every row carries `capabilities`, A126, else the site, else that cache at any age or shape, the run log naming the shape it used, else the copy checked in at `python/agentrealm_adk/reference/supplies.json`); it is a website page, not an API read, so it spends nothing from the call budget. A code the reference does not list gets no food, potion, weapon or break treatment, and no guess from its spelling (Loot no longer scores any code containing `potion` as one): the reference lists every subtype, and play still teaches the rest (A18, A46). One seed stays because the reference does not carry it: `loot.py` `NON_TRANSFERABLE` (the starting pocket knife cannot change hands). Footnote, low priority and deferred by Evan (not a backlog row): the reference also carries facts other seeds still hardcode or learn in play: a supply's worn `slot` (**Equip** still learns it from the snapshot's `worn` and a one-time `Wear` try, A55), the `life` class (`heart`, which A47 learns as `life_on_pickup`), and the `gem` class (`gem_cache_5/7/10` beside `gem`). A later cleanup could read those from it too.

## Milestones

This table is the backlog. Each row is one PR-sized item with a stable ID; IDs are never reused. Cite the ID in commits and PR bodies. Per-ID state lives in [`status.json`](status.json), which is not a source of truth: where it disagrees with this file, status is wrong. An item is ready when its state is not `done`, every ID in **Depends on** is `done`, its note does not start with "Waiting on", and no open pull request already covers it.

Items are grouped into milestones (M0–M14). A milestone is a heading, not a work item: it is done when all its items are. The scope and done-when of M4 and M6–M12 are in [`docs/PLAYABLE_AGENT_PLAN.md`](docs/PLAYABLE_AGENT_PLAN.md) **Milestones**, with the game facts and their sources in [`docs/GAME_NOTES.md`](docs/GAME_NOTES.md). Each milestone ends with an acceptance item that runs its done-when.

**Built (M0–M3). M5 is retired; it counts as done.**

| ID | Item | Depends on |
|---|---|---|
| M0 | **Discovery.** Docs read, hand play through MCP, [`docs/GAME_NOTES.md`](docs/GAME_NOTES.md) written. | |
| M1 | **Client and loop.** HTTP client, call scheduler, wall-clock pacing, 429/503 handling, `create`/`run`/`status`, `idle` and `wander`. | |
| M2 | **World model and pathing.** Tile and entity cache per map, local position tracking, A*, `explore`, `doors`, `goto`. | M1 |
| M3 | **Reflexes and scripted characters.** The reflex list, the full character file, the trace. | M2 |
| M5 | **Retired; counts as done.** Was a local-stack seed script; nothing was built. `status.json` keeps it `done` so it is never picked up as ready work. The agents target the public API at agentrealm.gg; there is no local stack for users of this repo. | M1 |

**M6: Executor.** Shipped: paced `Step`/`Wait` movement in the runner, `Use` and `Say`/`Broadcast` through `executor/pacing.py` with cooldowns carried across queues (A1), multi-intent queues, the two poll cadences, queue invalidation on reflexes (A2) and on path changes (A43), applying snapshot versions, deltas and health in the world model, and tick POSTs that carry the last applied observation version (A3). A4 recorded a live PASS on Olympuff in [`docs/acceptance/m6_olympuff_PASS.transcript`](docs/acceptance/m6_olympuff_PASS.transcript) (200+ applied Steps, no `movement_cooldown`, calm tick POSTs under a quarter of calm windows, zero API errors).

| ID | Item | Depends on |
|---|---|---|
| A1 | **Attack and speech pacing in the runner.** The runner sends `Use` and `Say` through `executor/pacing.py`, carrying cooldowns across queues. | |
| A2 | **Reflexes drop the held queue.** An alarm, hostile or hazard reflex replaces the held server queue on the next round trip. | |
| A43 | **Re-send on a path change.** A read or event that makes the rest of the held path wrong (`BlockChanged`, an entity on the path) replaces the queue. | |
| A3 | **Send `snapshot_version`.** Tick POSTs carry the last applied version so the server answers with deltas. | |
| A4 | **M6 acceptance.** Live smoke test against Olympuff: M6 done-when. | A1, A2, A3, A43 |

**M7: State machine and survival.**

| ID | Item | Depends on |
|---|---|---|
| A5 | **State framework.** `State` with `guard`/`act`/`done`, a priority dispatcher replacing `brain.decide`, `Sync`, `Downed`, `Explore`, `Idle`; the `list[Intent]` test seam. A state that claims the round but sends no intent falls through to the next state; that rule lives in the dispatcher (A44), so states do not add their own fallbacks. A state that means to hold the round with nothing to send (Sync, Downed, Idle, a Flee or Heal wait, Recover opening the chest) returns `StateOutcome(..., wait=True)`. The scheduler never reads position while the character is off the map: after `Died` or a self read with `placed: false` it polls `tick` until `Respawned`, re-reading self every 10 windows while self still says alive (A58 run 9 drew four 409 `not_on_map` there). | A1, A2 |
| A6 | **Threat table.** Damage per hit per hostile type from `Damaged`; the unmeasured default. | |
| A7 | **Safe-tile discovery.** `get_zone` while Travel searches for a hunting ground (A27, first: `zone_discovery.hunt_probe`), around the respawn point, and along the route, within the call budget. A terrain read's `safe` legend flag also marks safe tiles (`MapView.safe`), and such a cell is never zone-probed (A63 run 2: 307 of 741 calls were zone reads of town cells while standing still). The tradeoff: an agent's terrain read leaves out `brightness`, so a terrain-safe cell's brightness is never read and counts as 1 in `investigation.sight_range`, overstating sight in a dim safe zone. Discovery records safe tiles; **Retreat** (A9) and **Heal** (A10) walk to them, and **Recover** (A11) walks to a death chest only from a known safe tile on or beside it. A failed zone read drops that cell from probing. | |
| A8 | **Runtime directives.** `characters/<name>.directives.toml`, re-read on change; params with ranges and defaults; `never_attack` enforced in the executor. | |
| A9 | **Retreat, Flee and Escape.** `retreat_hits` and the `risk`/`lives_floor` formula; retreat to the nearest known safe tile a path reaches, else the town cell the world read gave (`survival.safe_goals`, `pathing.reachable_safe_goal`: the 4 nearest candidates not skipped are path-checked, then the nearest of the rest, the town cell last; a safe cell in the ground of any known hostile is no refuge and is skipped (Park, which walks as Retreat does, too): within `hostile_range` of one in view, or in the ground a remembered one holds (`hostile_ground.ground_by_hostile`, A22's sightings); a hostile it is running from (the fight's group, the hitter, any hostile in range) holds only the cells it gets to before we do, so one a step behind never covers the refuge we near, but one between us and a safe cell rules it out; and a cell stays skipped for 300 ticks once seen there (`Memory.safe_threatened`; a mark by a hostile still in view counts only while its ground covers the cell), so the pick does not turn back for it when the pack drops out of view; the town cell is the last resort on the same terms, and with neither left (every safe cell held or walled in) there is no refuge: Retreat and Park send nothing, so Flee's committed escape and its fight-back run next (A44), never a fall back to a safe cell a pursuer gets to before we do, which is the pacing run 7 died of (A63 run 4: Heal walked to a safe tile beside a pack and died; free-play run 7: Retreat walked back toward a safe tile 2 cells from the hostile it ran from while Flee stepped away, 6 hits in 19 s, 10 health to 1); a cell proven walled in, by a search from its own side whose passable cells run out within `FINE_NODE_BUDGET` without reaching us (`navigation.no_way`; a search from our side that runs out proves only that where we stand is shut), is skipped for 300 ticks (`Memory.safe_unreachable`), and so is one a Heal walk gave up on, or a Retreat or Park walk made no progress toward: a search cut short by its budget still counts as a way, so a walk whose remaining path has not shortened in a stuck window (A15's 300 ticks; `retreat.no_progress`) marks that cell for those 300 ticks and moves on at once to the next candidate, which it then keeps (A71: the lapse of the mark is no reason to turn back), else the town cell, which as the last resort is never marked this way while its walk has a path, only planned again (A15's progress record, `NavAttempt`, sampled but never made active; free-play run 3: Park spent 60 s two cells from a safe tile, logging "safe tile unreachable"); when the walk finds no path, a cell `no_way` proves walled in from its own side is marked at once, the town cell too, unless only this decision's oscillation escape shut the way; anything else proves nothing (a budgeted search can miss a way round, and one from our side that runs out shows only that where we stand is shut, maybe for one decision), so it marks nothing at once, but a whole stuck window with no step marks the cell, the town cell too; either way the next decision takes the next candidate, never the same empty decision for good (free-play runs 5 and 6: Park stood 60 s at one cell planning nothing toward town); a path whose first cell someone stands on only waits; Fight's retreat tail walks to the same cell (`pathing.retreat_safe_goal`); free-play run 2: Park, Retreat and Heal aimed at two safe tiles walled in by bush and wall, and Park timed out). **Retreat** fires only from health against a hostile in range (a hit's size comes from what is attacking), or, with `on_hostile = "fight"`, when the engagement's decision (A94) is not to fight a group that is coming for us: a member hit us recently, stands in reach (`survival.HOSTILE_REACH`, 1) or has moved closer in the last 30 ticks (`survival.threatening`, `WorldModel.entity_moves`); a boss always is, so **Boss** still retreats out of a fight it would lose (A38). A group that only stands nearby is avoided, not retreated from (A23 survive-a-fight run 2 retreated three times at 10/10). A hostile is a character of a `policy.hostile` kind, or an NPC whose type has shown itself hostile, a boss, or the one hitting us (`survival.is_hostile`; Server gaps); townsfolk and helpers never start Flee, Retreat or Fight. Every other threat check uses the same test: Gather's hostile bar, Heal's out-of-combat check, Explore's and the safe default's avoid, the path planner's danger cost (`CostGridParams.is_hostile`, set by `pathing.grid_params`) and the urgent poll rate (`poll_cadence.hostile_within`), so a townsperson never blocks a cut, repels a path or closes a frontier. It sends the whole path as one walk queue and lets that queue run (a reflex replacing it each round trip got one step per queue, A16 Walk run 4). Its path, and Fight's retreat tail, weigh no danger from the hostiles it is running from (the fight's group and the last hitter, `survival.pursuer_peaks`; each danger profile keeps its own corridor), so it takes the shortest way to safety instead of detouring round a chaser that follows anyway, and still keeps clear of other hostiles: a cell in the ground of a known hostile it is not running from, in view or remembered, costs a long detour, and a kept path that comes to cross the reach of one it did not cross when planned is planned again (`hostile_ground.ground_by_hostile`, `survival.hostiles_reaching`; A23 survive-a-fight run 1, A63 run 4). Losing ground (the distance to safety has not shrunk over 30 ticks while hits land), it drinks or eats what it carries, else, running not working, the engagement is decided again on that (A94) and it fights back the hitter in weapon reach when the decision is to fight, else replans weighing no hostile (`states/retreat.py`); the safe walk's stuck window pauses while it drinks or fights back (`Memory.retreat_paused`). **Flee** honors `policy.on_hostile` as before, not the win estimate; the estimate ships here for A23, on the assumptions in GAME_NOTES.md Open questions. Flee commits to a multi-step escape instead of re-picking one greedy step per decision, which two moving hostiles turn into pacing between two cells (A58 runs 4 and 6): Flee and Retreat share one threat picture, so they never pull opposite ways (free-play run 7): Flee runs toward the refuge Retreat picks (`pathing.retreat_safe_goal`, committed for both). The first step is the best single step (furthest from the nearest hostile; ties toward the refuge, else by summed distance to all hostiles; standing still is a candidate, so a cornered agent waits), and the rest is a route to the refuge when one is known and the agent reaches every cell of it before any hostile could, else up to 6 seen cells away from the hostiles under the same rule (`pathing.outruns`); a refuge behind a hostile is Retreat's to reach, not Flee's. It walks the escape until it arrives, its next cell is shut or would land nearer the nearest hostile than the best single step, or Flee stops running (`states/flee.py`, `pathing.flee_run`). While a weapon swapped out for a drink is still to be re-armed, **Heal** (priority 1) sends that one `Arm` first, so **Fight** and **Flee** start a tick later (A24). A hostile hit is an `Attacked`, or a `Damaged` whose `source_kind` is `npc` or `character`; trap and `occupy` (hazard ground) damage never counts (`threat.hostile_hit`, `WorldModel.attacked_tick`). The hitter is the entity a hostile `Damaged` names by `source_kind` and `source_id` (`WorldModel.attacker`); a later hostile hit that names no one (an `Attacked` on its own) clears it. With `on_hostile = "flee"`, Flee runs from that hitter when it is in view and hit us in the last 30 ticks (two of a hostile's swings), even when it stands just past `hostile_range` (`survival.flee_from`); a bystander hostile in view past `hostile_range` is not fled. While that pursuer is in view no other state's queue runs between its steps; its reflex step replaces any queue still running. `fight` keeps its own rule and only flees a target in range. Keeping other states' queues out while hit by something not in view moved to the redirect step 1 (AI plans, state machine executes). Flee is not a death march (A58 run 9 died fleeing one NPC that kept pace): once a hostile hit lands after Flee began, or the gap to the nearest hostile has not grown over 30 ticks (ticks, not decisions: under urgent polling Flee decides every tick or two and a step takes several), the engagement is decided again on running not working (A94), and Flee carries the decision out: it fights back when the decision is to fight, else walks Retreat's path to safety, and with no move away at all (no step from the hostiles, no Retreat walk) swings at the hitter in weapon reach above the health floor, since standing still only takes free hits; a `never_attack` target is never fought, and with none of these possible it keeps running (`states/flee.py` `instead_of_fleeing`). At or below the health floor (the next effective `retreat_hits` hits could kill, `survival.at_health_floor`), a fight we do not win is never picked, cornered or not (A16 Walk run 4). | A5, A6, A7, A8 |
| A10 | **Heal.** A reflex: hurt and no hostile in range. Heal decides how to heal, never when it is worth leaving the op: that line is the planner's `low_health` param (A89). Food within 3 cells (`FOOD_REACH`) is `Take`n, or walked to by one price (A89, `_food_target`): its walk on Heal's ground, a block on the straight way a held item opens costed at its break, food in reach costing none; food no walk reaches is not a target. Carried food, then a carried potion (only when health is low or the drink turns a fight, A96), is `Arm` + `Use` self. Food further away is the planner's (`fetch_item`, A61). Whether apples and berries heal on pickup or only carried and `Use`d is unmeasured (GAME_NOTES open measurements), so both are tried until A24 learns heal amounts and pickup vs drink. (Superseded by A155: food is eaten on pickup and never carried, and heals are the Supplies reference's.) A food `Take` or a drink the server rejects is handled by the rejection's code, never by a count of tries (A80); a drink that applied and drank nothing, by its cause (A76); both in one table, `executor/supply_refusals.py` (A89). Then Heal walks to the first known safe tile a path reaches (near town first, then nearest), else the town cell, as Retreat picks (`pathing.reachable_safe_goal`), skipping any safe tile in a known hostile's ground (within `hostile_range` of one in view, or held by a remembered one: `hostile_ground.ground_by_hostile`), or seen there in the last 300 ticks (`Memory.safe_threatened`), as `heal_explore` skips such an edge, so with every safe tile threatened it walks to the town cell, unless that is threatened too (A63 run 4: a walk to the nearest safe tile went into a known-hostile pack beside it and died). Every Heal walk (to food, a safe tile, or the safe zone's edge) is one walk (`states/heal._walk_toward`, A89): priced on Heal's one ground (`_ground`: hazards avoided, cells in a known hostile's reach, in view or remembered, `hostile_ground.reach_cells`, priced as a long detour), with a block on the straight way a held item opens costed at its break. When the cheapest way crosses such a block, Heal walks that way on the same ground and opens the first block it crosses once in the opener's reach, Break's way (`break_toward`); it never walks on Break's own grid. A kept path is planned again when it comes to cross the reach of a hostile it did not cross when planned (`survival.hostile_reach`, `Memory.planned_threats`; A63 run 4: Heal's walk to safe ground sent a 10-`Step` queue into a known pack and died). In a safe zone it samples regen while it walks to the zone's own unexplored edge (a `heal_explore` walk with every off-zone cell walled off, never the safe default, whose frontiers lie outside), and with none left it sends `Wait` and starts no walk or stuck attempt, so a long rest leaves stuck detection untouched (live Walk run: at 3/10 with no food Heal swapped five times in 14 s between `heal_measure` and "heal in safe ground" as the safe default walked out and the sample restarted). Only a measured "yes" for safe-zone regen is saved to the knowledge base; a "no" (200 ticks hurt in a safe zone with no health back) holds for that run, and then Heal never samples or walks to safe ground again: it raises one `heal_supplies` trigger (health, `regen: no`, and `potions` held: a reserve held back above the low line, A96) so the planner can `buy` or `fetch_item` food and potions, raised again only after a full heal, and sends nothing, so the plan's executor or the safe default moves. Only a hurt character can show regen, so a window at full health, or with `max_health` unknown, gives no verdict. Every Heal walk is a stuck attempt (A15, `pathing.bounded_step`): no path, or no progress in its window (the remaining path not shortening in 20 moves or 300 ticks, or pacing), gives it up with stuck detection's backoff in A90's one give-up memory, raising a `stuck` trigger (A58 run 7: Heal paced beside food it could not reach). Safe ground (the walk there, the rest, the regen sample) outranks a plan op in progress only when health is at or below the planner's `low_health` share of max (`healing.health_low`, default half); otherwise Heal yields to the op's executor (A84). The safe-tile walk commits to one tile: a give-up of it, or a check that rules it out (`pathing.safe_ruled_out`), gives it up in A90's memory (`stuck.give_up`), and safe ground stays given up while any give-up of a Heal walk to safe ground on this map backs off (`_safe_given_up`), with one `heal_supplies` trigger (`safe_ground: unreachable`), raised too when no safe cell is reachable at all; Heal never moves on to the next tile at once, and when to send the character back is the planner's (`retry_at_tick`, A89). Heal pushes no `buy` op; the planner decides purchases (A61) from the `heal_supplies` trigger. Re-arm after a drink and heal learning per supply type are A24. The regen clause can fail a whole A58 run when the character is never hurt in a safe zone; the A60 regen probe (`make probe-regen`) gets the character hurt to answer it. **Superseded by A130** (Evan: the game has no natural regeneration): the walk to a safe tile, the rest in a safe zone, the regen sample and its verdict, the `heal_explore` walk, the safe-ground give-up and the `regen` and `safe_ground` fields of `heal_supplies` are gone. Heal eats or drinks, else raises `heal_supplies` once and sends nothing; the safe pick round a pack is Retreat's and Park's. | A5, A7 |
| A11 | **Recover.** Walk to the death chest only when the spot is safe (any remembered one in reach is taken back wherever it lies, A122). Needs `pickup = true` and a known safe tile on or beside the chest on this map; Escape, Retreat, Heal, Fight, Flee and Pickup still run first, and with no plannable step it sends nothing and the next state moves (A61). Only the destination is checked: the route to it is not, which is A9's. | A5, A7 |
| A12 | **Cost-grid planner.** The cost table (fog, hazards, hostile danger, expiring occupants, break costs when nominated), walking the known prefix. | |
| A13 | **Two-level search.** Coarse 16×16 corridor search and A* in the perception window, each with a node budget per tick. The window search ranks a cell by the corridor search's tree (the next tile toward the goal: its centre, or the goal itself in the goal's tile), never by the tile we stand in, and a corridor is never dropped for a straight-line retry: that let two cells each rank below the other, and Travel paced between them until oscillation gave up the town (free-play run 4, `planner._toward`). A partial path ends only where the walk can go on (a cell beside the window's edge with a passable cell past it, or one the budget left unexpanded), never in a pocket the window search saw all round. On the corridor it is real-time search that learns (LSS-LRTA*): it ends on the end with the least cost plus estimate and raises every searched cell's estimate to what the way on from it costs (`NavSearchState.learned`, kept when the corridor is searched again for the same goal, A125, and dropped with a new goal or when the walk escalates), so a dead end looks as dear as the way out of it and the walk leaves it and never walks back in (free-play run 6: Park walked two cells into a pocket, found no cell better than it, and stood there 60 s). | A12 |
| A14 | **Rejection learning.** What each rejection code teaches the map (reflex 1 table): impassable, occupant cost for 30 ticks, locked doors and hunting closures in the knowledge base. `would_strand` is treated like any other code (reflex 1 table). | A12, A17 |
| A15 | **Stuck detection and escalation.** Steps 1, 3 and 5, backoff, frontier drop; the navigation fixtures and trace replay tests. **The walker commits to its path** (`navigation/walk.py`, through `pathing.commit_walk`): every walk, whichever state runs it (`replan` for Explore's goals and plan ops, `guided_step` for Travel, Recover, Level, Investigate and Boss, `bounded_step` for Heal and Loot), follows the path it chose until one of: arrival (the path is walked), a rejected step (`learn_step_rejection` ends the walk), a target change (goal, map or cell), or newly seen terrain that blocks it (a cell on it impassable on today's cost grid) or makes a new plan cheaper by more than 20% (`SWITCH_GAIN`; both priced by `planner.path_cost`). There is no per-decision re-pick of the route: a kept path whose next cell is still fog waits for the terrain read rather than turning round (Travel holds the round while a walk under way waits, as a wait that is not progress and for at most 50 ticks at one cell (`walk.FOG_HOLD_TICKS`), so the safe default does not step it away and back but a cell never seen cannot pin it; under any op the safe default never moves, and the op's executor clears the way or asks the planner and holds, A120), and a new path whose first step goes back to the cell the walk just came from is taken only when the kept path is blocked, or when the new path is cheaper overall and the kept path's hostile cost (`planner.hostile_cost`) is higher by more than `SWITCH_GAIN` (A16 Walk run 3). Ties are stable, so two equal paths never alternate (the walked one wins; `nearest_target` breaks equal-distance ties by cell). An escalation level that plans afresh (steps 1, 3 and 4) starts a new walk, since the old path is the one that got stuck. An explore walk (`explore`, `explore_area`) aims at a frontier cell, which reveals move nearly every decision, so it is kept while its target is still a frontier cell (or unseen) and reachable; once a reveal takes that cell away it re-aims at the nearest frontier it can reach without stepping back (`walk.follow_frontier`, `pathing.commit_explore`). Its frontier is committed (A71): a route to another frontier never replaces it, however much cheaper, and a walk that lost its path (another state moved the character) plans again to the same frontier; it changes only on a reveal, a target proven walled in (`no_way`), a backoff, or, as a backstop while stuck detection escalates a frontier it finds no path to but cannot prove walled in, 10 decisions in a row without one (`targets.MISS_LIMIT`), after which the planner's nearest frontier takes over. A direction clue (A32) is a target change: each decision, before Explore keeps its path, a walk whose target is not on the side a recent clue names is dropped when one of the planner's own targets (the op's area frontier, or the safe default's safe frontier, not backed off) lies on that side and is reachable (`pathing.clue_redirects`), so the next plan takes the clue's side, and a re-aim prefers it too. A clue naming ground the walk could not pick, such as frontier outside an `explore_area` op's area, never drops it, so the walk is not remade every decision while the clue lasts. Each goal keeps its own walk (`Memory.walks`), so a goal waiting on fog keeps its path while a later goal takes the move. The kept path and the new plan are priced on the grid the planner searched (the states pass their `grid_params` builder to `guided_step` and `bounded_step`). A walk is dropped wherever its path is dropped for good: a give-up or the oscillation guard's (`stuck.give_up`, even when another walk holds `m.path`), a break that opened the way, a backed-off target, Heal's backoff, and a directives reload; so a route given up on never comes back. This is the fix for A58 run 5, where fog showing the other of two equal routes one tile at a time turned the goto walk round to the cell it had just left (`tests/test_a58_run5.py`). **Oscillation guard:** dispatch checks once per decision, before any state runs, whether the character's last 6 cell changes stayed on at most 2 cells; if so it gives up the active movement target through step 5 (reason `pacing`; since A111 and A90, every walk that made a paced move, keyed by its target) and traces an `oscillation` event, whichever states caused the pacing (`navigation/oscillation.py`). The target is given up only when its own walk made one of the pacing moves; Retreat, Fight and Flee pacing with steps of their own back nothing off. Heal's and Loot's walks are stuck attempts of their own, so Heal or Loot pacing gives up its own food, pickup or safe tile here, like any walk. When only Flee and Retreat made the moves, the event carries `escape: true` and the guard hands the paced cells to them (`oscillation.take_escape`): on that same decision Flee plans a fresh committed escape (A9) that keeps off them, or Retreat replans its route around them; the cells are dropped at the next decision whether or not one of them ran, so survival pacing corrects itself in this one place rather than per state. | A5, A12, A14 |
| A16 | **M7 acceptance.** M7 done-when, as a gate: `scripts/smoke_m7_olympuff.py` and `agentrealm_agent/m7_acceptance.py`. The smoke script wakes a sleeping character with one `Wait` (and waits out a respawn), reads the world and position, sends the agent to one target 150 blocks east of its overworld start (`--target` overrides; with the planner on a directives goal `travel:point:<map>:<x>:<y>` pinned on top of the stack by `acceptance_smoke.pin_goto`, which the planner plans below and cannot drop, since the policy `goto` never reaches the stack under the planner; with `--no-planner` the built-in `goto`), and plays the rest of the hour on `characters/olympuff_m7.toml` (pickup on, since Recover needs it).
