Hooks run from the runner through `AcceptanceHooks` (`acceptance.py`, which also holds the request-error counter); the smoke scripts take `--character-id` or `--character-name` (A59). `before_tick` sees the world the decision saw, before the response is applied. A run fails on: any death under `--stop-on-death` (the first one ends the run; by default deaths are counted and reported, not failed, and play goes on after the respawn, A63 run 3); a tick where `should_retreat` held outside Sync, Downed, Escape, Retreat, Heal or Flee (`SURVIVAL_STATES` in `acceptance_survival.py`; Boss is not on the M7 list); a Recover `WithdrawFromChest` that runs anywhere but a known safe tile (Recover withdraws only while standing on a known safe tile, judged on the cell the queue puts the agent on); 24 Step-sending decisions in a row at one cell with one reason (a loop; waits such as Heal resting are not movement); more than 3 oscillation-guard give-ups within 6000 ticks (sustained pacing, which also stops the run; guard events that gave nothing up, survival states doing the moving, are reported, not counted); any API error; with the planner on, 3 planner errors in a row (`PLANNER_ERROR_STREAK`; an error is a failed call, a reply that is not a JSON object, or one whose ops are all invalid, and a lone one the backoff recovers from is counted, not fatal), any refused key (401/403), or no accepted plan at all (`acceptance.PlannerHealth`, shared by every smoke gate, whose summaries print the totals; live Walk run: 30 calls failed with 401 and the gate still printed PASS); and, on a run of at least 95% of an hour, navigation and regen. **Give-up rule:** navigation passes only when the agent stood on the target, or stuck detection gave up on that same target (map and cell) with a reason, such as a route that needs a block broken. Give-ups on other goals (explore frontiers, shops) are counted and reported, never a pass. Regen passes once `regen_known` has an answer, yes or no (a yes saved by an earlier run on the world counts). Heal actions are reported, not gated. **The target is a `travel` op (A61):** Travel walks it and nothing defers to it, since no other state can take the move; reflexes such as Heal still act. A `travel:point` op is done once the agent stands on its target and pops, so it is never walked back to. Any `travel` op (whatever its `to`: point, town, shop, entrance with a cell, hunting ground) whose destination, as it resolves now (`travel.resolve.travel_given_up`), is a cell stuck detection gave up on leaves the stack for good, pinned or the planner's; a `shop` with no cell and a `hunting_ground` resolve past given-up cells to the next best candidate, and leave only when none is left; an `entrance` at `0, 0` (the nearest unexplored door, which changes) is not matched (`Runner.drop_given_up_ops`, once per op, before the planner's turn each window; a new head drops the path and every committed walk, like any head change; a planner reply that re-sends it is filtered before it reaches the stack, A35), and a pinned one is unpinned: neither the backoff's end, a planner reply nor a directives reload brings it back (second live Walk run, where the op stayed in the stack's directives part and was walked again at 168, 210, 236 and 255 s). Since A90 the give-up is not run-scoped: like every give-up it lapses when its backoff ends (`travel.resolve.given_up_travel`, read from `NavStuckMemory.given_up`; `Runner.note_lapsed_give_ups` logs each lapse as a `stuck` line), and sending the op again is then the planner's call. This replaced the hub exception (town and shop give-ups lapsing after 1200 ticks or 16 cells, free-play run 2). **No return to a given-up target:** the gate counts each return, a run of decisions acting on a `travel` op (`acted_op`) that resolves to a cell given up at an earlier tick, prints the count, and fails the run on any. The loop, Recover-withdraw and retreat-miss checks live in `SurvivalAcceptanceMetrics` (`acceptance_survival.py`, shared with A29 and A40), with `OscillationAbortTracker` beside them (shared with A40); each gate names its own survival states (`survival_states`), and `stop` is read when the abort fires, so the smoke scripts can assign it after the metrics are built. The live hour that passes this gate is A58. **Superseded by A130** for regen: the regen clause is gone, and a full hour judges navigation only. | A4, A9, A10, A11, A13, A15, A44 |
| A58 | **M7 live hour.** Run `make smoke-m7-olympuff` for the full hour until it passes the A16 gate, and commit the redacted PASS transcript under `docs/acceptance/`: redaction removes credentials and replaces every live character name and id with a placeholder (A59). A failing run is an agent bug to file, not a gate to loosen. More than 3 oscillation-guard give-ups (A15) within 6000 ticks aborts the run with exit 1, so a pacing agent never burns the hour. Probe runs that did not pass are in [`docs/observations/A16_live_play.md`](docs/observations/A16_live_play.md) and [`docs/observations/A58_live_play.md`](docs/observations/A58_live_play.md). The M7 target is a `travel` op on top of the stack (a pinned directives goal under the planner, A16), so only Travel walks it until it arrives. The regen clause and the A60 probe are gone (A130: the game has no natural regeneration). Until the basics pass (no death, no API errors), live tests are 5-minute runs (`--seconds 300`, survival only) rather than the full hour. | A16, A59 |
| A59 | **Character-agnostic runs.** The agent plays whatever character it is handed. A character file (`python/characters/<profile>.toml`) is a behavior profile: policy, goals and world. Its file stem is the **profile name**; the TOML `name` field goes away, and directives stay beside it as `characters/<profile>.directives.toml`. The character to play comes from the command line or the environment (`--character-id` / `AGENTREALM_CHARACTER_ID`, or `--character-name` to pick an existing one from `list_characters`, filtered to the profile's `world` by `character_select.find_character_ids_by_name`; zero or several matches is an error naming the choices). An explicit flag overrides the environment: `--character-id` or `--character-name` wins over `AGENTREALM_CHARACTER_ID`, and passing both flags is an error. An API error while listing characters exits 2 with one line, like any other selection error. With `--character-name`, startup makes three reads (`list_characters`, then `self` and `world`), which use up the burst of 3. The same profile can run on any character. **Local files:** the JSONL trace is keyed by profile plus character id, `python/.state/<profile>.<character_id>.trace.jsonl` (under `STATE_DIR`), so two characters on one profile never share or overwrite a trace. There is no per-profile local binding file; the character id is chosen at run time. The world knowledge base stays shared per world (`python/.state/worlds/<world_code>.json`, A17), which A16's regen gate read until A130. **One profile per invocation:** `create`, `run`, `status` and `metrics` take exactly one profile (drop `nargs="+"`); running several characters is several invocations. **Creating characters:** running never creates one. Creating is explicit: the runner's existing `create` subcommand and the starter agent's existing `create` subcommand (A52), each taking `--name`, `--avatar` and `--model-agent` (with defaults) instead of reading them from the profile. `create` prints the new character id and writes no local file; the user passes that id to `run`. The M6/M7 smoke scripts never create. **Offline commands:** `metrics` and `compare-metrics` need no key, so they take a trace path (`metrics` also takes a profile plus `--character-id` to build the keyed path), never `--character-name`. **Committed files:** no live character name or id anywhere in the repo (code, configs, scripts, README, docs, status.json notes, tests; fixtures use obviously fake names). Existing acceptance transcripts and observation notes are scrubbed to placeholders in the A59 implementation PR. Done when every entry point runs against a character chosen at run time, files are keyed as above, only the two `create` subcommands create, no committed file names a live character, and `make test` covers selection by id, by name (including world filtering and ambiguity), the missing-character error, file keying and both create paths. | A16 |
| A60 | **Regen probe.** A short live run that gets the character hurt so Heal can answer the A16 regen clause, which no A58 run has measured because the character was never hurt. `scripts/probe_regen.py` (`make probe-regen`, same character selection as the M7 smoke: `--character-id`, `--character-name` or `AGENTREALM_CHARACTER_ID`, A59; never creates a character) wakes the character and runs the normal agent from wherever it stands on `characters/regen_probe.toml` (`on_hostile = fight`, explore, pickup off). Its directives (`characters/regen_probe.directives.toml`) set `risk = 1.0` and `fight_margin = 2.4` (1.0 before A81), both within range, since before A85 the win estimate at the defaults never let a 10-health character take on an unmeasured hostile (default hit 2); `retreat_hits` and `lives_floor` stay as shipped. Nothing new steers: **Fight** swings, **Retreat** leaves when `should_retreat` holds (through the win estimate at health 6 against a hostile measured at 2: by then it has hit, A81), and **Heal** walks to a known safe tile and samples regen (`note_regen_sample`), saving a "yes" to the world knowledge base (`save_regen_yes`). The watcher is `RegenProbeMetrics` (`regen_probe.py`, on `SurvivalAcceptanceMetrics`) run through `run_acceptance_smoke`. The probe stops on the first of: `regen_known` answers; the cap (`--seconds`, default 1800); under `--stop-on-death`, the first death, which fails it (by default a death is reported and the probe plays on). It prints the verdict and exits 0 only when regen answered (and, under `--stop-on-death`, with no death) (a "no" holds for that run only and is not saved). A "yes" already in the knowledge base answers on the first decision. With fewer than 6 lives (`lives_floor` 3) the effective risk falls and it does not take the fight, so the probe likely ends at the cap. Offline tests (`python/tests/test_regen_probe.py`) cover each stop condition and that a probe's "yes" passes the M7 gate's regen clause on a later run. Run it before the A58 hour when the knowledge base has no regen answer. **Superseded by A130:** there is no regen to measure, so the probe, its profile and directives, `scripts/probe_regen.py`, `make probe-regen` and `test_regen_probe.py` are removed. | A10, A16, A59 |
| A44 | **Dispatcher fall-through.** A state whose `guard` holds but whose `act` sends no intent yields the round to the next state, so a guard/act mismatch can never freeze the agent. The dispatcher enforces it once; a test drives each shipped state through that case. The one exception is typed, never keyed on state names or reasons: an outcome with `wait=True` is an intentional wait and keeps the round. Each yielded `"State: reason"` is kept on the outcome's `yielded` list, and in the reason when no state sends anything. | A5 |
| A61 | **AI plans, state machine executes (step 1).** States stop choosing goals: reflexes keep their guards, executors guard on the top plan op (`my_op`), and with no op Explore runs the safe default, so exactly one thing picks the movement target and a non-forced decision is never idle (**Architecture: AI plans, state machine executes**). Removed every self-start path: the `policy.goals` fallback in `replan`, Gather reading `goals`, the Travel queue, the curiosity budget, the interest list, door looks, **OddBreak**, Shop restock and buy signals, and the goto-first deferrals. Tests: `test_architecture.py`. Step 1b (the walker commits to its path, A15, #109) and step 2 (the strategist and planner wiring, #108) have shipped. `potion_reserve` is planner-only: no state reads it, and M8's gate checks it. M8 (equip, potion restock), M9's look clause and M10's read-and-say clause now depend on the planner choosing the equip, buy, travel-to-entrance, read and say ops; the live scenarios check that. Since A65 one reflex speaks on its own: **Greet** says one hello to a likely helper in sight, only at a decision window (never from the held-queue probe, so it never drops a running walk queue). | A5, A34, A44 |
| A64 | **Decision latency.** One decision must fit in a window or two, so polls stay on time and urgent polling every tick stays possible. A23 survive-a-fight run 2 blocked the main thread for 5–17 s per decision (162 of 302 s), so walk queues finished long before the poll that saw them, and a fight could not be polled every tick. Profiled causes: `navigation/planner.py` `nearest_target` runs one unbudgeted A* per target in distance order and skips past every unreachable one, so the hunting search's whole-map frontier (`HUNT_SEARCH_AREA`, hundreds of targets) costs many full-box floods (13.7 s a call); `danger()` loops over every hostile for every expanded cell, and `hostile = ["npc"]` counts every townsperson; `runner.reflex_while_held` runs the full `_decide` on every held-queue poll. Fixed: `nearest_target` finds its target with one flood that stops at the cheapest target, then one A* to it (#132); danger is one table per search (`navigation.planner.danger_map`), a lookup per cell, and only NPCs of a type known hostile price it (`survival.is_hostile`); the held-queue probe runs only `dispatch.PROBE_STATES`, no executor; its `Memory` snapshot (taken so a probe leaves no trace, free-play run 5) shares the corridor searches rather than copying them (`memory.SEARCH_CACHES`), about 1 ms on a long run's memory. `tests/test_navigation_speed.py` bounds a decision on the 30k-cell fake map, alone and with 400 known hostiles, and the held-queue probe on that crowded map. Done when a live run also logs no window gap above 1 s outside network time. | A12, A13 |
| A66 | **Park before exiting.** The world does not pause when the client stops, so a run that ends on field ground can lose a life unattended (seen after a timed `--seconds` smoke run). When a run stops (its time limit, Ctrl-C or SIGTERM), the runner parks for up to `--park-seconds` (default 60; 0 turns it off) before it returns: it keeps playing windows with only the survival reflexes (Sync, Downed, Escape, Retreat, Heal, Fight, Flee) and **Park**, which walks Retreat's path to the nearest known safe tile a path reaches outside every known hostile's ground, else the town cell (`states/park.py`, `dispatch.PARK_STATES`); a safe tile it makes no progress toward in a stuck window is ruled out for 300 ticks and it moves on to the next, else town (never ruled out this way), as Retreat does (A9; free-play run 3). No plan op runs. It ends at once when the character already stands there, is downed, or knows nowhere safe on its map, early on a second stop signal (the process then stops waiting on the runner after `ABORT_JOIN_SECONDS`, 5, so the queue clear may not happen), and after the cap otherwise; a death while parking is read from `alive`, not from a `Died` event a response may drop; then one empty tick clears the intent queue, and a `park` line and trace record give the outcome (`parked safe`, `park timed out`, `park aborted`, `no safe ground known`, `no path to safe ground` once every safe cell it knows is ruled out (A9), `downed`, `died while parking`) and the position. The park phase is not the scenario: acceptance hooks hear only `on_park_start` and `on_park_end`, `ParkSplit` keeps its API errors apart, a smoke run's seconds leave it out, a death while parking does not fail the end-of-run alive check, even under `--stop-on-death`, and the scripts print it on its own line. `Sleep` at shutdown (PLAYABLE_AGENT_PLAN.md) is not sent. Tests: `test_park.py`. | A9 |
| A67 | **Free-play scenario.** A live run where the planner decides everything: `scripts/smoke_m8_olympuff.py` on a copy of `characters/olympuff_m8.toml` whose directives file holds `goals = []`, planner on, no `--stop-on-death`, park on. Nothing is pinned, so it checks whether the progression arc (A62) carries the character from stage to stage on its own: shopping and equipping in stage 2, gem hunting, clues, entrances. At under 95% of an hour the smoke checks only the short-run M8 gates, so its `PASS` says nothing about gear or gems; the observation doc judges those. Runs are recorded in [`docs/observations/A67_free_play_runs.md`](docs/observations/A67_free_play_runs.md). | A35, A62, A66 |
| A68 | **Break never flips weapons.** A67 run 1: at stuck step 2, Break sent one `Arm` every decision for 370 s, alternating knife and sword, and never stepped or escalated. `pick_supply_for_capability` (`break_memory.py:157`) looks only at `w.held_supplies`, which leaves out the armed weapon, so the unarmed one always wins. And Break's `[Arm, SetPosition]` is unpaced, so only the `Arm` is sent (`brain.py:142`). Fixed: the armed weapon is a candidate and wins a tie (#139), and Break steps toward its block and arms only once in reach (#146). A test: two weapons with the capability, one armed, and the decision steps. | A15, A28 |
| A69 | **Travel to a shop stops beside the item.** A67 run 1: `travel to: shop` with no x, y resolved to the nearest shop cell, which is an item for sale, and walking onto it took a bronze_sword for 15 gems that no `buy` op asked for. Travel now ends next to a shop cell, or any cell with an item for sale, never on it, and no walk steps onto a priced supply (A27); buying stays Shop's job (A21). | A21, A27 |
| A70 | **The planner sees stalls and entrances.** A67 run 1: State had no sign that the character had stood on one cell for 370 s, so 22 replies kept the stack. They "dropped the 0,0" that `plan.py:157` fills into a symbolic `travel` and `stack_lines` (`strategist.py:656`) echoes back. That doubt also led the planner to swap `travel` town for the walk that stalled. State also lists no known level entrance, though stage 1's readiness asks for one. Fixed in #139: the echoed stack leaves out 0, 0 and shows `goes_to`, State carries a `stall` line (no move, gems, item or level change for 30 s, with the last decision), and `level_entrances` lists known entrances. | A35, A62 |
| A71 | **Commit to the target and the goal it serves.** Pacing stopped at its source rather than guarded against: a state no longer re-picks "nearest X" each decision. `targets.py` keeps one commitment per walk label (`Memory.targets`): the target and the plan op it serves, kept until reached or gone, proven impossible (no path at all, or stuck detection's give-up at the end of its ladder), the op changes, or nothing pursued it for 300 ticks (the episode is over). A target with no path that is not proven walled in is escalated by stuck detection, and let go after 10 decisions in a row without one. Never because another candidate came nearer or another state walked in between. Each state keeps its own key (`targets.py` lists them: `explore`, `explore_area`, `safe` for Retreat and Park, `heal_safe`, `heal_explore`, `heal_food`, `travel`, `shop`, `loot`, `level`, `level:entrance`; Gather keeps `Memory.gather_target`). Committed: Gather's grass, bush, pile or walk-out cell (`m.gather_target`; a pile out of sight is kept; a target whose first step is taken holds the round for up to 5 s instead of letting the safe default walk away and back), Explore's frontier (the walker keeps only paths to its own target, `walk.commit`), Retreat's and Park's safe tile, Heal's safe tile, zone edge and food, a symbolic `travel` (shop, hunting ground, nearest door), Loot's supply (let go too when stuck detection backs its walk off, or on a map change), and Level's door or frontier and its entrance. **Stops:** a prerequisite becomes a stop before the target, which stays: an entrance filed `locked` with a `needs` item not carried, that item in sight, is walked to and taken first; with no room for it, or 3 refused `Take`s, the stop is given up for that entrance and the walk goes on (a supply code matches `needs` as Shop's `buy` does, `healing.supply_matches`: exactly, never as a substring). **Detour** (a reflex after Recover): a free gem, a life, or food while hurt in view, whose extra steps off the route still ahead (the queued steps too, priced from where the character stands) fit what it is worth (A73), on ground clear of known hostiles, is walked to; Pickup takes it and the walk resumes. **A `gather_gems` region is committed too:** a planner reply that moves the head's region (`x, y` in another 16×16 region; another cell of the same one is the same target) is applied with the old region kept, and said in `last_reply_rejected`, unless the region is exhausted (barren or poor), Gather went 30 s without getting nearer it or, once there, without a cut there taking effect, or the call carries new information (a death, a new map, a hurt, or a hostile pack seen) (`gem_yield.keep_gather_region`; free-play run 4 moved it 4 times in 50 s). **New information wakes the planner:** a `discovery` trigger (`discovery.py`) for an NPC not seen before, a new level entrance, an item the gems can now buy, or a known-hostile pack in a 16×16 square with none seen before; what was known at the start primes the record, each find is raised once, the finds waiting are one trigger (the newest 16), and a discovery alone starts a call no sooner than 5 s after the last call ended (A83). A reply with a new head op is applied at the next action boundary (no queue held, at most 10 s later), never mid-walk; the survival reflexes still act at once. The safe default's walk serves no op, so the first plan (or any plan over an empty stack) is applied at once and cuts that walk's queue at its next step (free-play run 6: the safe default's ten queued steps ran on after the first plan landed and took the character into a gristlewick). Distance thresholds shared between states are out of scope. | A15, A35, A61 |
| A72 | **The planner sees signs.** A sign was read only when the planner happened to send a `read` op with its cell; nothing told it a sign was there, so the character walked past signs. State `signs_seen` lists the signs and statues seen on this map (`MapView.readable`), unread first, then nearest, at most 5 (`strategist.SIGNS_SHOWN`), each with `map_id`, `x`, `y`, `block`, `cells`, `dir` and `read` (the knowledge base's `read_cells`, `investigation.cell_was_read`), plus the count of unread ones. A readable cell on any known map seen for the first time and not read is a `sign` discovery (`discovery.py`), raised once a run, under A71's 5 s gap and action-boundary rule. No sign detour: Detour is built on a ground supply that Pickup takes, while a sign is a cell a `Read` from sight answers, so reading stays the planner's `read` op, which walks into sight (Investigate, A30). | A30, A71 |
| A73 | **Detour sees the whole walk and judges a find by the steps it adds.** A67 run 4: two gem triples were missed on the first pass. (a) Fixed in #158: Detour priced finds against `m.path`, which starts a held queue ahead, so three gems 2–4 cells off the first walk cost 5–7 extra steps instead of 1–3; it now checks the held queue's remaining Steps then `m.path` (`pathing.route_ahead`). (b) A triple 5 cells off an Explore walk was past the fixed 3-cell reach and the 4-step allowance; Gather took it 255 s later. Now every valuable in view is priced by the steps it adds: the walk over known ground from where the character stands to the find and on to the best cell of the route ahead, less the steps the walk takes to that cell anyway (`detour.extra_steps`), with no reach cut. Each kind has its own allowance (`detour.allowance`): a gem pile 8 steps, 4 more for each gem pile next to it (a cluster, up to 16), a life 16, food while hurt 4. Only on ground `gather_ground` allows. A detour stays a short insert: the walk keeps its committed target and resumes after (A71). | A71, A20 |
| A74 | **Equip clears a non-weapon from the armed slot.** A67 run 4: a potion left armed from an earlier run blocked the sword, because `best_equip_upgrade` arms a weapon only while the slot holds a weapon or nothing (`equip.py:175`). 15 `equip` ops each finished "nothing to equip" at once. Heal records whatever is armed as the weapon to re-arm (`states/heal.py:308–309`), a potion included, and re-armed the potion over the sword. Equip treats an armed non-weapon that no state owns as an empty slot, and Heal records only a weapon. | A19, A24 |
| A75 | **A `buy` with no shop item in sight walks to the shop.** A67 run 4: `shop_outcome` sends nothing when no priced supply is in view (`states/shop.py:66–67`), so the Explore safe default ran for 70 s and walked about 100 cells from town into a chugbug pack. A `buy` whose item is not in sight inserts a stop at the nearest known shop cell (A71's prerequisite rule, beside the item as A69 has it), and explores toward town only when no shop is known. | A21, A69, A71 |
| A76 | **The M8 potion gate counts a drink only when a potion is used up.** Gate fixed: `heal_potion` counts a self-`Use` **Heal** sent with a potion in hand only when its result is `applied` (`AcceptanceHooks.on_intent_result`) and the held plus stowed potion count (plus the armed potion when `held` leaves it out, `healing.potion_count`) on the next decision is below the count when it was sent, or when a `SupplyUsed` event names our character and a potion while that drink is out. A drink a queue without it replaced (forgotten after that response's results, as `Runner._forget_replaced_self_use`), a refused or `applied_no_effect` `Use`, and one cut short by a death count nothing. Heal fixed: a drink whose `Use` applied (`applied` or `applied_no_effect`) is kept (`Runner._note_applied_self_use`) and read at the next decision (`Runner.file_noop_use`). It drank something if the supply is no longer carried (held, stowed or armed, by id: the snapshot's armed `id`, `WorldModel.armed_id`), our own `SupplyUsed` names its code, or health rose. Otherwise the cause comes from the observation (`supply_refusals.noop_cause`), is traced as `heal_noop`, and is filed as that supply's record beside A80's refusals (`supply_refusals.NoopUse`): something else in the armed slot → the next drink sends its `Arm` whatever `armed_code` says, or, when that queue already armed it, holds; full health → drunk again only once health is below that reading; armed, hurt and nothing changed → held until the drink's situation changes (`supply_refusals.noop_situation`: map, the ids we carry, max health) or health falls below the reading it was filed at. That situation leaves out the cell and the armed slot, which a walk and the re-arm after every drink change without changing what the drink does, so the same drink is never resent into it; regen does not lift a hold, but any fall in health does, so a potion is never unusable while health falls (A67 run 5 died at 2/10 holding two). A drink of a potion while another of that code sits in the slot sends its `Arm` (`armed_id`), so it is not a `Use` of the armed one again. The armed supply is a drink candidate even when the snapshot leaves it out of `held` (`healing.carried_heal`). The wrong target (run 4's cause) cannot recur: the drink targets `self` (A24). A67 run 4: `heal_potion` was set by any self-`Use` sent with a potion in hand, so it read yes for a `Use` at 228 s, at 8/10 health, that drank nothing (potions 2 → 2). A full run could pass the milestone without Heal ever drinking. The gate counts a drink only when the carried potion count drops after the `Use` (or a heal event names it). Heal also finds out why that `Use` did nothing and stops resending one that changes nothing. | A25, A24 |
| A77 | **A held-queue probe leaves Heal's tries alone.** Fixed in #161; superseded by A80, which removed `heal_tries` and the try cap. A67 run 5: the probe that runs the reflexes on each "queue held" poll (`runner.py:584`, `reflex_while_held`) restores the plan, path, targets and `heal_rearm` (`runner.py:760–783`), but not `m.heal_tries`. Heal's `use_carried_heal` counts a try (`states/heal.py:308`) before the drink is sent, and the probe drops the drink because it is not a reflex (`runner.py:775`). Six polls after the first walk used up both starting potions' 3 tries (`healing.py:43`, `:55`), so neither Heal nor Retreat's losing-ground drink (`retreat.py:216–218`) could drink them, and the character died at 2/10 holding both. The probe saves and restores `heal_tries`, or a try is counted only when its intents are sent. | A24, A9 |
| A78 | **Gather and Detour keep off a hostile's post and the way past it.** Fixed in #162 (`WorldModel.sightings`: a post and the reach it hit from bar ground out of view, `gather_safe.known_reach` and `route_clear` on Gather's and Detour's targets and walks, piles bound to the region Gather works) and #164 (sightings and hostile types kept across runs, `knowledge_base.hostiles`). A67 run 5: `gather_ground` (`states/gather_safe.py:62`) bars a cell only for a hostile in view within its bar of that cell, so piles 5–15 cells from a roaming gristlewick, often out of view, stayed targets. Detour checks the same (`states/detour.py:191`) and prices hostile reach only as costly on its walk (`:219`). Four trips there ended in hits (10 → 3) and the run's one death. Gather's pile target also left the named `gather_gems` region. A hostile that has hit us bars its last-seen post and the route through its reach for a while after it drops out of view, and Gather's pile target stays in the named region. **Superseded in part by A162:** hostiles keep no post (Manual §11 Hostiles). The post, its reach, its fade, faded-post pricing and `note_came_for_us` are gone. A hostile out of view holds its bar round the cell where it was last seen: a moving one for `SIGHTING_TICKS`, a blocked one (`Sighting.still`) for `BLOCKED_SIGHTING_TICKS`. | A22, A71, A73 |
| A79 | **A `buy` is done when it has bought.** Fixed in #161: a `buy` is done once one more of its item is held or stowed than the fewest since it reached the top (`Plan.held_before`). A67 run 5: `goal_done` counts a `buy` done when any matching supply is held or stowed (`plan.py:771–772`). With 2 small_potions held, 8 `buy` small_potion ops finished at once, and raising `potion_reserve` to 3 did not help. A `buy` is done when the held count of its item has gone up since the op came on top (or, for a potion, when it reaches `potion_reserve`). | A21, A35 |
| A80 | **Heal acts on why a `Take` or drink was refused, with no try cap.** A67 run 5 died at 2/10 holding two potions that a cap of 3 tries per supply (`HEAL_MAX_TRIES`) had written off; the cap hid why they were refused. Now the runner keeps each server rejection of a food `Take` or a drink (`Runner._note_supply_refused`) and files it at the start of the next decision (`Runner.file_supply_refusals`, `Memory.supply_refusals`: the code, the action, the tick and the situation), after the response's observation and any position read, so the situation is the one that decision sees, not the world before the refused queue ran (its `Step`s, an `Arm` that applied before its `Use` was refused). Each is traced as `heal_refusal`, with a code it does not handle by name marked unknown. The action comes from the code ([API rules § Tick Rejection Reasons](https://agentrealm.gg/docs/api); `supply_refusals.refusal_action`): `nothing_armed` or `not_held` on the drink's `Use` → the next drink sends its `Arm` whatever `armed_code` says; `not_held` on its `Arm`, `supply_gone`, or any `permanent` code → that supply is forgotten; `target_not_nearby` or `target_out_of_range` on a `Take` → Heal walks onto the food's cell before taking it, and food that moved is aimed at afresh; a `state` category or a `transient` code (downed, a cooldown, `store_unavailable`, `conflict_lost`) → tried again no sooner than one weapon cooldown later (`REFUSAL_WAIT_TICKS`, 10 ticks), once alive, so a blocker that persists costs one refused intent a second, not one a tick. Anything else, and the arm or walk once already tried (the `Arm` was in the refused queue, the `Take` sent from the food's cell), holds that supply until its situation changes (`supply_refusals.refusal_situation`: map, cell, armed code, held ids, max health, and the food's cell), so the same refusal is never sent twice into the same situation. Health is left out: regen, poison or a hit moves it every few ticks without changing anything a refusal depends on, and would resend the refusal each time. An applied `Take` or drink clears its record; a drink that applied and drank nothing is filed again by its cause (A76). Records are written only from server results, never in the held-queue probe (A77). | A10, A24 |
| A82 | **Free-play run 8: Gather keeps working past a far hostile, Detour prices risk, an unreadable reply keeps the plan.** (1) Gather stalled 177 s with no cut (263–440 s): every known cell to cut was barred by a hostile 10–13 cells away, so Gather sent nothing and the safe default, hurt, stood off it (`explore.keep_away`). Now, with every known cell to cut held by a hostile and none reachable, Gather walks on to the nearest frontier with no known hostile, in view or remembered (`hostile_ground.hostiles_within`), within `GATHER_HOSTILE_RADIUS`, by a route clear of reach (`gather._plan_clear`, target kind `clear`), kept until a cell to cut or a gem pile it may take comes into sight and a clear route reaches it (what is in sight but unreachable is tried once, `Memory.gather_clear_seen`), and, like Gather's other kept walks, waited on through a taken first step (A71); its status is `walking to ground clear of hostiles`. The safe default's keep-away holds only while a near hostile is closing in (`survival.approaching`); with no step away from one that is not, it explores. (2) Detour walked a character at 4/10 to a pile 2 cells from the gristlewick pack, the same pile as run 7, and it dropped to 1/10. A find within `RISK_RADIUS` (`GATHER_HOSTILE_RADIUS`, #162's danger radius) of a known hostile, in view or remembered, is taken only while one hit from each of them (threat table) would leave health above Retreat's floor (effective `retreat_hits` × the hardest of those hits; `detour.risk_allowed`); a detour under way ends once health no longer covers it, without giving the find up for the run. A `gather_gems` with `fight` takes the risk, as it lifts Detour's other hostile tests. (3) A planner reply that was not JSON (589.6 s) cleared the stack, dropping `gather_gems` and `buy` for the safe default. A reply that is not a JSON object is now a failed call (`Strategist._fail`): the stack and its walk stay, its triggers go back, and the backoff applies before the retry. **Since A88:** item 1's walk to the frontier clear of hostiles is the ordinary edge target of Gather's one search (A88). | A22, A71, A73, A35 |
| A83 | **The planner's timer is a gap after the last reply, not a rate.** `timer` was raised once `AGENTREALM_PLANNER_REPLAN_S` had passed since the last call was *sent* (`Strategist.last_call_at`), so a reply slower than that was followed by the next call at once: back-to-back calls with no time for the plan to act, and a risk of overrunning the budget. Now `Strategist.last_reply_at` is stamped when a call ends (`_settle`: its reply applied, kept, dropped or failed) and the timer counts from it, so `AGENTREALM_PLANNER_REPLAN_S` (default 15 s) is the gap between one reply and the next timer call. A discovery alone waits `DISCOVERY_GAP_S` (5 s) after the last reply too, the same rule for both gaps. `last_call_at` still stamps the send for the budget. Events (stuck, hurt, death, map, clue, goal done or dropped, directives, idle) still start a call as soon as none is in flight, within the budget and any backoff. The planner prompt says it is asked again a few seconds after each reply. | A35, A71 |
| A84 | **Heal's regen measure yields to the plan and walks to one safe tile.** Free-play run 9: at 9/10 health Heal walked to measure regen and circled for 217 s (304–521 s) between safe tiles it could not reach at (389–392, 393–394), giving each up and starting on the next, while the planner's `travel` to town never ran (`states/heal.py:109–116, 194–218`). Fixed at the root: safe ground (walk, rest, regen sample) outranks a plan op in progress only when health is low (`healing.health_low`, at or below half of max); the safe tile is picked with the same reachability Retreat and Park use (`pathing.reachable_safe_goal`: walled-in cells proven by `navigation.no_way` and ruled-out cells skipped), committed (A71), and a give-up of it marks it in `Memory.safe_unreachable` and gives safe ground up until the next full heal, or at low health for 300 ticks (`Memory.heal_safe_given_up`), with one `heal_supplies` trigger, so unreachable tiles cannot chain into a loop. Tests: `test_freeplay_run9.py`. A89: the low line is the planner's `low_health` param, and a safe give-up holds while it backs off in A90's memory, not until a full heal (tests now `test_heal.py`). **Superseded by A130:** Heal no longer walks to safe ground at all. | A10, A9, A71 |
| A92 | **The planner weighs a purchase against what is held.** In the last three free-play runs that had gems to spend (A67 runs 7, 8 and 10; run 9 bought nothing), the planner bought another box of matches with boxes already held, on the rumor clue "potato patch, take matches": a second box in run 7, a third in run 8 with its only 5 gems, and a fourth in run 10 with 3 held (put on the stack at 13 s, bought at 28 s for 5 of its 8 gems). State showed held counts but not what each item is for, nor what the gems were saving toward. Whether a purchase is worth it depends on the situation, so under A87 it is the planner's call: the fix is its information and guidance, not an executor rule such as a cap on held items. State `held` now lists, per item, the count held and stowed and what the Supplies reference (A54) says it does: `class`, `use` (its use effects), `heal`, `damage` or `defense`, `slot`, and `used_up_on_break` (`supplies.what_it_does`). New State `upgrades_for_sale`: the gear seen for sale that beats the best weapon (`damage`) or armor for its slot (`defense`) owned, cheapest first, with price, gain and `gems_short`. The planner contract asks it, in plain words, to weigh each buy against what is held and what the gems save toward. The `buy` op doc now says outright that holding some neither skips nor finishes a buy (#161, A79). | A35, A54, A79, A87 |
| A94 | **Free-play run 10: fight or flee is one decision per engagement, and a target is priced by the danger round it.** (1) Flee and Fight took turns against one gristlewick (320–331 s): it dealt 4 damage and took 7 before Flee broke off at 3/10. Fight decided on `would_lose` over the group in range, Flee's fall-back on its own test (the weapon had hurt the type, cornered, swing back anyway at the hitter), and Retreat on `would_lose` again, so each re-decided on what moved: a hostile stepping in or out of range, whether a step closed on it, whether Flee was outrunning it (`states/flee.py` `instead_of_fleeing`). Now fight or flee is one decision per engagement (`engagement.sync_engagement`, `Memory.engagement`), read by Fight, Flee and Retreat: the win estimate (`survival.win_ratio`, A81/A85: live health, each type's measured hits and misses, the armed weapon) over the engagement's group, which only grows while it lasts (a member stepping out of range is still in it), against a bar (`engagement.fight_bar`): `fight_margin`'s with `on_hostile = "fight"`, never with `flee` while running works, and once running cannot open distance (Flee's probe, or Retreat losing ground; `engagement.cannot_outrun`) the break-even ratio 1, or the margin when lower, for either policy, since running then only takes free hits. It changes only when the estimate does: health crosses the bar, a hit or miss moves a type's odds, a new hostile joins, or running is found not to work. Fight swings or closes in while it says fight; Flee runs while it says flee and, once running fails, fights back or walks Retreat's way as it says; Retreat's `on_hostile = "fight"` trigger is its "do not fight" and its losing-ground fight-back its "fight"; `should_retreat` takes the decision, so Boss and the survival acceptance gates read it too, and Gather's fight on a hostile that shadows it asks what an engagement with it would decide (`engagement.would_fight`). AGENTS.md carries the rule: `survival.would_lose`, with no caller left, is retired, and no state adds a second fight test. Gone: the weapon-has-hurt test (`weapon_has_hurt`, a second decision beside the estimate: since A81 the estimate prices our swing from the weapon's published damage, and since A85 a type never measured at the world's base attack power), and the cornered fight-back and swing-back-anyway as fights picked against the decision; with no move away at all, Flee still swings at the hitter in reach above the floor rather than stand. An engagement ends once no hostile is in range and none has hit us within 30 ticks. (2) 150 s without a cut (165–314 s): Gather and Detour went 4 times for the pile at (429, 351), 6–8 cells from the NE posts, and each trip ended in a flee and a retreat (`states/gather.py` pile pick, `states/detour.py` risk gate). Gather took the nearest pile by straight line ahead of any grass, and kept it as its committed target through each flee. Now a target's price is its walk with the danger on it, at the target and along the path (`gather._price`): known reach stays costly and route-checked, and ground within `NEAR_GROUND_RADIUS` (6, the planner's danger radius) of what a known hostile holds or of one in view is priced at `NEAR_GROUND_STEPS` (6) a cell, as a faded post's ground is (`hostile_ground.near_reach`, `Danger.priced_cells`), so it is priced, never barred. Gather picks the cheaper of the cheapest pile and the cheapest grass, a pile counting Detour's `GEM_PILE_STEPS` (8) cheaper since it is a sure gem; Detour counts a find's price against its allowance (`Danger.price`). A forced retreat is new information about the hostile that forced it: a hostile that joins a fight with us off its post (one still on it came out for nobody) stretches that post's reach to where we stand (`WorldModel.note_came_for_us`, as a hit already did; A85's `POST_REACH_CAP` still bounds it), which widens the ground it holds and the ground priced round it, and the fight has Gather's committed target and Detour's find priced again on their next decision (`Memory.reprice_targets`) rather than kept. No per-target memory and no counts: the outcome updates the one hostile-ground picture every pick reads. Tests: `test_engagement.py`; `test_a58_run9.py` and `test_survival_runs.py` now pin the decision (a hitter we lose to is neither closed on nor fought, one we beat is). **Superseded in part by A162:** hostiles keep no post (Manual §11 Hostiles). The post, its reach, its fade, faded-post pricing and `note_came_for_us` are gone. A hostile out of view holds its bar round the cell where it was last seen: a moving one for `SIGHTING_TICKS`, a blocked one (`Sighting.still`) for `BLOCKED_SIGHTING_TICKS`. | A9, A23, A22, A73, A82, A85 |
| A95 | **Free-play run 11: Equip probes only what the Supplies reference leaves open, and a remembered post fades on evidence, not time away.** (1) Equip's slot probe (A55) sent `Wear` for every held non-weapon with no known slot, so it tried to wear matches. Rule: a slot comes from the snapshot's `worn`, else from the Supplies reference (A54): its `slot` for a subtype it classes as `armor` or `accessory` (`supplies.worn`, `supplies.worn_slot`). A subtype it lists in any other class (tools, consumables, gems) is never worn (`equip.never_worn`), so only a subtype the reference does not list, or lists as worn with no slot, gets the one `Wear` try (`equip._best_learn_wear`). Listed armor is scored in its reference slot from the start. (2) After a 2.4 h break between sessions, all 13 remembered posts had faded below the forget line on world time, a pack's standing post among them. Rule: a post's decay counts evidence, the time its post was in sight with its guard gone, never time away. `Sighting.fade_post` halves strength every `EMPTY_POST_HALF_LIFE_TICKS` (10 ticks, 1 s) looked at empty, times its spells (up to `POST_MAX_SPELLS`, 4), counting at most `EMPTY_POST_HALF_LIFE_TICKS` for one look since the time between looks shows nothing. A post nobody looked at keeps its strength, out of sight, on another map, or between sessions; A85's world-time half-life (`POST_HALF_LIFE_TICKS`) is gone. #180's "seen empty fades fast" holds: a post looked at empty goes within a few seconds. With no time fade left to release it, a guard's last-seen cell off its post holds by the passer-by rule whatever its post's strength (`hostile_ground._last_seen_holds`: seen there within `SIGHTING_TICKS`, and not that cell in sight with it gone); after that only its post holds. Tests: `test_equip.py` (`SlotTest`), `test_freeplay_run9_hostile_memory.py` (`RememberedPostsFadeTest`). Observations: `docs/observations/A67_free_play_runs.md` **Run 11**, defects 2 and 3. **Superseded in part by A162:** hostiles keep no post (Manual §11 Hostiles). The post, its reach, its fade, faded-post pricing and `note_came_for_us` are gone. A hostile out of view holds its bar round the cell where it was last seen: a moving one for `SIGHTING_TICKS`, a blocked one (`Sighting.still`) for `BLOCKED_SIGHTING_TICKS`. | A19, A54, A55, A85, A87 |
| A96 | **One rule for when a carried potion is spent.** Free-play run 11: the planner bought a potion for the reserve and Heal drank it 1 s later at 7/10 in town, so the reserve never built: Heal drank any carried potion whenever health was below max (`states/heal.py:115–117` ran `use_carried_heal` ahead of the `health_low` check). Now one rule (`healing.spend_potion`), read by every drink (Heal, and Retreat losing ground, through `healing.carried_heal`): a potion is spent when health is low (`healing.health_low`, at or below half of max), or in a fight the win estimate says the drink turns (`engagement.drink_turns_fight`: at the healed health the engagement would fight where it now does not, or win, a ratio above `BREAK_EVEN`, where it now loses; an unknown heal counts as a full one). Otherwise Heal eats carried food or rests (superseded: rest by A130; carried food by the Manual, which has food eaten on pickup and never carried, pending A155). The planner is told the same rule in its contract (`healing.POTION_RULE`) and State shows `potions` (held and stowed) and `potion_drunk_at` (the health line), so its reserve is planned on what the executor will do. Retreat losing ground re-decides on running not working (`engagement.cannot_outrun`) before it asks, so the drink is weighed against the bar a fight that cannot be run from has. The ticks a drink takes (`Arm`, `Use`, re-`Arm`) are not priced apart: the win estimate at the healed health is the whole test, replacing the spec's three-tick comparison. A `heal_supplies` trigger carries `potions`, and the contract says potions held there are the reserve held back, so the planner weighs food, rest or waiting before buying more. Each potion held is asked for its own heal, so a larger one can be drunk where a smaller one would not turn the fight. The M8 gate's "Heal drank a carried potion" is unchanged and now needs health to fall to the low line (or a fight a drink turns) during the run. Tests: `test_freeplay_run11.py`. A89: the low line is the planner's `low_health` param (default half); tests now `test_potion_spend.py`. | A10, A9, A61, A94, A87 |
| A98 | **Every API call goes through the pacer.** Free-play run 12 sent 4 tick calls in about 1 s (a `Use`, then "queue held" polls) and got 429 `rate_limited`, failing the run gate. The runner paced its decision windows, but the pacer only slept: nothing counted what was sent, so any path that called the client on its own (startup and wake reads, the world read and its retries, the minimap read) spent outside it, and the held-queue poll had no model of the server's bucket to stay inside. Now the pacer is that model (`pacer.py`: burst 3, one token per tick window, about one a second asleep, emptied by a 429 with its `Retry-After` honoured), one per character id as the server keys it, and `Client._call` spends from it before every `/characters/{id}/…` request, so the rule holds at the call site for every caller, present and future. The runner waits on the same pacer (`pacer_of`). Tests: `test_pacer.py` (held-queue polls sent back to back stay in budget; a sleeping character at one a second; Retry-After; every character route spends and account routes do not). Observations: `docs/observations/A67_free_play_runs.md` **Run 12**, defect 1. | A1, A87 |
| A99 | **Free-play run 12: one weapon-to-arm rule for Equip, Gather and the planner, by the job at hand, and the planner's equip op can name what to arm.** The planner bought a bronze mallet (25 gems), having twice judged it not worth it, on the belief that Equip keeps the sword armed if the mallet would shut the hunting ground. Equip ranked weapons by `gem_price` (`equip.py` `_best_for_slot` on the armed slot) and armed the mallet: strength 2 + 6 + 1 = 9 over the ceiling 7. About 11 s later Gather re-armed the sword to cut grass (the mallet cannot cut), and the sword stayed armed: three rules for which weapon to arm (the planner's, Equip's, and Gather's first held cutter). Rule: the weapon to arm is one function, `equip.weapon_to_arm`, by the job at hand: fighting (Equip arms for it, and State `upgrades_for_sale` lists weapons by it, each with `cuts`) or cutting grass (Gather's `_cut_tool`: a held item that cuts, one a cut does not use up first; the armed one wins a tie, and a cutter it betters is not put back after, while a weapon that cannot cut still is, `Memory.gather_rearm`). Both order by `equip.weapon_rank`: a weapon that keeps strength (`equip.loadout_strength`: `survival.BASE_ATTACK_POWER`, the weapon's published `damage`, worn armor's `defense`; B138) within the highest hunting-ground ceiling known (`equip.hunting_ceiling`: the knowledge base's `hunting` cells and this run's zone reads) beats one that does not, then more published `damage` wins; with no ceiling known damage alone decides. Price and measured per-type hits no longer rank weapons (worn slots keep A19's scoring). So a held weapon over the ceiling never replaces one within it, nor fills an empty slot (no weapon armed counts as bare hands, which are within it; `upgrades_for_sale` takes the same baseline when no weapon is owned), and one armed over it is swapped back for one within. AGENTS.md carries the rule: never add a second weapon ranking. New State `hunting_strength` (strength as armed and worn now, and that ceiling). An `equip` op with a `code` now arms or wears exactly that held item, whatever the rule says (`equip.named_equip`): a fight the hunting ground does not decide, such as a level boss, is the planner's call. The contract states the rule in a paragraph of its own, says a weapon that cannot cut is put away while gathering and adds nothing to gem work, and stage 2 no longer says "equip the best" without it. Tests: `test_equip.py` (`HuntingCeilingRuleTest`), `test_gather.py` (`GatherArmsACutterTest`), `test_strategist.py` (`test_weapon_upgrades_follow_equips_hunting_ceiling_rule`, `test_prompt_states_the_best_weapon_rule`). Observations: `docs/observations/A67_free_play_runs.md` **Run 12**. | A19, A27, A81, A92, A87 |
| A100 | **Free-play run 12: no planner ask before the first full read.** The first ask went out on the `map` trigger as soon as the position was known, before any self read or inventory (State: `armed=None`, `held={}`, `gems=None`), so it planned purchases the character already had; the next ask dropped them 10 s later. Rule: the planner is not asked until position, self and inventory have each been read at least once (`WorldModel.synced`: `self_read`, set by `apply_self`, and `inventory_read`, set by a snapshot's `inventory`). Triggers raised before then wait in the inbox and go with the first ask. Tests: `test_strategist.py` (`CadenceTest.test_no_ask_until_self_and_inventory_are_read`). Observations: `docs/observations/A67_free_play_runs.md` **Run 12**. | A35, A87 |
| A101 | **Free-play run 13: a stage whose readiness is met moves on, unless the planner names a concrete, reachable reason to stay.** Every stage 2 readiness test passed all run (10/10 health, 3 potions, bronze sword armed, bronze mail worn), yet the planner kept gathering gems "for iron gear" with `upgrades_for_sale=none seen`: no iron item was known for sale anywhere, so it never moved on to levels. The arc said "move on only when its readiness is met" but not that a met stage must move on, and State gave no per-stage readiness to judge from. Under A87 the stage call stays the planner's: the fix is its facts and the rule, not a coded stage switch (no stall detector exists for this; `StallClock` counts gems gained as progress). New State `readiness` (`strategist.readiness`): per stage, each check and `met`. Stage 1: `shop_known` (a shop cell recorded or any item price seen), `entrance_known`. Stage 2: `health_at_bar` (`READY_HEALTH_FRACTION`, 80% of max), `potions_at_bar` (`READY_POTIONS`, 3, by `healing.potion_count`), `better_weapon_armed` (the armed weapon ranks above the starting knife by the one weapon rule, `equip.weapon_rank`, A99, so a mallet over the hunting ceiling does not count), `armor_worn` (worn armor with `defense`). The arc's stage 2 bar is written from the same constants. The arc now says a stage whose readiness is met is done and moves on; staying needs a concrete, reachable reason named in the why, such as an upgrade listed in `upgrades_for_sale` whose `gems_short` the gems will cover soon; saving for gear not known to be for sale is not a reason. The contract says `upgrades_for_sale=none seen` means no better gear is known for sale anywhere. Stage 1 now says how to find a level entrance when none is known: a `travel` to `entrance` with no x, y walks to the nearest door seen on this map whose warp is not known yet, else the nearest door seen on this map, and does nothing while no door has been seen there (the `travel` row of `plan.OP_FIELDS` now says the same; it used to say "the nearest known"), so `explore_area` out past the town, read signs, ask NPCs; a later stage that needs an entrance and knows none comes back to stage 1. Tests: `test_strategist.py` (`test_readiness_shows_each_stage_check_by_check`, `test_better_weapon_follows_the_one_weapon_rule`, `test_stage_2_bar_in_the_arc_matches_the_readiness_constants`, `test_a_met_stage_with_no_upgrade_for_sale_has_nothing_to_save_toward`, `test_arc_says_how_to_find_an_entrance`). Observations: `docs/observations/A67_free_play_runs.md` **Run 13**. | A35, A92, A99, A87 |
| A102 | **Free-play run 13: one pack-room rule for every pickup, and the planner chooses every drop.** To make room for a bought potion, Shop dropped a box of matches on the shop tile; it came back as shop stock priced 5 gems, though the planner meant those matches for two clue areas. Shop, Pickup and Recover each made room by dropping the lowest `loot_score` held (`loot.pickup_room`), a code-level ranking of what is worth keeping, and the planner's State had no slot count, so a buy that needed a drop was planned blind. Rule, one function every state that picks up into a slot asks (`pack.make_room`, carried out for the states by `states.room`: Shop, Loot, Pickup, Level's prerequisite, Detour's pick; Gather takes only gems, which take no slot, and Recover's withdraw only takes what fits): a supply that fits, or a gem or life, is taken; with a full pack, what to give up is always the planner's call, never a code preference (an item not used yet still smashes, burns or fights). `buy` and `fetch_item` take an optional `drop` (the held item to give up); an op that names nothing it may drop is dropped with the reason and the droppable items, so the planner's next ask carries the choice (`states.room.planned_take`); the reflexes (Pickup, Recover) never drop and skip instead. Even a named drop never takes a supply the plan reserves (`pack.reserved_supplies`: for each op left on the stack, one carried supply it will use, by `use_block`'s or `equip`'s code, `fight_boss`'s `armed` and `worn`, `compose`'s held fragments (one per slot, as Solve composes them from what is held), or `break_block`'s tool for its capability, one per op unless a break does not use it up or the armed supply has it), and never lands on a known shop cell or a cell with a priced supply on it (`pack.drop_makes_stock`): a planned pickup that needs a drop there steps to a neighbouring cell still in reach first (`pack.drop_spot`), and with no such cell goes back to the planner too. New State `pack` (slots used and total, and the reserved items by code); `held` already shows what each item does (`use`: attack, cut, smash, burn…, with damage, defense, heal), and gains `breaks` for capabilities learned in play that the reference does not list; `upgrades_for_sale` lists a chest seen for sale whose Supplies-reference `chest_capacity` beats the slots carried now (`carry slots N -> M`), the other answer to a full pack; a contract paragraph on pack room says to weigh buying it against giving an item up. That a drop on a shop cell turns into stock is observed, not in the Manual (GAME_NOTES). `loot.pickup_room` and `loot.worst_droppable` are gone. AGENTS.md carries the rule. Tests: `test_pack_room.py`. Observations: `docs/observations/A102_pack_room.md`. | A20, A21, A35, A87 |
| A103 | **Free-play run 14: pacing stalls the op that cannot progress, and a block is tried only with a capability known to open it.** (1) Break and Explore alternated between two cells for 306 s: the oscillation guard fired 11 times, but it gives up only the active walk's target (Explore's frontier), so Explore picked the next frontier and Break kept its `break_block` op, whose steps toward the block reset its stall clock every time. Rule: when the guard fires and the top op's executor was one of the pacing states, and the walk it gave up was not that op's own (`OWNER_WALKS`: then the op picks another target, or drops itself as Travel does), the op stalls at once, by A104's rule (`states/dispatch.py` `_stall_paced_op`): remembered in `Memory.stalled_ops` and moved below the next op that can make progress, or dropped when none can; its `goal_stalled` or `goal_failed` reason names the executor, the cells and the other pacers, and the trace's `oscillation` event carries the op. Escape-only pacing (Flee, Retreat) is unchanged. (2) The stuck ladder tried every held capability (cut, chop, smash, burn) on every blocking cell, swapping the armed item each time: 34 `Arm`s, 10 blocks, about 120 s, nothing opened. Rule: a block is tried only with a capability known to open it, its block type's documented opener (`break_memory.BLOCK_OPENERS`, from the published "What breaks what": bush cut or burn, tree chop or burn, rock smash or blast, mountain and wall blast; GAME_NOTES Breaking blocks) or one that opened that cell before (`known_openers`); one that failed there is never tried again, as before. Stuck step 2 nominates (`nominate_on_path`) and the cost grid prices (`break_costs_for_planning`) only such blocks; with none on the route the step fails `no_known_opener` (`no_break_reason`) and the ladder reveals a way round, and the `stuck` signal carries that reason. A `break_block` op whose capability failed on that cell, that nothing held has, or that its block in view is not known to open is dropped at once with the reason (`break_state._op_refusal`), not left to stall for `PLAN_STALL_SECONDS`. Known gap: an oddity dressed as another block type that needs a capability its type does not list is not tried (GAME_NOTES, unverified). Tests: `test_oscillation.py` (`PacedOpTest`), `test_break_memory.py` (`KnownOpenersTest`, `StuckLadderOpenersTest`, `BreakOpRefusalTest`), `test_plan.py` (`test_break_block_with_nothing_to_break_with_is_dropped_at_once`). Observations: `docs/observations/A67_free_play_runs.md` **Run 14**. Pacing half subsumed by A90: every walk that paced is given up whichever state sent it, a given-up block is refused by Break, and the top op stalls only when its executor paced with a step that was no walk. | A15, A28, A34, A104, A87 |
| A104 | **Free-play run 14: a stalled op moves below work that can make progress, and its failure is remembered.** In 600 s the plan never let `gather_gems` run: a `travel` to a level entrance walled off, a `break_block` on a rock it could not reach and then could not break, and an `equip` sat on top through repeated stalls. The stall rule dropped each after 30 s, and the planner, whose own notes said "stuck" three times, sent the same targets back on top each reply; 0 gems all run. Rule, on the plan stack and the same for every op (A87): an op on top that makes no progress for `PLAN_STALL_SECONDS` (30) is remembered in `Memory.stalled_ops` (`stalled.py`: target, the op without its `why`; why; tick; and what was known then: the items carried, the clue count, and the ways known, door warps and level entrances), and moved just below the next op on the stack not itself remembered as stalled (`Plan.demote_current`, raising `goal_stalled` with the op now above it); with none, it is dropped as before (`goal_failed`). A directives op moves only among the directives ops, which stay on top. A planner reply that puts a remembered op back on top has it moved just below its first op that can make progress, or left out when it has none (`stalled.below_progress`, in `Strategist._settle`), and `last_reply_rejected` says so; an op already below one that can make progress stays where it is. A record holds until something gives a reason to retry (`stalled.retry_reason`, checked each window by `Runner.expire_stalled_ops`): an item carried that was not at the stall (a new opener), a new clue, or a new way known; losing an item is no reason. Nor does it come back on top when the ops above it end: `Plan.advance` moves a head still remembered as stalled below again, or drops it when nothing below can make progress. A remembered op that finishes is forgotten. `Runner.expire_stalled_ops` reads nothing while no op is remembered. The newest 8 are kept. New State `stalled_ops` lists each with its why and tick; the contract says what the rule does and to put work that can make progress above a stalled op, or give it a reason to work. Tests: `test_stalled_ops.py`; `test_travel.py` (`test_unresolved_op_moves_below_after_the_stall_window`). AGENTS.md carries the rule. Observations: `docs/observations/A67_free_play_runs.md` **Run 14**. | A34, A35, A87 |
| A105 | **Free-play run 15: with no region named, Gather works the ground where the character is, priced by walk and yield.** After a level entrance was given up about 100 cells from the old gem region, the planner sent `gather_gems` with no x, y ("gather where the character is") and `explore_area` nearby; Gather walked about 100 cells back to its old known grass instead, since it always walked to the nearest known grass and weighed unexplored ground only on safe ground or with every known cell barred. Rule, the same step-cost Gather prices every target by, no case for a give-up (A87): working anywhere (no target region it works only in), the cheapest known grass (`_nearest_clear`, priced by `_price`) is weighed against the nearest unexplored ground's edge clear of hostiles (`_explore_instead`; not on safe ground, in a skipped region, off `gather_ground`, or backed off by the explorers' stuck detection, `nav_stuck.filter_frontiers`; an edge Gather walks up to stops being one at the terrain read there, which fills every cell of the window). Each costs its walk plus one cut, an action like a step, for each gem the op still needs (`count` less the gems held) at the yield there (`GemOdds.cost`): the region's measured yield pulled toward the map's `expected_yield` (`gem_yield.region_yield`), and `expected_yield` on unsampled ground. The edge also pays the steps to find grass past it, one cell in (walkable + 1) / (grass + 1) of the map's known ground, so grass as near as the edge is always cut first. Far known grass wins only when its yield advantage outweighs the walk. The walk to the edge is a committed target (`FRONTIER`), kept until its cell stops being an edge, then priced again; `gather_status` says "walking to unexplored ground". The planner's contract and the `gather_gems` row of `plan.OP_FIELDS` say what no x, y does. Tests: `test_gather.py` (`GatherPricesUnexploredGroundTest`), `test_gem_yield.py` (`RegionYieldTest`). Observations: `docs/observations/A67_free_play_runs.md` **Run 15**. **Since A88:** the edge is always one of Gather's targets, priced the same way (`_price` plus `GemOdds.work`), with or without a target region (inside it while Gather works one). | A22, A63, A71, A87 |
| A106 | **Free-play run 15: a buy is done once its gems are spent and the item is owned, and State says what ended lately.** After buying the `middle_chest`, the planner kept `buy middle_chest` on the stack for 22 s and called the held chest "a consumable": State showed it as `class: consumable` (the Supplies reference's class for chests), and nothing in State said the buy had finished once its `goal_done` trigger had gone out with an earlier call. Rules: (1) a purchase finishes the head `buy`: Shop records the gems held each time it sends a Take for it, so gems gained after a refused or stale Take never hide the purchase that lands (`Plan.paid_from`, head-op progress like `held_before`, reset for each new head and kept across a probe's snapshot), and `plan.goal_done` counts the buy done once those gems are spent and the item is owned, held or stowed, or a chest that raised the pack, whatever the held-count baseline says. A new head starts unpaid, so a purchase seen after its buy already ended by the held count never finishes a second buy of the same item. (2) New State `ops_ended_recently` lists the newest 8 ops that finished (`done`) or were dropped (`dropped`, with why) in the last 300 s, newest first, with `seconds_ago` (`strategist.EndedOps`). It outlives the reply: a `goal_done` trigger goes out once, with one call, and a later prompt still says the buy is done. (3) `held` shows a chest as `class: chest` with `carry_slots` and `applied`, not as a consumable. Whether a chest raised the pack is one rule, `supplies.chest_applied` (larger than the starting chest, and the pack holds that many), read by both `plan.goal_done` and `held`. The contract says a done op is left off the stack. Tests: `test_planner_state.py` (`BuyDoneTest`, `HeldChestTest`, `OpsEndedRecentlyTest`). | A21, A35, A92, A102 |
| A107 | **Free-play run 15: State shows whether a known route reaches each level entrance, and the arc picks by reachability and cost.** The entrance chosen was 50+ cells into unexplored, blocked ground, nearest by straight line; the only route check was stuck detection after 90 s of walking. Rule: `level_entrances` gives each entrance on this map a `route` and `steps` (`navigation.entrance_routes`): `path_known` (a walk over known ground reaches it), `frontier_only` (the way runs through `unexplored` cells), `known_blocked` (a pocket known walls close off, or no way even through fog) or `not_searched` (not checked within the budget, including a known-ground retry it cut short); one on another map shows `other_map`. `steps` is the route the goal-first search found, up to 3 times the shortest walk, and the prompt says to compare it loosely. The check is one goal-first A* (weight 3) per entrance, nearest first, with a known-ground-only retry when the route crosses fog, all within one `FINE_NODE_BUDGET` shared by every entrance, so it costs no more than one cell-level search. So only the nearest few entrances are checked, and distant ones mostly show `not_searched`. The stage 3 arc says to pick an entrance by route and cost, not distance alone, and to explore toward one no known path reaches when that is the best option, rather than send one long travel into unexplored ground. Tests: `test_planner_state.py` (`EntranceRoutesTest`, `EntranceLineTest`). | A26, A35, A101 |
| A108 | **Free-play run 16: one Use-self path for a held consumable, and an `equip` that applies nothing is not done.** The `middle_chest` bought in run 15 was never applied: the pack stayed 10/10 and State showed it held but not applied. The planner tried `equip middle_chest` (finished as done, nothing applied) and then `use_block middle_chest` (an `Arm`, nothing more). The game docs (saims docs Gameplay.md, supply classes: "A chest upgrade is a consumable too") apply a chest by `Use` on yourself, like a potion; the agent instead raised carry capacity on the chest's `Take`. Rules (A87): (1) one Use-self path, `states/use_item.use_on_self` (the paced `Arm` + `Use` self, `Memory.self_use` for the refusal and drank-nothing filing of A76 and A80, `Memory.self_use_rearm`, re-armed by `use_item.rearm_after_use`, for the weapon put back), for every held consumable whose documented effect applies to the holder (`supplies.used_on_self`: class consumable with a `use_effects` in `SELF_EFFECTS`, `heal` and `chest` today; a new one, a timed effect, say, is added there). Heal drinks through it. (2) New op `use_item` (`code`), run by the new **UseItem** state: it uses one held item of that code on the character, is done once that item is used up (`Plan.used_id`, head-op progress like `paid_from`), and dropped with why when none is held or its `Use` was refused or did nothing; its validator takes only a `used_on_self` code, and its item is reserved from drops (`pack.reserved_supplies`). (3) A bigger chest is always better, so UseItem, a reflex below Greet, applies the held chest with the most slots above the pack's (`supplies.chest_upgrade`) as soon as one is held and no hostile is in range; never in the held-queue probe. (4) Carry capacity rises only once a chest's `Use` on yourself went through, its supply no longer carried or our own `SupplyUsed` naming it (`loot.learn_chest_upgrade`, from `Runner.file_noop_use`); never on a `Take` (the snapshot has no capacity field). A drink's health-rose and full-health causes, and the retry of a drink that did nothing once health falls (`supply_refusals.can_try`), apply only to items that heal (A89: one rule, `supply_refusals.effect_measure`): a chest whose `Use` did nothing is not used again after a hit. (5) An `equip` that names an item is done only when it is armed or worn; one not held, not armed or worn by its class (a potion or a chest: the reason points to `use_item`) or refused is dropped with why (`equip.cannot_equip_reason`); a named weapon while another state holds the armed slot waits. The contract says how a chest is applied. Tests: `test_use_item.py`; `test_loot.py` (`RunnerLootLearningTest`). | A24, A35, A47, A76, A87, A97, A106 |
| A109 | **Free-play run 16: a committed walk that balloons is priced again against the other cut targets.** Gather took 90 s (21–112 s) to reach a committed grass cell 18 cells away, walking a long way round, and kept it the whole way while grass in its own region lay much closer: A71 kept a target until reached or out of reach, whatever the walk came to cost. Rule: a committed target is also let go when the price of the walk still ahead rose since it was last priced (a wall found ahead, a detour) and it now costs more than the cheapest other candidate by both margins, `OUTPRICE_RATIO` (1.5×) and `OUTPRICE_STEPS` (8) (`targets.outpriced`, in the shared commitment code). The other candidates are priced only on such a rise, and only past both margins does the walk give way, so a nearer candidate alone never moves it and two near in price never trade places. Gather prices its kept pile or grass at each replan by the step price it picks with (`_price`, a pile `GEM_PILE_STEPS` cheaper; `gather._outpriced`), against the cheapest other known pile or grass. The rise is measured along the walk it last priced (`Memory.gather_walk`): the rest of that walk from the cell the character stands on. Standing off it, because another state walked the character away, is no rise (A71: another state walking in between is never a reason); the walk from there is the new baseline. The steps already walked are spent either way, so only the walk ahead is priced. Tests: `test_commit_target.py` (`BalloonedWalkTest`, including `test_another_state_walking_us_away_is_no_rise` and `test_a_long_way_round_gives_way_to_a_nearer_pile`). **Since A88:** Gather applies it to every committed target, edges and the unseen region included. | A71, A105 |
| A110 | **Free-play run 16: a buy needs a known seller.** The plan gathered gems "toward the raft" all run for the island door in the east lake, with no raft in `shop_prices` anywhere known, and stage 3 waited on it. Rule (A87): State `no_seller_known` lists the items the Supplies reference prices for sale in some world (`supplies.sold_codes`, a `gem_prices` entry; gems, lives, the starting kit, slot rows and other items never sold are left out, having no seller to find) that no shop known sells (`seen_prices`), that are not in sight or in a ground chest in reach, and that are not owned (`strategist.no_seller_line`). The contract says a buy needs a seller, that a gem price in the Supplies reference is not one, and that gems saved toward an item listed there buy nothing; a goal that needs one goes to find a seller or the item (travel to a named place, explore, ask NPCs, read signs) or works on a goal reachable now, and the arc's stay rule says the same for any stage. Tests: `test_planner_state.py` (`NoSellerTest`). | A21, A35, A87, A92 |
| A111 | **Free-play run 17: pacing gives up every walk that took part, a stuck walk's break walk included, and the block is remembered.** Break and Explore paced between two cells for about 100 s, 3 `oscillation` events. Break walked to the block the Travel walk's stuck step 2 had nominated, and Explore stepped back toward a frontier. The guard gave up only the active attempt, Explore's frontier, because Explore had moved last; the Travel attempt stayed at step 2, and its break came back for another 40 s. Rule (A87, the A103 guard generalized): dispatch notes which stuck attempt each move belongs to (`oscillation.note_move`, `walk_attempt`: the active attempt when the walk is its own, or Break's walk, `stuck.BREAK_GOAL`, while the attempt is at step 2 and the walk ends on the very block it nominated; a planner `break_block` or Heal's cut toward food to another block is no attempt's), and when the guard fires it gives up every attempt whose walk made one of the paced moves, not only the active one (`oscillation._give_up_walks`): one whose own walk moved is given up as before (`stuck.give_up`, reason `pacing`); one whose only moves were its break walk leaves step 2 for the reveal (`stuck.escalate`, reason `pacing`), and the block is remembered by A104's rule as a stalled `break_block` on the map it was on (`Memory.stalled_ops`, `StalledOp.map_id`, recorded for every stalled op; `stalled.remember`, `find` and `forget` match a record of a target that names a cell on its map (`stalled.map_local`: x, y and no `map_id` of its own) only on that map, the plan reading them for the map it last settled on, `Plan.map_id`, and any other op's record on every map, so a stalled `buy` or `equip` still waits for a reason to retry after a map change; why `Break paced between … walking to it: no way to the block`), so the planner sees it in `stalled_ops`, a planner `break_block` for it is held below work that can progress, and no stuck ladder, cost grid or Heal walk nominates it on that map (`stalled.break_cells`, by map, the `skip` of `break_memory.nominate_on_path`, `break_costs_for_planning` and `no_break_reason`, which reports `stalled_break` when only remembered blocks stand on the route) until a new item, clue or way gives a reason to retry. Break now keeps the block it nominates on the attempt (`stuck.set_break_target`), so the walk the guard gives up names it. The `oscillation` event lists each walk given up (`goals`) and the blocks remembered (`breaks`); an op whose own walk, its step 2 included, was given up stays on top, as A103 says. Tests: `test_oscillation.py` (`PacedBreakStepTest`, including `test_a_break_walk_to_another_block_is_not_the_attempts`), `test_break_memory.py` (`StalledBreakTest`, including `test_a_block_remembered_on_another_map_is_nominated_here`), `test_stalled_ops.py` (`MapScopeTest`). Observations: `docs/observations/A67_free_play_runs.md` **Run 17**. Subsumed by A90: the guard keys each move by its walk's target, not by the active attempt (in free-play run 18 the Break walk was filed under no attempt while Explore's was active, so only Explore was given up), and a given-up block lives in the walk-side give-up memory (`break_memory.given_up_blocks`), not in `stalled_ops`; `stalled.break_cells` and `walk_attempt` are gone. | A15, A103, A104, A87 |
| A112 | **Free-play run 17: a buy of an item eaten on pickup is done once paid, and State says what such an item does.** The planner bought a `golden_cap` (heal 6, eaten on pickup) at 10/10 health. The Take landed and gems fell by 8, but the cap was eaten on its Take and never held, so `plan.goal_done` (paid and held, A106) never counted the buy done: Shop kept the op and walked 100 s toward another shop until the planner dropped it. Rules (A87): (1) a buy is done when its Take lands and its price is paid, whether the item is then held or used up on the Take: `plan.goal_done` reads the A106 per-head paid progress (`Plan.paid_from`) and counts the item got when it is held or stowed, a chest that raised the pack, or used up on its Take (`supplies.eaten_on_pickup`, the Supplies reference's `eaten_on_pickup`). Only a Take that landed pays: a Take refused (`shop.note_shop_result`) or gone stale (`shop.sync_shop`, now run before the plan settles) clears `paid_from`, so a later gem drop, from a death say, finishes no buy. (2) New State `for_sale_eaten_on_pickup` lists the items seen for sale that are eaten on pickup, with price and what the reference says they do (`strategist.eaten_on_pickup_line`, `supplies.what_it_does` now carries `eaten_on_pickup`). The contract says such an item applies the moment it is taken and is never held, so a heal eaten on pickup bought at full health heals nothing and wastes its gems. No code rule forbids the buy: the call is the planner's. Tests: `test_planner_state.py` (`BuyDoneTest`, `EatenOnPickupForSaleTest`). | A21, A54, A106 |
| A113 | **Free-play run 17: a fight decided on holds until it ends.** Travel and Fight took turns against one hostile at its post for 20 s: Travel stepped past it, Fight closed in and swung once, the hostile stepped back to its post out of `hostile_range`, and Travel walked on past it again (6 swings, 3 hits landed, 3 damage taken). Fight's target was only a hostile in range, and the engagement ended once none was in range and none had hit within `THREAT_MEMORY_TICKS`, so a walking op resumed between swings. Rule (A87, the A94 engagement generalized): with no hostile in range and none hitting, an engagement whose decision is fight holds while Fight still has a target in it, and Fight goes on with that target.
