The target is one rule for both: `fight.fight_target`, the nearest hostile in range, else the nearest member still in view (`engagement.held_members`; no boss, nothing `never_attack` forbids), when Fight can swing at it or step closer (`fight.held_target`, passed by dispatch to `sync_engagement`). So the engagement never says fight with nothing Fight can act on. A chase after a member out of range must work, as running must for Flee: once the gap has not fallen below the nearest it has been for `CHASE_PROBE_TICKS` (30, Flee's probe window), the member is out of reach (`engagement.chase_on`, `Engagement.chase`). The fight ends when its target is dead, gone from view, forbidden or out of reach, or the decision turns (health, hits and misses, a newcomer, running found not to work), and Retreat, above Fight, still runs on its own rule. A flee decision ends as before. Observations: `docs/observations/A67_free_play_runs.md` **Run 17**. Tests: `test_engagement.py` (`FightGoesOnTest`, including `test_a_chase_that_never_closes_ends` and `test_the_fight_ends_with_the_target_fight_cannot_swing_at`; `test_the_engagement_ends_when_nobody_is_in_range_or_hitting`). | A23, A94, A87 |
| A114 | **Free-play run 18: with the ground in reach all seen, the way on is the planner's, and it sees the ground cell by cell.** The character entered its first level (map 17), talked to the scarecrow (hedges burn with a match) and read the sign, then took random steps for 45 s within 7 cells on "look around: nothing left to explore" while holding 3 matches, and never burned a hedge. In run 19, in the same level, a hostile beside it shut every neighbour, so the same fallback held "boxed in" for 70 s and asked no one, and the planner, which saw no cells, aimed its one burn at the cell the hostile stood on. Rule (A87), the planner decides and is given what it needs: (1) the safe default, with no reachable frontier, never steps in place and never parks because of a hostile beside it: it raises one `no_way_on` trigger per cell and sends nothing (`explore.ask_the_planner`; `_look_around` removed), only when no frontier is left to walk to at all (`explore.NOTHING_TO_EXPLORE`), never when a frontier's step is only taken for now or its walk is escalating, which holds the decision; (2) State `map` draws 15 cells each way round the character on every call, overworld and levels alike, one character per cell, with a y label per row, an x ruler and a legend of the marks used: ground, grass, hazards, water, each breakable type, doors (locked apart), signs, unseen and no-ground cells, and known hostiles, bosses, other NPCs, characters, gems, items, items for sale and chests in view (`strategist.map_lines`); (3) the contract states only the rules the planner needs: how to read the map, that a block is destroyed only by an item whose capability opens its type and that a `break_block` names the cell and the capability, that a destroyed block may grow back, and that no attack can be made from a safe zone; held items with their capabilities and the clues read were already in the prompt; (4) Break drops a `break_block` aimed at a cell in view with no block on it (walkable ground, where a hostile stands, or no ground) with why, as it already did a block whose type's openers are known and exclude the capability (`break_state._op_refusal`); a block of a type with no known opener (a door, a type the rules never name) may still be tried, so its opener is learned. This covers `break_block` only; the `use_block` half of run 19 defect 2 is A119. (5) **The planner can choose a fight, inside a level or out.** Fight never ran on a safe tile, and no op named a hostile, so a character pinned on safe ground beside a weak hostile could only wait (run 19: about 265 s beside one snotling, on and off). A `fight` op names an NPC (`npc_id` or `npc_type`) and Fight carries it out whatever the engagement would decide; an `npc_type` fight takes the nearest of that type when one first comes into view and pins its id (`Plan.fight_npc`), so it is one fight, never the next of the type in turn or a nearer one mid-fight; it is done once that NPC is dead or out of sight, and Fight's `act` drops it with why when its NPC was never in view, is a boss (`fight_boss`'s), is `never_attack` or no swing or open step reaches it (`fight.can_engage`; its guard only reads). The planner contract says what `fight` and the `cornered` trigger mean. No swing is ever sent from a safe tile, where no attack can be made; Fight's move there is the step off it toward the target (`fight.off_safe_step`), for the op and the reflex alike, and the retreat tail walks back behind the swings; at the health floor that step is never taken, or Retreat walks back and Fight steps off again until death, so the op is dropped with why for the planner (`fight.held_on_safe_ground`). State `nearby_npcs` gives each NPC's cell and its swing at us as the engagement prices it (expected damage through our armor, every 15 ticks, whether its type has hit us before, a boss's health), and `our_swing` ours, so the planner can judge the fight; the engagement rule itself (A94) is unchanged. (6) **Cornered, never standing taking hits.** Run 19 died 10 → 0 in 17 s holding 2 potions: Flee had no step and held "nowhere to flee" for 8 decisions with no drink and no swing. Cornered, Flee now drinks a carried heal that is due (Heal's own drink, `heal.use_carried_heal`, by the potion rule A96), else swings at the nearest hostile in weapon reach it may attack, below the health floor too (standing still only takes free hits), else raises one `cornered` trigger per cell and holds for the planner (`flee.cornered`). **Since A120** the `no_way_on` ask is every executor's too, not only the safe default's (`states/ask.py`, `states/way.py`): an obstructed op routes round, opens a block with a held item, leaves a hostile to the engagement or a planner's `fight` op, and only then asks, with `blocked_by` and `tried`; a `fight` op whose Fight can neither swing nor step asks without holding the round, so Flee still acts, and the A114 drop of a `fight` op no open step reaches stands. Tests: `test_no_way_on.py`, `test_fight_op.py`; the M4 test world's hedge is a `tree`, the type the game gives hedges. | A9, A28, A35, A87, A94, A96, A103 |
| A115 | **Free-play run 18: carry capacity is derived from what the server tells us, never assumed.** Run 17 applied a `middle_chest` (Use on yourself) and the pack rose to 30, but run 18 started at 10: `WorldModel.carry_capacity` began at the blue chest's default on every start. The API serves no capacity and no carried chest's subtype (checked against the saims API docs: snapshot `inventory`, `GetSelf`, the owner sheet; history omits `SupplyUsed`; **Server gaps**). Rule (A87): capacity is recomputed at every inventory sync (`WorldModel.sync_carry_capacity`) as the carried chest's Supplies reference `chest_capacity` (`carried_chest`, the chest a `Use` on yourself applied, `loot.learn_chest_upgrade`; None for the blue chest a character starts with), no more than a `carry_capacity_full` refusal left room for (`full_at`, `loot.full_bound`: the slots carried for a `Take`, plus `n - 1` for an all-or-nothing `WithdrawFromChest` of `n` supplies), and never below the slots the inventory carries now. The applied chest, the one input only this character's own `Use` shows, is kept per character in the world knowledge base (`kb.extra` `carried_chests`, `loot.load_carried_chest` at start, `save_carried_chest` at exit); the capacity itself is never stored. A respawn brings the blue chest and clears it; a full refusal whose bound is below the recorded chest's size forgets it. Tests: `test_carry_capacity.py`. | A47, A108, A87 |
| A116 | **Free-play run 18: a reach-limited intent is sent only from a position the server confirmed within reach.** Pickup's `Take` was refused `target_not_nearby` 3 times: the reflex fired while a walk queue was held, judged reach from the tracked cell, and the walk's unseen Steps had already moved the character. Rule (A87), one check for every reach-limited intent (`reach.in_reach_from`: `Take`, `WithdrawFromChest` and `DepositToChest` one block, corners included; a `Use` on a block, an NPC or a character the armed weapon's reach, or the reach of what an `Arm` earlier in the same queue arms, one block for a tool): when a reflex replaces a held queue, its intents go only if each reaches its target from every cell the held walk may have taken us to (`held_queue.possible_positions`), the same gate that already stops a Step that may land on a blocked cell (`executor/held_queue.unconfirmed_send`); otherwise we stop where we are and re-read position before the next decision. Without a held queue the position is already confirmed (a position read, or our own Steps' results). The reach rules live in `reach.py` (`NEAR`, `weapon_reach`, `supply_reach`), which Pickup, Fight, Break and Solve read. Tests: `test_reach.py`. | A20, A61, A87 |
| A119 | **Free-play run 19: a `use_block` is sent only where something there accepts it.** Run 19's Solve re-sent match `Use`s on a snotling's cell and its death cell, neither holding anything usable, and plan validation did not check the target (`docs/observations/A67_free_play_runs.md`, run 19 defect 2). A114 covers the `break_block` half through the breakable opener table. Doors, pedestals and similar `use_block` targets have no such table, so the rule is general: a `use_block` whose target cell, as State last showed it, holds nothing usable is refused at plan validation with a reason the planner sees, and a `Use` that resolves with no change is not re-sent unchanged. Tests cover an empty target cell and a no-change `Use`. | |
| A120 | **Free-play run 20: every op keeps its walk and clears its own way; the safe default never stands in for it.** Under a `travel` to the NE level entrance (532, 311), Travel's walk failed its cautious window at (530, 349) and climbed to stuck step 2, where Travel yields so that Break opens the nominated block. Break had no move that decision, so Explore ran as the safe default and walked the character south, then toward a frontier 120 cells west, until time ran out, 36 cells short (`docs/observations/A67_free_play_runs.md`, run 20; the runner session's read of the trace: no `stuck`, `oscillation` or `goal_stalled` record, `travel` the only op throughout, Break walking north to (532, 343) only at 597 s). The trace keeps no Travel reason, but the code path fits only one reading: a cautious window that fails climbs to step 2 and returns no step once a block is nominated, so the route search did not fail; the walk was in step 2, and the safe default walking for no op kept it from finishing. Rule (A87), Evan's option A for travel: an executor keeps the walk for its own op; Explore runs for an explore op or for no op, and never stands in for Travel (`explore.HOLD_THEIR_WALK`). (1) On unexplored ground Travel walks toward its destination through unseen cells, priced as passable until seen otherwise, and replans as cells come into view (the cost grid already does this). (2) When Travel cannot resolve or take a step toward its destination, it raises one `no_way_on` trigger with why and the op (`states/ask.ask_the_planner`, shared with the safe default's A114 ask; one ask per op, cell and reason, `Memory.no_way_on`) and holds the decision while the planner answers; unanswered, the op stalls and is moved below (A104). At stuck step 2 it yields to Break as before, and Explore holds rather than walk the character off the way, so the ladder's window runs out and it goes on to reveal or gives up. (3) The survival reflexes act above the hold. (4) With `--no-planner` the built-in plan answers the ask by exploring the ground round the cell it came from (or anywhere, once that is explored), then the op runs again (`plan.builtin_way_on`). (5) Each tick record in the trace keeps `yielded`, why each state above the one that decided sent nothing. The planner contract says what a travel's `no_way_on` means. **Widened by Evan to every op with a target** ("if a goal or target is obstructed we need to try to unobstruct it, not get distracted"): the op's own executor keeps the walk; obstructed, it routes round, then opens or breaks the block with a held capability (`break_toward`, the opener table) or deals with a hostile under the A114 fight rules; only when it cannot clear the way does it raise `no_way_on`, saying what blocks it and what it tried, and hold, survival preempting. Explore then runs only for an `explore_area`, with no op, or as the `--no-planner` answer. #219 was the travel slice and the shared ask. **The rest (every other op):** one ladder for every executor (`states/way.py`, PLAN.md **Architecture**): (a) the op's walk routes round; (b) with no way round, the executor opens the first block on the cheapest way a held item is known to open (`way.open_the_way`, priced as Heal's walks are, A89, and carried out by `break_toward`; never the op's own target cell, so a `use_block` target a held item could open is used, not opened), or, for a walk on the stuck ladder (Travel, Shop `buy` on its walk to a shop or town, Level `enter_level`, Boss `fight_boss`), Break opens the nominated block at step 2 (`way.break_due`), only when the stuck attempt is the op's own walk by its goal label (`way.OWNER_WALKS`, moved from dispatch, which reads it for the stall clock too): an attempt left at step 2 by another walk (the safe default's, Heal's, an earlier op's) never stands in for the op's ask, so Break never opens another walk's block while the op waits; (c) a hostile on the way is left to the engagement (Fight is above every executor) or to a planner's `fight` op, never fought by the executor on its own, and is named in the ask; (d) only then one `no_way_on` with why, the op, `blocked_by` (the hostiles whose reach the way crosses and the first block on the straight route, with whether a held item opens it, `ask.what_blocks`) and `tried` (only this op's own attempts since it reached the top, A129), and the decision holds (`way.clear_the_way`). Per op: Loot (`fetch_item`), Investigate (`read`, `say`), Solve (`use_block`), Shop (`buy`, its own step to a supply and Travel's `walk_to` to a shop or town) open the way themselves (Loot and Investigate before their bounded walk would give the target up, Solve and Shop once their walk finds no step); Level, Boss and Travel leave it to Break at step 2, then open it themselves or ask; Break (`break_block`) with no way to its block asks with what blocks it; Fight's `fight` op, when it can neither swing nor step, asks without holding, so Flee below it still acts (its A114 drops stand); an op with nothing to work on asks at once with why (Loot with none in sight, its walk to one in sight given up asking by that one's cell; Investigate with no such NPC; Solve with the item or a fragment missing; Shop with nothing to take, or no shop or town known; Gather with nowhere to work, its one search already covering unexplored ground, A105; Boss with its op's conditions not met, so Boss now guards on the op and asks instead of leaving it to the safe default). Equip, `use_item` and `wait` have no walk: they finish, drop with why, or hold as before. **Every op whose executor cannot act asks, once:** the safe default's hold under an op (`explore.hold_for`) asks for an executor that sent nothing and neither asked from this cell (`Memory.no_way_on`) nor left the decision to Break at step 2 of its own walk (`Memory.break_yield`), as Equip waiting on a busy armed slot does (`no_way_on` "equip: Equip has no move"); a planner's `wait` is its own hold and `use_item` uses, waits out a cooldown or drops with why, so neither asks. **A shut way is not searched again while it stays shut:** `clear_the_way` keeps what it was found under (`Memory.way_shut`: the executor, the op, the map, the cell, the target, the map's tiles by `Tiles.revision`, what is held, and what lapses by time alone: the blocks backed off from, `break_memory.given_up_blocks`, so a block whose A90 backoff lapses is tried again (widened by A129 to every walk given up and still backed off, the op's own included), and the cells the route learnings keep out or price, `plan_sets`) and skips `open_the_way` while that holds, and `blocked_by`/`tried` are worked out only for an ask that goes out. Explore runs only for `explore_area`, with no op, or as the `--no-planner` answer (`explore.HOLD_THEIR_WALK` is every executor but Explore). One ask per op, cell and reason (`Memory.no_way_on`, `ask.ask_key`; the safe default's hold asks for an op unless that op asked from this cell, an earlier op's ask never standing in), reset whenever the op's executor acts again (dispatch). The planner contract says any op may send `no_way_on` with `blocked_by` and `tried`. `held_supply` moved to `break_memory` so the way helper can reach `break_toward` without an import cycle; `dispatch.OWNER_WALKS` lists Shop's `travel:*` and `explore_area` walks, so Break at step 2 of Shop's walk is the buy's progress. Tests: `test_travel_holds_its_walk.py` (walking on into unseen ground, one ask and a hold, Explore holding at step 2 and the ladder going on, survival during the hold, the ask dedup, the built-in answer, `yielded` on the decision); `test_runner` (the trace's `yielded`); `test_clear_the_way.py` per op family (obstructed then cleared: Loot, Investigate, Solve, Shop open the tree themselves, Level, Boss, Travel through Break at step 2; obstructed then asked: one ask with `blocked_by` and `tried` and a hold, a hostile named, Break with no way to its block, an op with nothing to work on, a `fight` op that cannot close; survival during the hold for every walking executor and for `wait` and `use_item`; `HOLD_THEIR_WALK` covers every executor); `test_states.DispatcherFallThroughTest` (an executor with nothing to do asks and holds, one that yields leaves Explore holding and asked for once); `test_clear_the_way` also: a stale step-2 attempt of another walk never stands in for Loot's ask, Equip with a busy slot asked for once, `wait` and `use_item` asking no one, and the shut way searched once until the tiles change; `test_break_memory` (another walk at step 2 never stands in for a stalled buy). | A15, A27, A34, A87, A104, A114 |
| A121 | **Free-play run 20: the readiness bars are guidance the planner weighs against their time, not a gate.** The stage 2 potion bar (`READY_POTIONS`) cost about 300 s of a 600 s run: two small potions at 10 gems each came from grass at about 0.2 gems a cut, and nothing in State or the arc weighed that time against moving on (`docs/observations/A67_free_play_runs.md`, run 20). Under A87 the call stays the planner's; no coded switch. **Rate:** `GemYieldTracker` measures the current gather, the stretch of rounds with a `gather_gems` on top (`note_gathering`, from the runner each round): every gem the counter gained while it was on top over its minutes. Once it has run `RATE_MIN_SECONDS` (60), State `gather_run` adds `gems_per_min` and `gather_minutes`; the figures stay shown after the gather ends, until the next one starts. Any other op on top for a round (a `buy` put ahead, an A104 demotion) ends the gather, so the next one measures afresh and shows no rate for its first `RATE_MIN_SECONDS`; survival reflexes (Heal, Flee, Fight) act under the op on top and do not end it. **Cost:** State `readiness` stage 2 adds `to_close` (`strategist.cost_to_close`): for each unmet check a purchase closes (`potions_at_bar`, `better_weapon_armed`, `armor_worn`), the `gems_short` of the cheapest seen for sale that closes it, each alone (none for gear carried, which an equip closes), and `minutes` at `gems_per_min` (left out before a rate is measured; `NO_GEMS_EARNED`, `never at gems_per_min 0`, while gems are short and the rate shows 0). A check nothing seen for sale closes, or that no purchase closes (`health_at_bar`), is left out. **Arc:** the readiness bars are guidance the planner weighs, not a gate; a bar whose cost in time outweighs what it is worth may be skipped, moving on, and the why names the trade. A skipped check stays skipped while its minutes still outweigh what it buys: by itself it neither drops the character back a stage nor calls for a stage 3 restock, so drop-back and restock apply only to checks not skipped for their cost; it is weighed again when its minutes fall. Tests: `test_strategist.py` (`test_readiness_shows_what_each_unmet_check_costs_at_the_gem_rate`, `test_arc_weighs_the_bars_against_their_time`, `test_a_check_skipped_for_its_cost_sends_no_drop_back_or_restock`), `test_gem_yield.py` (`test_state_shows_the_current_gathers_gem_rate`, `RunnerGatherRateTest` through the runner's full tick). | A62, A63, A87, A101 |
| A122 | **Free-play run 21: every death chest of ours is remembered, the planner sees each, and Recover takes back any in reach.** Evan watched the character walk past one of its own death chests in the wilderness, left by an earlier death, and ignore it. `WorldModel` held only the latest death chest, in memory only, so a later death or a restart forgot it; State never named a death chest; and Pickup left every death chest to Recover, which knew only the latest. The published docs (checked 2026-10-11) give no owner on a chest entry or tile and no expiry for a dropped chest; `self.death_chest` names only the latest. Under A87 the planner gets the facts: `WorldModel.death_chests` holds every chest our deaths dropped (from `Died` and the served `death_chest`), by chest id with map, cell, the tick it dropped and the item codes last seen in it, and drops one once it is seen empty, or gone while in reach (out of reach, a chest missing from sight may only be unseen); a chest dropped is not remembered again, even while `self.death_chest` still names it (its id is kept, the latest `death_chests.GONE_KEPT` across runs). `knowledge_base.death_chests` keeps them per character across runs (`death_chests` and `death_chests_gone` in the world file). State `death_chests` (`strategist.death_chests_line`) lists the nearest `DEATH_CHESTS_SHOWN` and a count of the rest: cell with distance and bearing or another map, contents or `unknown`, and `in_view`; the contract tells the planner to `travel` to `point` at its cell to take one back. Recover withdraws from any remembered death chest in reach, wherever it lies (one with nothing that fits the pack is passed over for the rest), and walks as a reflex only to one on a known-safe spot: the newest anywhere on this map, an older one once in view. No new op. | A11, A20, A114 |
| A123 | **Supplies reference rows carry capabilities (saims B138), and a weapon's speed counts (saims B147).** The site's `supplies.json` replaced each row's `use_effects` and per-stat fields (`attack_range`, `damage`, `heal`, `teleport_range`, `chest_capacity`, `defense`, `light_radius`, `grants`) with a `capabilities` list: each entry a capability (`attacks`, `cuts`, `heals`, `holds`, `protects`, …), its trigger (`used`, `equipped`, `worn`, `picked_up`) and its `amount`, `reach`, `cooldown_ms` or `duration_seconds` (saims Manual **Supplies reference**, API **Supplies reference**). Weapon families now swing at their own cooldown, 0.8 s (the knife) to 1.5 s (bows) in Olympuff, carried as `cooldown_ms` on `attacks`. (1) `supplies.parse` reads the new shape into the same `Supply` fields: block openers from `cuts`/`chops`/`smashes`/`burns`/`blasts`, `heal` from `heals`, `chest_capacity` from `holds`, a weapon's `damage` and `attack_range` from `attacks`, an armor piece's `defense` from `protects` (armor without it 0, as before), and `use_effects` as the agent's names for what armed or used gear does (`supplies.USE_EFFECT_OF`); and the new `attack_cooldown_ms` from `attacks`. It still reads the shape before B138, so a stale cache works; both shapes give the same `Supply` for every subtype served. Nothing read the old `light_radius`, `teleport_range` or `grants`, so they get no field. (2) The weapon cooldown counts wherever attack timing or weapon ranking is computed: every `Use` is paced by the cooldown of the supply in hand, converted at the world's tick rate (`executor.pacing.attack_cooldown_ticks` with `PlayContext.tick_hz`: the runner's single `Use`, Fight's and Boss's attack queues, and an `Arm` + `Use` such as Gather's cutter or a drink, `states/intents.arm_and_use`), where a fixed 10 ticks drew `attack_cooldown` from a mallet; the pacers take the cooldown as a required argument, and `DEFAULT_WEAPON_COOLDOWN_TICKS` is derived from the default cooldown at the default rate, for costs measured in swings only; the win estimate swings once per cooldown (`survival.attack_interval_ticks`, on the default-rate scale its assumed hostile interval uses), retiring `OUR_ATTACK_INTERVAL_TICKS`; an empty slot or a non-weapon ranks 0 for arming and `upgrades_for_sale` (nothing armed swings nothing), so any held weapon within the ceiling is armed into it, while the win estimate keeps A81's assumption that such a slot swings as the starting knife (`survival.fighting_weapon`); `equip.weapon_rank` orders by damage a second, compared exactly as a fraction (`equip.damage_per_second`: one swing's expected damage over the cooldown) within the hunting ceiling, so the bronze sword (1 s) outranks the harder-hitting bronze mallet (1.3 s); a weapon's reach falls back to the reference's `attacks` reach before play learns it (`supplies.attack_reach`). Hunting-ground strength is unchanged: attack power, weapon damage and worn defense, no cooldown (saims Manual). State `held` gives a weapon's `cooldown_ms`, `our_swing` its `every_ticks`, and `upgrades_for_sale` the damage a second gained. The knife's 0.8 s makes the win estimate 25% kinder to the starting kit: at default params it now takes an unmeasured weak hostile it refused before; the regen probe profile's `fight_margin` rises 2.4 → 3.0 to keep its thresholds. (3) The bundled `reference/supplies.json` is the new shape; the old table is a test fixture (`tests/fixtures/supplies/before_b138.json`). (4) Checked and unchanged: saims B142 (NPC parameters on the capability model, `invincible` dropped, NPC health runtime state) changes no field the agent reads: NPC entities still carry only a boss's `health` and `max_health`, and nothing here read `invincible`; saims B146 (a trap strikes with attack power equal to its grade) is a game note: the agent prices no trap hit. Tests: `test_supplies.py` (both shapes parse to the same `Supply`, every question answers alike on the old and new tables, the cooldown is read, every question on the new shape), `test_executor_pacing.py`, `test_runner.py`, `test_fight.py`, `test_boss.py` and `test_gather.py` (a `Use`, and an `Arm` + `Use`, waits the cooldown of the supply in hand, at 10 Hz and 20 Hz), `test_survival.py` (a swing every cooldown), `test_equip.py` and `test_strategist.py` (damage a second ranks weapons; any weapon held is armed into an empty slot, and is an upgrade with no weapon owned; the win estimate keeps its knife), `test_reach.py` (the published reach). **Since A153:** only a weapon's swing or break is paced by the cooldown (`executor.pacing.use_cooldown_ticks`); a drink, a chest, a tool or a trap `Use` and its `Arm` go out on the next tick, and a refused drink retries on `supply_refusals.REFUSAL_WAIT_TICKS`. | A54, A81, A99, A116 |
| A124 | **Free-play run 21: what closing a readiness check costs, in time, for health and for upgrades.** (1) `health_at_bar` sat unmet for 440 s at 7/10 and stage 2 was never met: A121's `to_close` left health out (no purchase closes it), the planner assumed health recovers while gathering off safe ground, and Heal defers to the op on top above `low_health` (A89). (2) Saving 50 gems for an upgrade at 2.3 gems a minute (about 22 minutes) kept the plan in stage 2 all run, and `upgrades_for_sale` gave only `gems_short` to weigh it by (`docs/observations/A67_free_play_runs.md`, run 21). Under A87 the call stays the planner's: facts and the real rule, no coded switch. **The rule, from the published docs:** health comes back only from a heal (food eaten, a potion drunk) or a respawn, at full health; the docs give no regeneration, safe ground only stops damage, and resting hurt there has measured none. **State:** `readiness` stage 2 `to_close` now always shows an unmet `health_at_bar` (`strategist.health_to_close`): `health_short`, and the ways it closes: `drink`, a carried potion and its heal (the smallest that closes the gap, else the largest); with none carried, `buy`, the cheapest potion seen for sale with its `gems_short` and `minutes`; and `rest` (the nearest known safe tile, `walk_seconds` there at the character's speed, `rest_minutes_estimate` at the measured rate) only once safe-zone regen is a confirmed "yes" on the world and a rate was measured. The rate is timed between rises, never from wherever a rest began (which reads high): on Heal's rest decisions back to back on safe ground (`healing.note_regen_rate`), each stretch from one health rise to the next adds its rate to the world's newest `REGEN_RATES_KEPT` (`healing.add_regen`), and `regen_per_tick` is their median, so each rest refines it and one bad stretch cannot set it; a world saved with a "yes" and no rate is measured the same way. Any heal sent, by any state (a potion or food `Use`, a food `Take`: `note_heal_pending`), ends the stretch and the yes/no sample; so do any other decision between rests, a fall in health and a change of max health, and timing starts again at the next rise. It stays rough (a rise is seen up to one decision late), so State calls it an estimate. The docs publish no rate to fall back on. Carried food is left out: Heal eats it whenever hurt. `upgrades_for_sale` adds each upgrade's `minutes` at `gather_run` `gems_per_min`, the same as `to_close` (`strategist.minutes_at`, shared). **Contract:** health does not come back by itself, on safe ground or off it; a rest heals only where `to_close` lists one; an op that leaves health below a bar leaves it there; the ops that close it are a `use_item` (drink before health is low), raising `low_health`, a `buy`, or a `travel` to the safe tile then `wait`. The arc says what `to_close` shows for health, and that with no way listed nothing the character has or sees for sale closes it. No new op, no change to Heal. Tests: `test_strategist.py` (`test_readiness_shows_how_an_unmet_health_check_closes`, `test_contract_says_health_does_not_come_back_by_itself`, `test_upgrades_for_sale_show_the_minutes_at_the_gem_rate`), `test_heal.py` (`test_measures_regen_then_saves_yes`, `test_regen_rate_is_timed_between_rises_and_refined_by_each_rest`, `test_one_bad_stretch_cannot_set_the_rate`, `test_the_rate_counts_only_once_regen_is_confirmed`, `test_a_heal_sent_by_any_state_ends_the_stretch`, `test_another_decision_between_rests_ends_the_stretch`, `test_food_eaten_ends_the_stretch`, `test_only_yes_is_read_from_knowledge_base`). **Superseded by A130** for regen: the `rest` route, the rate measure (`note_regen_rate`, `add_regen`, `regen_per_tick`) and their tests are gone; `to_close` shows `eat` and `buy` (held heals count toward the bar, A132), and the contract says health rises only from potions and food. | A89, A96, A101, A121, A87 |
| A125 | **Free-play runs 21 and 22: a walk on an op's behalf makes net progress toward its target (A87).** Run 21: from 0 to 148 s, 0 gems. Detour set off three times for the gem-pile cluster near (414, 414) and each was given up short, with Gather's walk to (435, 410) going back north between them. Root cause: `DETOUR_TICKS` gave a detour 100 ticks (10 s) to take its find, but a walk moves about a step a decision, so a detour of 10+ cells timed out on time alone; only that one pile was skipped, so the next pile of the same cluster was picked next. Run 22: Travel's legs to the north-west entrance (300, 316) went (375, 398) → (374, 399) → (392, 407) → (371, 382) `[cautious]` → (388, 371) over 50 s and ended 5 cells farther off, held on "Travel holds its walk". Root cause: each leg is a window-sized path along a corridor priced through unseen ground; a reveal that made a tile on the corridor dearer searched it again and wiped what the window search had learned (`NavSearchState.reset`), so the next leg could lead back over ground already found to go nowhere; and each ladder level measures progress on its own plan from a fresh window, so a walk along one leg after another counted as progress while it stood no nearer. **Rule:** (1) a detour is taken only when its gain beats its cost (`detour.allowance`) and, once taken, is kept until its find is taken or a concrete reason drops it (no step, no progress over its walk's window, the find gone, its ground, path or risk no longer clear); never for the time it takes (`DETOUR_TICKS` is gone). (2) Each leg brings the target nearer by what the walk knows: the window search's learning is kept when the corridor is searched again for the same goal (`NavSearchState.reset`), so every leg ends below the estimate the walk has just learned for where it stands. (3) Every level of the ladder (A15, A90) counts progress only when the plan's measure falls below the lowest the walk has had since it was taken up (`NavAttempt.net_best`, `stuck.observe`), not below that level's own, so no fresh level forgives a walk that stands no nearer. The measure is the path's length plus the rest from its end by the corridor search's estimate when the walk has one for its target (`stuck.measure`, `planner.rest_estimate`: along the corridor's tree, raised by what the window search learned), else the straight line, so a route round an obstacle, even one wider than the window, lowers it as it is walked and is progress. The whole ladder still runs (walk, cautious, break, reveal, alternate route), and once it gives up the executor clears the way or asks the planner (A120), the ask's `tried` leading with how each level failed (`GiveUp.reasons`, `way.walk_given_up`). A walk taken up again after another state moved the character, or a new way found (`stuck.walk_again`), starts the bar afresh. Tests: `tests/test_walks_make_progress.py`. | A15, A90, A105, A120 |
| A126 | **Free-play run 22: a Supplies cache is fresh only in the current row shape.** Run 22 loaded a cached `supplies.json` saved 58 minutes earlier, before the game deploy that moved the table to `capabilities` (saims B138). It was in the old shape (`use_effects` and per-stat fields), and `supplies.load` used it because it was under the hour max-age, so all 11 weapons lacked `cooldown_ms` and swung at the 1000 ms default (`docs/observations/A67_free_play_runs.md`, run 22). Rule: a cache is fresh only when it is young and in the current shape, every row carrying a `capabilities` list (`supplies.current_shape`); an older-shape cache is stale whatever its age, so the agent fetches the served table and replaces the cache. When the fetch fails it falls back to the cache at any age or shape, else the bundled copy, as before, and the run log's `supplies reference` line names the shape it used (`stale cache, shape before B138`, `bundled, capabilities shape`). The bundled `reference/supplies.json` was checked against the served table (2026-10-11): it is the capabilities shape and identical. Tests: `test_supplies.py` (a young old-shape cache is refetched and replaced; a young current-shape cache is used with no fetch; an old-shape cache is used, and named, when the fetch fails; the bundled copy is the current shape). | A54, A123 |
| A127 | **Free-play run 22: a planner reply holding several JSON objects gives its last plan.** The reply held a plan, then "Correction:" and a second plan. `parse_reply` sliced from the first `{` to the last `}`, so the slice was not JSON and the whole reply was dropped, a rest-on-safe-ground plan with it; health stayed 7/10 for another 50 s. Rule: a reply that is not one JSON object is scanned for every top-level object (`strategist.json_objects`: from a `{` to the `}` that balances it, braces in strings not counted; a span that is not valid JSON is skipped whole), and the last one `parse_plan_payload` accepts is used, so a correction wins. Acceptance is `plan.accepts_plan`, built on `plan.plan_fields`, the top-level step `parse_plan_payload` itself runs: an object with at least one of `goals`, `params` or `notes` that it keeps. Unknown keys are dropped, a wrong-typed key is refused alone, and `"params": null` counts as omitted. With none, parsing is as before. More than one object found is logged. The contract already asks for one JSON object only. Tests: `test_strategist.py` (`ParseReplyTest`), `test_plan.py` (`AcceptsPlanTest`). | A82, A87 |
| A128 | **Free-play run 23: walks keep off the reach of hostiles seen, a pack is fought whole, and a walk under attack never stops to wait.** At 293 s, travelling to the NE entrance, a bogmaw, wartlurch and grubhulk pack came into view 8 cells off; Travel kept the path it had and walked on beside the pack, taking hits from 301 s. Retreat then stopped twice on "the step may land on a blocked cell", a potion was drunk, and Fight chose to close on npc 226, one of the three; the character died at 310 s from 6/10 (`docs/observations/A67_free_play_runs.md` **Run 23**, defect 1). Rules (A87): (1) **A walk on an op's behalf keeps off the reach of the hostiles it has seen and the engagement would not fight.** `hostile_ground.walk_reach` is the ground within `hostile_range` plus `WALK_REACH_MARGIN` (1) of every hostile in view, and what remembered ones hold (`known_reach`); `pathing.op_walk_sets` prices it `costly`, so the walk routes round a pack. A hostile in view the engagement would fight (`engagement.would_fight`, its pack counted, and not forbidden by `never_attack`) is left out: a hostile on the way is the engagement's (A120, Evan: "if a goal or target is obstructed we need to try to unobstruct it, not get distracted"), so the walk goes on and Fight takes it in range. Only hostiles the engagement would not take on (a pack, one too strong) and remembered ones out of view, which it cannot weigh, are kept out of. `pathing.guided_step` takes it as `reach`: a kept path (or committed walk) that comes to enter it is planned again, and a new plan that still enters it is not walked (`pathing.enters_reach`; the cells it walks while leaving the reach it stands in do not count). The same holds at every level of the stuck ladder: the plan an escalating walk would walk, and a reveal step, are held to the reach. The path is dropped and the walk goes back to its first level with a fresh window and no break target (`nav_stuck.hold`), so the ladder never climbs on the hold and no reveal, alternative-route or break walk goes through, and the executor clears the way or asks the planner (`way.clear_the_way`, A120's `no_way_on`) and holds; the walk goes on once a way round opens. `blocked_by` names the hostiles in view whose reach the way crosses, and the remembered ones out of view by the ground they hold (`ask.what_blocks`); the contract tells the planner a fight op on one out of view is dropped, so it routes elsewhere, waits for the ground to fade, walks near to see it again, or explores. Every op executor's walk takes its sets from `base.op_walk`, once per decision, and hands the same `reach` to the way it clears: Travel's walk and Shop's walks (`travel.walk_to`, and Shop's walk to a supply in sight), Level's walk to an entrance and to the item a locked entrance needs (the stop is held, not given up, while the entrance walk goes on), Loot's and Investigate's `bounded_step` walks (`bounded_step`'s `reach`: held with no give-up), Solve's walk to a cell to use from, and Break's walk to its block. The way an executor clears keeps to it as well: since A129 a walk with no step opens the first block on its way, and it does so only when the walk to that block stays out of the reach (`way.open_the_way`, `break_toward`'s `reach`), else it asks. Walks with their own rule keep it (Gather and Detour, A105; Heal and Retreat, A89, A9; Recover walks only to a known-safe spot, A122); Fight's and Boss's ways, whose target is a hostile, and Explore's frontier walk, do not take it. (2) **The engagement's estimate weighs every hostile that can reach the character** (`engagement.decide` over `survival.can_reach_us`): the group's members in view, every other hostile in view within `FIGHT_REACH_RADIUS` (6, the path planner's danger radius) of us, and any within `GROUP_JOIN_RADIUS` of one counted, so a pack counts whole. Who Retreat runs from and the group's growth (`combat_group`) are unchanged. A 3-pack the estimate cannot beat decides flee, so Retreat or Flee runs, never Fight closing on one of it; `drink_turns_fight` and `would_fight` weigh the pack the same way. (3) **A reflex walk under attack never stops for a position it cannot confirm.** When the Steps replacing a held queue may land on a blocked cell (`held_queue.STEP_DOUBT`) and a hostile hit us within `THREAT_MEMORY_TICKS`, the runner (`Runner.move_under_attack`) sends the best Step that lands open from every possible position (`held_queue.open_step`: the walk's own first Step, else the one keeping furthest from the hostiles hitting us, then nearest where the walk was going; never one nearer the nearest of them), else hands to Flee's cornered move (`brain.cornered_decision`, `flee.cornered`: a due heal, a swing in reach, or the planner's `cornered` call). Not under attack, it stops and re-reads position as before. Tests: `test_hostile_reach.py` (a weak lone hostile on the way is left to the engagement and fought, a strong one asks; a route round a seen pack, and round one that comes into view mid-walk; a pack across the only way asks the planner, never walks through or climbs the stuck ladder, and the walk goes on once it moves off; a walk already at an alternative-route, reveal or break level is held and sent back to its first level, and so is a level the ladder climbs to in the same decision; a block beside a pack is neither walked to nor opened, and the walk asks; Loot's `fetch_item`, Investigate's `read`, Shop's `buy` in sight, Solve's `use_block` and Break's `break_block` each hold on a strong pack across the only way and ask once; Level's walk to an entrance's key holds and keeps its stop; `blocked_by` names a remembered hostile out of view; the pack's damage summed in the win estimate and a 3-pack not fought; Retreat under attack sends a Step, or Flee's cornered swing, not a Wait; the cornered move runs in the decision's own context, `brain.play_context`, so it is paced at the world's tick rate and runs no op while parking). | A87, A94, A113, A120 |
| A129 | **Free-play run 23: an op tries for real before it asks, and says only what it tried.** At 511 s Travel to the NE entrance (532, 311) raised `no_way_on` "no step toward it" with `blocked_by` "tree at 521,352" and `tried` "a way round, then opening a block on the way with a held item", but no `Use` went to (521, 352) and no break was recorded there, though the armed bronze_sword chops; the planner answered `explore_area` (510, 340) r12, which ended at once, and Travel's give-up lapsed at 541 s with no op left to retry it (`docs/observations/A67_free_play_runs.md`, run 23). Two causes: `way._tried` reported what was held, not what was done; and `way.open_the_way` counted a way round through unexplored ground that the walk, already without a step (its destination given up), could not take, so it chose no block. Rule (A87): (1) `tried` lists only what this op actually attempted: the walk's search for a way round, then each break the knowledge base records since the op reached the top (`break_memory.attempts_at`, `Plan.top_since_tick`: the records are kept across runs, so an older one is another op's or run's) and a `Use` in flight (`Memory.break_pending`), with its result, on a block of the way: the cells `open_the_way` picks from (the priced way, then the straight route) and the blocks it chose for that target from other cells since then (`Memory.way_blocks`). With the position unknown it is the search alone. (2) Once the executor's walk has no step (`way.clear_the_way` calls `open_the_way(..., no_step=True)`), only a way round over known ground counts; the way is priced with the held openers' blocks at their break and unexplored ground beyond at fog cost, as the cost grid does, and when the cheapest way still goes round through unexplored ground, the first block on the straight route a held item opens is the one opened, before any ask. Loot and Investigate, which call `open_the_way` before their step, still count a way round through unexplored ground, which their walk takes. (3) A shut way is searched again when any walk's give-up on the map lapses, the op's own included (`way._shut_key`), so a give-up that lapses with the op still on top has its walk and its way-clearing run again. The planner contract says `tried` is only what was done. Tests: `test_way_tries.py` (a tree on the way, unexplored ground beyond and round, a bronze sword held, the walk given up: a step to the tree and a chop, no ask; `tried` is only the search with nothing done or the position unknown, names a recorded failed chop, a chop still in flight, a chop on a block chosen from another cell, off the straight route from where it asks, and never a break recorded before the op reached the top; the op walks again once its give-up lapses); `test_clear_the_way` (`tried` with nothing opened is the search alone). | A15, A87, A90, A120 |
| A130 | **Free-play run 24: health rises only from potions and food; there is no natural regeneration.** Evan, the game's designer, corrected the premise A10, A60, A84 and A124 were built on: in Agent Realm "rest" means nothing; health does not regenerate on safe ground or anywhere, and rises only from heals (a potion, food, or food eaten on pickup); the Manual describes no regeneration either. In free-play run 24 Evan watched the character run into town, walk round doing nothing, and go back out: Heal's walk to safe ground and its regen sample (`REGEN_MEASURE_TICKS`, about 20 s) and the planner's `rest` route at work. Rule (A87: give the planner true facts; remove a workaround built on a false premise): (1) Heal heals only by food (in reach, or carried) and a potion due (`healing.spend_potion`); with neither it raises one `heal_supplies` trigger (health, max health, potions held) and sends nothing, so the op on top goes on. It never walks to safe ground to wait for health, at any health; safe ground stays the refuge Retreat, Park and Flee walk to from attack. (2) Gone: the regen sample and rate (`regen_known`, `save_regen_yes`, `regen_per_tick`, `note_regen_rate`, `add_regen`, `note_regen_sample`, `Memory.heal_regen_*`, `RegenStretch`), Heal's safe walks (`heal_rest`, `heal_measure`, `heal_explore`, `pathing.HEAL_TARGET`, `SAFE_WALK_GOALS`), the A60 regen probe and the M7 gate's regen clause. (3) State `readiness` `to_close` `health_at_bar` shows only real ways to close it (`strategist.health_to_close`; held potions and food already count toward the bar, A132): `eat`, the nearest free food in sight that Heal's own rule lets it take (`healing.food_to_take`: the refusal table and the `heal_food` backoff, shared with Heal's pick), its heal, where it is and the `walk_seconds` there; `buy`, the cheapest potion seen for sale with `gems_short`, `minutes`, and `at`, the nearest known shop, with the `walk_seconds` there (nothing records which shop stocks the potion, so that shop may not). Never a `rest`. (4) The fact is stated once in `docs/GAME_NOTES.md` **Healing** and in the planner contract (safe ground, potions, the arc); `low_health` now means only when a carried potion is drunk. (5) A knowledge base an older agent saved loads cleanly: `survival.safe_zone_regen` and `safe_zone_regen_rates` are dropped at load, and the `survival` section with them when left empty (`knowledge_base.RETIRED_KEYS`). (6) With Heal yielding when it has nothing to heal with, Pickup reaching food in reach now asks the refusal table before it sends a `Take` again (`supply_refusals.can_try`, A80), as Heal does. Tests: `test_heal.py` (`test_nothing_to_heal_with_never_walks_to_safe_ground_or_waits`, `LowHealthLineTest`: the op goes on at any health, no safe-ground walk with no op, the line spends a potion, Retreat still walks to safe ground under attack); `test_strategist.py` (`test_readiness_shows_how_an_unmet_health_check_closes`: eat and buy with the walks, a held heal counted, no `rest` even with old regen keys; `test_contract_says_health_rises_only_from_potions_and_food`); `test_knowledge_base.py` (`test_saved_regen_keys_are_dropped_on_load`); `test_strategist.py` `test_eat_shows_only_food_heal_would_take`; `test_m7_acceptance.py` (`test_a_full_hour_asks_no_regen_measure`). | A10, A87, A89, A124 |
| A131 | **Free-play run 24: a buy walks only toward a seller of its item.** The plan was to buy a raft (30 gems), sold by the lake fisher in the east town near (626, 389), known from his line "Raft, thirty gems". With 30 gems, `buy raft` sent Shop's walk west, hopping between shop cells in the west town that sell no rafts: (408, 395) → (383, 385) → (389, 371) → (382, 368) → (371, 394), never toward the fisher 200 cells east (`docs/observations/A67_free_play_runs.md`, run 24; Evan: "ran into town and walked around, did nothing there… seems aimless"). Cause: Shop walked to the nearest known shop cell out of sight, whether or not it sells the item (`states/shop.py` `_shop_cells_out_of_sight`, `_shop_out_of_sight`). Rule (A87): (1) a buy walks only toward a seller known to sell its item (`sellers.sellers_of`, the one seller test): a shop cell last seen selling it, which the knowledge base now keeps with its price (`travel.knowledge.record_shop_cell` `sells`, `iter_shop_sales`; a cell holds one supply, so a new sighting replaces it, and a cell in sight without the item forgets it, `forget_sale`); a sign, scroll or NPC line with a phrase naming it and a gem price, at the cell it was found; or the place the `buy` op names (new optional `x`, `y`, `map_id`, all three or none, so a warp never moves it: the planner's reading of where a clue says the seller is, a town say). **Knowledge base change:** shop cells saved before A131 have no `sells`; each is kept as an `unchecked` seller of every item a price was seen for (`iter_unchecked_shop_cells`), walked to only after every known seller, and checked off once in sight (its `sells` recorded from what is there, or emptied). (2) Of several, the cheapest walk is taken (this map first, then the straight-line estimate). A shop cell is walked to until it is in sight; a clue's or the op's place is searched within `SELLER_SEARCH_RADIUS` (20) once it is in sight (an `explore_area` round it), and when that search runs out the place is searched no more for this buy (`Plan.sellers_searched`, A106 head progress) and the next seller is taken, and the knowledge base keeps it as no seller of the item with the tick (`travel.knowledge.note_not_sold`), for every later buy, `refuse_unsold` and State, until newer evidence the item is sold lapses the record: a clue offering it found after the search, anywhere (`sellers.lapse_not_sold`, run after each clue is captured), or a shop seen selling it (`record_shop_cell`); a shop cell reached without the item counts the same, so stale shops are never walked between, and once every seller was reached the buy is dropped with how many. (3) A reply's `buy` with no seller known is left out with why in `last_reply_rejected` (`sellers.refuse_unsold`, A110's seller rule); one that reaches the top anyway (a directives buy) is dropped by Shop with why, never sent on a walk, and the walk to town to look for any shop is gone. A price seen with no place is no seller: State `no_seller_known` reads the same test. (4) New State `sellers`: for each item the stack buys (each buy op resolved as Shop resolves it, its own place included; `no_seller_known` too), then up to `SELLER_ITEMS_SHOWN` (8) more a shop is known to sell (nearest first, with how many more), its sellers nearest first with `by`, `at`, `map_id`, `steps` (a straight-line estimate, `other_map` off this map), the shop's `price`, and `search_radius` for a place to search; `none known` for a wanted item with none. The contract says what it means and to give a buy the place a clue names. Tests: `test_shop.py` (`ShopWalksToASellerTest`: a raft sold only by a far NPC walks toward it past nearer shops, and is searched for round where it spoke; an item with no known seller is dropped; the nearer of two sellers is chosen; a clue names the item and a gem price in one phrase; `StaleSellerTest`: two stale shops each reached once, then the buy dropped; `PreSellerShopCellTest`; `SellerPlaceTest`: x, y and map_id together, a plan seller across a warp; `SearchedPlaceTest`: a place searched without the item is remembered, a re-sent buy refused, a named place refused until a newer clue, records lapsed by a newer clue or a shop seen selling it; `ShopOutOfSightTest`: a seller known only by town is walked to and searched for there; `RunnerLearnsWhatAShopSellsTest`), `test_planner_state.py` (`SellersLineTest`: the line, and a reply's buy with no seller refused with why, one naming its seller's place kept; `NoSellerTest`). | A21, A71, A87, A110 |
| A132 | **Free-play run 24: the health bar counts held heals, so no potion is drunk only to meet a readiness bar.** At 238 s the planner drank a potion at 7/10 because "Health 7/10 is 1 short of the stage 2 bar"; that left 2 potions, one under the potion bar, and the plan then flipped between buying a raft and regathering gems for a third potion (347 s, 382 s, 436 s), finishing neither (`docs/observations/A67_free_play_runs.md`, run 24). The bars were read apart: meeting the health bar with a drink broke the potion bar it had just met. Rule (A87: the planner's facts, no coded switch): (1) stage 2 `health_at_bar` counts held heals: health plus the heal of every potion and food carried, held, stowed or armed (`healing.held_heal`, each by its Supplies `heal`), must reach `READY_HEALTH_FRACTION` of max; `readiness` stage 2 shows that sum as `health_with_held_heals`. `to_close` `health_at_bar` drops `drink` (a drink leaves the sum where it was): `health_short` is what health and held heals together still lack, closed by `buy` (the cheapest potion seen for sale, now shown with potions carried too) or `rest`. (2) The contract says a potion is for an actual health need (the survival floor, `low_health`, or a fight ahead that needs the health), never drunk only to meet a readiness bar. (3) The arc says the potion bar and the health bar trade against each other through held heals, so drinking to meet one breaks the other. No change to Heal or `POTION_RULE`. Tests: `test_strategist.py` (`test_held_heals_count_toward_the_health_bar`: 7/10 with two potions held meets the bar; `test_readiness_line_shows_health_with_held_heals`: stowed potions and held food summed on the line; `test_contract_says_a_potion_is_never_drunk_only_to_meet_a_bar`; `test_readiness_shows_how_an_unmet_health_check_closes` without `drink`). | A87, A96, A101, A121, A124 |
| A133 | **Free-play run 24: a walk crosses water with a fording supply, and nothing strands it there.** The character bought a raft and headed for the lake door at (659, 400); `supplies.py` mapped `fords` to the `water` effect, but no walk read it: water was never ground to a walk, and nothing armed the raft before a water step (`docs/observations/A67_free_play_runs.md` **Run 24**). The Manual's Supplies capability table: water is walkable while a `fords` supply is armed or worn, and on water an `Arm`, `Wear`, `Remove` or `Drop` that would leave none is refused `would_strand`. Rule (A87), one rule for every walk and every queue: (1) a walk plans over water while a fording supply is carried, held, armed or worn (`supplies.fords`, `WorldModel.fords`), passed in explicitly and never set on the view: `WorldModel.walkable` and `WorldModel.frontier` (the water's edge a frontier too), `CostGridParams.fords` for the cost grid, `open_the_way`, the held queue and every walk, and the character's own for `door_graph`'s searches of other maps, so a route through a door crosses water too; (2) every queue a state sends is followed from where the character stands (`fording.ford_queue`, applied once in `dispatch._run_states`): before the first move onto water with none armed or worn, a held fording supply is armed; on water, a loadout change that would leave none armed or worn ends the queue there, so Equip, Gather, Break, Solve and a drink send no `Arm` mid-water unless the fording supply is worn, what the state wrote to memory is put back only when the cut dropped a loadout change (`fording.dropped_loadout`, `fording.LOADOUT_MEMORY`: the re-arm and pending records of Break, Solve, Gather and a self `Use`), never for a Step cut after a kept `Arm`; a reflex's lone Step under attack (`held_queue.open_step`) counts water as open only while a fording supply is already armed or worn (`WorldModel.afloat`), and Flee's cornered move goes through the same gate and rollback as dispatch (`fording.ford_gate`, one helper for every send path); a drink whose `Arm` would strand is skipped in `flee.cornered` itself, so the swing or the `cornered` ask acts instead and no reflex stands taking hits, and a queue cut to nothing falls through like a state that sends nothing; (3) ashore with a fording supply armed and no water within `REARM_LOOKAHEAD` (8) cells of the walk ahead (`Memory.path`, so an island hop or a walk along the shore keeps it in hand), a queue that is no escape (`fording.ESCAPES`: Flee, Retreat, Escape, whose ticks matter more) and neither moves onto water nor arms anything itself first arms the weapon the one weapon rule picks; Fight's attack queue arms it first itself, its swings paced by the weapon's cooldown, since a fording supply swings nothing (`fording.rearm_ashore`, `equip.weapon_to_arm`, A99); (4) the raft is armed, not worn (its `fords` trigger is `equipped`), so on water it leaves no weapon: under attack there (hit recently or a hostile in range) with only a fording supply in hand, the reflex Ashore, above Retreat, Heal, Fight and Flee, walks to the nearest reachable land priced by the cost grid's hostile danger, and the normal fight-or-flee rules take over once ashore (`states/ashore.py`, `fording.stranded_unarmed`); a walk on an op's behalf already keeps water cells within a seen hostile's reach costly (A128 `walk_reach`), so it does not start a crossing such a hostile can intercept; (5) the planner's `equip` op arms anything the Supplies reference puts in the armed slot, a tool such as the raft as well as a weapon (`supplies.armed_in_hand`, `equip.named_equip`): in run 24 `equip raft` was dropped as "not armed or worn"; (6) a `would_strand` refusal marks the cell the character stood on when it was refused as water, each such cell kept up to `WATER_CELLS_KEPT` (`fording.note_would_strand`, `Memory.water_cells`), so the same change is not sent from there again. State `held` shows `fords` true for a fording supply, and the contract tells the planner that water is walkable with one, that nothing is armed or drunk on water, and to fight and drink from land. Tests: `test_fording` (a path across water only with a fording supply carried; the walk arms it before the first water step; no `Arm`, drink or drop of it on water, a worn one excepted; Equip on water falls through without a loop; the weapon armed again ashore, not while the next step is water; a `would_strand` refusal marks the cell it came from, each kept; a cut `Arm` leaves no re-arm or pending record; no re-arm in front of an escape (dispatched on land with the raft in hand) and one in front of another reflex (Pickup), none with water on the walk ahead; a kept `Arm` before a cut Step keeps its memory; a lone Step under attack treats water as open only afloat; a cornered drink on water is cut and leaves no `self_use` record, and Flee's cornered move then swings or asks, never stops (A114); landed with the raft in hand, Fight arms the weapon, then swings at its cooldown; on water no weapon is armed for a fight; reading the view sets nothing on it; a route through a door crosses water; the water's edge is a frontier with a fording supply; attacked on water with only the raft in hand it steps for land, not a weapon, and not once the raft is worn or it stands ashore; a walk's crossing keeps off water in a seen hostile's reach). | A87, A99, A120, A123 |
| A134 | **Free-play run 24: oddities are shown to the planner.** Evan watched the character walk past blocks without ever checking what they yield (`docs/observations/A67_free_play_runs.md`, run 24). Grass beats ordinary trees for gems, so that is fine, but nothing pointed the planner at oddities, which pay far better: the Manual puts one hand-placed oddity in each god's region, two steps off its road, a block out of place (one rock in a garden, one bush in a wheat field, one tree in a maze) that, broken with the capability it needs, always drops a gem cache worth 5, 7 or 10 gems by ring and returns in 10 minutes; and a block type alone where it does not belong is often destroyable and worth checking. Rule (A87: the planner gets the facts and decides): (1) `oddities.find` judges the blocks of the State map's 31x31 square on the terrain the agent remembers: a breakable block is odd when no other cell of its 7x7 neighbourhood is of its type, at least 24 of its 48 neighbours are seen, and one other type holds at least half of those seen; (2) each one found is remembered in the world's knowledge base (`oddities`: `"<map_id>,<x>,<y>": {block, seen}`) and judged again on every look: one whose cell still shows its block but no longer passes the rule (more of the ground round it seen) is dropped, while one whose block is gone stays so a broken one shows when it is back; its tries are break memory's rows for its cell; (3) State `oddities` (`oddities.oddities_line`) lists the 5 nearest on this map, found or remembered, with cell, block, `may_break_with` (each held capability known to open its type or that opened it, not failed there, else `none held`), `failed`, `last_try` (capability, result, tick) and, once opened, `back_at_tick` (10 minutes on); (4) the contract says what an oddity may yield with the Manual's numbers, and the planner breaks one with `break_block` (Break already refuses a capability that failed on the cell). Tests: `test_oddities` (a lone rock in grass is listed and a field of rocks is not, a remembered oddity no longer odd once more is seen is dropped, a broken oddity carries its return time, a failed capability is recorded and not offered). | A114 |
| A135 | **Free-play run 24: food first out of a fight; a potion is for a fight.** Evan, watching run 24: "I saw them waste a potion again. There are bushes everywhere and berries everywhere. Potions should be reserved for battle and levels." The runner session's trace: both drinks in run 24 were the planner's `use_item small_potion`, sent as "arm and use small_potion", never the Heal reflex, the cornered rule or readiness code: at about 238 s in part 1 (7/10 to 10/10, "1 short of the stage 2 bar", no hostile engaging, no food within 10 cells) and about 60 s into part 3 (7/10 to 10/10 in the home town, a snotling's hits a few seconds before but none engaging or within 8 at the drink, no food within 10 cells; a potion bought again soon after). Rule (A87, one rule for every drinker): (1) **in combat or about to be** (`healing.in_combat`: under attack, `survival.recently_attacked`; a hostile in range; a boss fight; inside a level, `map_level` above 0; or, for the planner's drink, a fight next on the plan, `use_item.fight_ahead`: `fight`, `fight_boss`, `enter_level`, or `gather_gems` with `fight`) a potion is spent as before, at the low line or when the drink turns the fight (A96). (2) **Out of combat** lost health comes back from food: food lying within `healing.FOOD_REACH` (3 cells) first, then a bush there a held blade cuts (`cut` only: burning one spends a match), whose berries are then taken like any food. The bush and its drop come from `supplies.BLOCK_FOOD` (a bush drops berries, Manual §16), and only an item a break does not use up counts (`supplies.kept_on_break`). Food near is one test: `healing.food_near` lists the candidates (on A130's `healing.food_to_take`), State shows them, and `states/food.food_route` prices them by Heal's one walk price (A89; Heal's ground and walk grids move to `states/food.py` so UseItem reads the same route). A potion is drunk out of combat only at the low line (`low_health`, the survival floor) once Heal could act on no food near this decision (a target no walk reaches, or a break that sends nothing, lets it drink). In combat a potion that is due goes before any walk for food, and Heal breaks no block for food in combat. (3) Every drinker asks the one rule, `healing.spend_potion`: the Heal reflex, Retreat losing ground and Flee cornered (A114) through `use_carried_heal`, and the planner's `use_item`, which asks `spend_potion` too, ordered (in combat or with a fight next, the planner's call stands), with Heal's food route, and UseItem drops a drink it refuses with why (`use_item.potion_refused`; the route is only looked at, so Heal's food commitments stay as they were) out of combat while Heal has a food route (the why names the food, its cell, heal and walk, and State `food_near`; food no walk reaches is no reason) or while health is above the low line (put the `use_item` directly above the fight it is for); A132 already stopped drinking for a readiness bar. (4) State `food_near` lists the food and bushes near with kind, cell, code, heal and `walk_seconds` (straight walk at the character's speed, `healing.walk_seconds`, the one helper A124's and A130's `to_close` use too; the line reads the run's memory, so what Heal leaves out it leaves out, and it is not shown without one), the heal route out of a fight. (5) `POTION_RULE` and the safe-ground contract say a potion is for a fight and food heals out of one. `break_memory` now takes `CAPABILITIES` from `supplies`, as `plan` does, so `healing` may use it. Tests: `test_food_first.py` (at 7/10 with berries or a bush near, food is taken or the bush cut and no potion drunk; at the low line food near still goes first; food no walk reaches, or a bush whose break sends nothing, lets the potion be drunk at the floor; a bush only a match opens is no food; under attack at low health a potion is drunk with food near, by Heal and by `use_carried_heal` as Flee and Retreat call it; recently attacked, Heal drinks before cutting a bush; in a level a potion is allowed; `fight`, `fight_boss`, `enter_level` and `gather_gems` with `fight` next let the planner drink; the planner's out-of-combat potion is refused with the food or bush named, not for food no walk reaches, and above the low line with none; at the survival floor with no food near a potion is drunk; State `food_near`). | A87, A89, A96, A114, A124, A130, A132 |
| A136 | **Free-play run 24: a fight decided on holds, Fight closes by a walk, and two states taking turns with no walk are settled.** From 234 s to the end of part 3 (366 s) the character paced (432, 416) and (433, 415): Fight "close on npc 247", then Flee, every second, on a snotling standing still at (435, 413). No swing, no hit; 91 `oscillation` events gave nothing up, since both states' steps were no walk (`unwalked`). Cause: Fight closed with a greedy step (`fight.close_step`, a neighbour strictly nearer), so from (432, 416) it stepped to (433, 415), where the one nearer cell was shut; there `can_engage` said no and Flee ran ("the target is out of weapon reach with no open step closer"), and from where Flee stepped back a nearer step was open again. The engagement still said fight throughout; what flipped was whether Fight could act, read off the cell it stood on. Rules (A87, A113 generalized; a drink is weighed against the held decision, `engagement.drink_turns_fight`, so a fight held on lower odds spends no potion to clear the bar again but still drinks when the drink lifts it above break-even, and `cannot_outrun` keeps what was weighed): (1) **Fight closes by a walk.** `close_step` is the first step of the shortest walk over open ground to a cell within weapon reach, searched within `APPROACH_RADIUS` (12) of the target, or the character's perception when further: ground fixed round the target, never a bound measured from where Fight stands (that would shrink as it closes and drop a walk that loops out), so every cell of the walk can still close and `can_engage` (read by Fight, Flee and `held_target`) never changes as Fight closes; Boss closes the same way. (2) **A fight decided on holds** (`engagement.fight_holds`): a decision of fight stays fight until the fight ends, health reaches the survival floor against the hostiles weighed (`survival.at_health_floor`), none of them is left in view (the floor always applies, so a fight with nothing weighed in view ends), or a hostile the estimate had not weighed joins it (`Engagement.weighed`, the group and every hostile `can_reach_us` counted). A hit or miss that moves the odds no longer turns it alone; a newcomer, a pack, still can, so a second hostile still sends the character running. (3) **The oscillation guard settles a flip with no walk** (`oscillation.bench`): when two or more states paced with steps that were no walk and nothing came of it since the window began (no hit taken, no swing sent), the losing side (the one going against the engagement's decision: Flee while it says fight, Fight while it says flee; else the state that took the last step; never the side carrying out the decision, Fight while it says fight and Flee or Retreat while it says flee, and never Retreat, which runs at the survival floor; when only those paced, a `pacing` trigger with `benched: null` asks the planner once per flip episode) is benched for `FLIP_COOLDOWN_TICKS` (`BACKOFF_BASE_TICKS`), lifted early once the engagement's decision changes; dispatch skips a benched state (`oscillation.benched`). The same states flipping again within twice the cool-down raise a `pacing` trigger, and the contract tells the planner to decide for it. Observations: `docs/observations/A67_free_play_runs.md` **Run 24** (defect 1). Tests: `test_fight_flee_no_flip.py` (a stationary weak hostile behind a corner is closed on and killed with no Flee between; a walk that loops out can close from every cell of it; a fight with no weighed hostile in view ends; Retreat is never benched, nor Fight while it says fight; the fight holds on a drop in the odds and turns at the floor or on a second hostile; an unwalked Fight/Flee flip benches Flee within the window, a turn in the decision lifts it, a second flip asks the planner). | A87, A90, A94, A113, A114, A128 |

**M8: Gear, economy and combat.** Loot (A20) ships walk, take, withdraw, drop-when-full, and gems-first for ground code `gem` (GAME_NOTES, Obs). Larger-chest capacity is A47's, assumed in play; hearts-first reads the Supplies reference (`heart`, class `life`, A156), which retired A47's life-code learning, and the Manual answered stowed `Drop` (A152, A154).

| ID | Item | Depends on |
|---|---|---|
| A17 | **Per-world knowledge base.** `python/.state/worlds/<world_code>.json`: load, save, sections, shared by the world's characters. Loaded once when `run` starts, saved once at exit; one `run` process per world. | |
| A18 | **Item table.** `items` in the knowledge base, keyed by `supply_subtype_code`. A row holds only served facts. `attack_range` and `gem_price` are positive integers that overwrite the last: `attack_range` from a `Use` rejected `target_out_of_range` (it carries the reach judged by, B100), filed under the subtype armed in that same response's observation (`get_self`'s `attack_range` names no subtype and can trail an `Arm`, so it is not used); `gem_price` from supplies on entity reads and snapshot entities. `heal_amount` and `heal_on_pickup` were A24; A155 retired them (dropped at load, `knowledge_base.RETIRED_ITEM_KEYS`). `life_on_pickup` is A47. `weapon_damage` is `{npc_type_code: max observed hit}`: the largest `NPCDamaged` amount this subtype has landed on that NPC type, raised only by a larger hit. A hit counts only when it is the one `NPCDamaged` on the block and tick an applied `Use` resolved, no other character was in sight when the `Use` applied and at the response's observation (`NPCDamaged` reaches everyone who sees the block and a miss emits nothing, so any other possible attacker makes the hit unattributable), and exactly one NPC was seen on that block when the `Use` applied (its type is the key); it is filed under the armed subtype after that response's observation. Damage is rolled from 1 up to a cap the target's defense lowers (docs/GAME_NOTES.md Combat), so the number is a lower bound on the weapon's best hit against that type, not the weapon's damage stat. Nothing reads it since A99: Equip ranks weapons by the published `damage`. `damage_taken` is `{npc_type_code: max observed hit}`: the largest `Damaged` amount from that NPC type while this subtype is the only filled worn slot. `damage_without` is `{npc_type_code: max observed hit}` from that NPC type while no worn slot is filled, filed only under the subtype that was worn alone and taken off by our own `Remove` (none after two or more pieces come off together, or gear lost any other way, such as death), and only until the next loadout change, death or map change. Both count a hit only when the worn loadout held for the whole response: `Damaged` events cover the response's ticks but the loadout is read at its end, so a response whose snapshot loadout changed, or that applied a `Wear`, `Remove` or `Drop`, records no armor hits. `damage_saved` is `{npc_type_code: damage_without − damage_taken}`, recomputed from the row's two maxes whenever either changes and present only while the gap is positive: how far the item lowers the best hit seen from that type, not the item's defense stat. Multi-piece loadouts record nothing. `capabilities` is A46. | A17 |
| A19 | **Equip.** Score slots, swap when a carried item is better. Shipped: priority-3 **Equip** state (`Arm`, `Remove` + `Wear`); weapons are the Supplies reference's `weapon` class (A54); worn slot comes from snapshot `worn` seen this run (`WorldModel.worn_slots`), else the Supplies reference's `slot` for listed armor and accessories (A95); like-with-like scoring (`weapon_damage`, `damage_saved` on shared NPC types weighted by the threat table, else both `gem_price`s, starting kit = 0) with a 25% gain bar; refusal memory per (subtype, slot); armed slot left alone while Heal, Solve or Break holds it, and otherwise a non-weapon there (a potion or tool left armed, even from an earlier run) counts as an empty slot that any held weapon fills (free-play run 4: a potion armed in run 3 stayed armed, and 15 `equip` ops finished "nothing to equip"). A held subtype whose slot neither gives, and that the reference does not class as a weapon, tool, consumable or gem, gets one slot-learn `Wear` try from Equip (A55, A95). Since A61 Equip runs only for a plan `equip` op (optional `code`), and finishes it when no upgrade is left. Since A99 weapons rank by one rule shared with the planner (`equip.weapon_rank`: within the highest hunting-ground ceiling known first, then published `damage`), and an op's `code` arms or wears exactly that item. | A5, A18 |
| A20 | **Loot.** `Take`, `WithdrawFromChest`, `Drop` junk when full; gems first for ground code `gem`, then priced gear, potions and food. The Loot state and reflex 4 (walk, `Take`, `WithdrawFromChest` one supply, `Drop` the worst held supply for a better pickup, skip when not worth it; since A102 no reflex drops: what to give up is the planner's, `pack.make_room`), carry space from the snapshot's `inventory` at the sourced 10 slots, `carry_capacity_full` and `not_transferable` learned from rejections, Recover (A11) at the death chest skips a doomed `WithdrawFromChest` when the pack is full (since A102 it never drops to make room), withdraws the best supplies by id rather than the lowest ids, and gems-first scoring for ground code `gem` (GAME_NOTES, `docs/observations/A20_live_play.md`). Hearts-first, stowed `Drop`, and larger-chest capacity are A47. Since A61 the in-reach part is the **Pickup** reflex; Loot walks only for a plan `fetch_item` op. | A5 |
| A21 | **Shop.** The executor for the plan's top `buy` op (A61): it takes a priced supply in sight whose code the op wants, walking next to it first. A `buy` gets one more of its item: it is done once one more is held or stowed than the fewest since it reached the top of the stack (`Plan.held_before`), whatever was held before; one picked up or withdrawn for free on the way counts too, since one more is what the op is for and it costs no gems, so a drink, a drop or a death does not leave it owing two (free-play run 5: with 2 potions held, each `buy` small_potion finished at once, 8 times). The count leaves the armed item out: if the armed supply is not also listed in `held` (GAME_NOTES open measurement), arming the buy's item and arming something else again could finish it with nothing bought; once that is measured, count the armed item by id. Nothing restocks on its own: `potion_reserve` is planner-only. The `Take` in flight is settled when it is applied or gems drop after it is sent, and forgotten on a rejection, on leaving the shop cell, or after `SHOP_PENDING_TICKS` (10), so a later unrelated gem drop counts for nothing. With a full pack, room is the shared pack rule (`pack.make_room`, A102): Shop drops what the op's `drop` names, and otherwise hands the buy back to the planner. A supply whose `Take` is rejected `SHOP_MAX_REFUSALS` (3) times is skipped until the loadout, gems or map change. With nothing the op wants in sight, the shop is the buy's prerequisite (A71), never left to the safe default (free-play run 4: Shop sent nothing and Explore walked about 100 cells from town into a chugbug pack): Shop walks Travel's route (`states.travel.walk_to`, so stuck give-ups and hub lapses apply) only toward a seller known to sell the item (A131, `sellers.sellers_of`), the cheapest walk first, committed (`targets` key `shop`) until it comes into sight: a shop cell seen selling it (`travel:shop`), or the place a clue or the op names (`travel:seller`), searched within `SELLER_SEARCH_RADIUS` (20) once it is in sight. The op is dropped when the item is in sight but costs more gems than are held, when no seller of it is known, or when every known seller was reached without it for sale (`Plan.sellers_searched`; a shop cell reached without it is sold out until its stock returns, A159); when the walk to each seller left is given up, Shop asks the planner and holds (A120), and the op is dropped after `PLAN_STALL_SECONDS` unanswered. Stepping onto a priced supply buys it, so only Shop's `Take` buys: no walk steps onto one (`WorldModel.for_sale`, impassable in the cost grid but as a search's goal, and never queued as a Step), and Shop walks up beside the item it names (free-play run 1: `travel:shop` stepped onto the shop's sword, 21 → 6 gems, for a planned potion). | A5, A18 |
