| A22 | **Gather.** Gems from grass and gem piles (never bushes, which drop berries, A81) on any known ground off `avoid_blocks` hazards with no known hostile near, the open field included: within `HOSTILE_DANGER_RADIUS` (6) of the hostile that hit us, a step (`GATHER_SHADOW_MARGIN`) past our weapon reach or `hostile_range`, whichever is further, of any other (A63 run 3: one following at 4–6 blocks without attacking stopped all cutting for 45 s; A63 run 4: a bar inside `hostile_range` sent Gather to cells Retreat walked straight back from, 4 times in 34 s). Hostiles out of view count too (free-play run 5: Gather and Detour walked the character to gem piles beside a guard's post until it died, 12 hits for 16 damage): the world model keeps a `Sighting` of every NPC and character seen (`WorldModel.sightings`): the cell it was last seen on, its post (the cell an NPC stood on for `POST_STILL_TICKS`, 50, in view), and its `reach`, the furthest from that post it has hit us from. Only a post holds ground: a roamer's first-seen cell holds none, and a hit from one with no post teaches no reach. One out of view holds its bar round the cell it was last seen on for `SIGHTING_TICKS` (600, on any map); one with a post holds `hostile_range`, or its reach when further (at most `POST_REACH_CAP`), plus a step round that post, in view or not, until the post fades (A95: only while it is in sight and empty; a post nobody looks at keeps its strength), kept on a map left behind for a return; a sighting with no post whose cell is in sight with the hostile gone, a faded post, or an `NPCDied` for either is forgotten. The held ground is worked out once per decision (`hostile_ground.danger`); Retreat, Park, Heal and Flee read the same picture per hostile to pick a refuge (`hostile_ground.ground_by_hostile`, A9). Whether a sighting is a threat is asked at use (`is_hostile`), so a type found hostile later counts (`hostile_ground.known_reach`). Hostile NPC types and the sightings of their NPCs are saved to the world knowledge base at exit and loaded at start (`knowledge_base.hostiles`: `npc_types` `{"<code>": {"hostile": true}}`, with `max_hit`, `hits` and `misses` since A85, `hostile_sightings`), so a later run keeps off the same ground from its first decision (free-play run 6); a loaded sighting is forgotten by the same rules, a passer-by after `SIGHTING_TICKS` of world time and a post once faded (A95: by looks, so it keeps the strength it was saved with until seen empty), and is then dropped from the file. No cell in that ground is gathered, and every walk Gather plans to a pile, grass, off safe ground, toward a named region none of which is known yet, or off a hostile that shadows it (`_replan_gather`, `_plan_out`, `_walk_to_region`, `_move_off`; free-play run 7: the region walk and the move-off walk priced none of it) prices the reach of hostiles in view and remembered as costly and takes no target whose path still crosses it; a kept walk whose way ahead comes to cross it is planned again (`route_clear`; reach that holds where we stand is not counted), trying the next nearest, up to `ROUTE_TRIES` (3) per kind. A kept target found unsafe is let go at once (A71), so a hit on the way to a pile, which stretches the hitter's reach to where we stood, re-prices that pile. A `gather_gems` op with `fight: true` is the planner choosing the risk: it lifts the remembered and route tests (the in-view bar stays). A hostile within 6 blocks for `SHADOW_SECONDS` (15) without hitting us is fought (`engage`) when `on_hostile = fight`, it may be attacked and an engagement with it would start as a fight (`engagement.would_fight`, A94); otherwise Gather walks in one go to ground at least `MOVE_OFF_DISTANCE` (12) from it, grass there first, instead of inching to the nearest cell it does not cover; survival there is the reflexes' job (Retreat, Flee, Fight, Heal). Safe zones stay eligible, since breaking a block works there, but field cells (not known safe by a zone read or a terrain read's `safe` flag) are cut first; a safe cell is cut only when no field cell is left to cut. Gather learns ground that does not cut instead of ruling it out: a `Use` on town grass was `applied_no_effect` every time (A63 run 2: 168 cuts of one town cell; GAME_NOTES.md), and after `NO_EFFECT_ZONE_CUTS` (3) such cuts in one region, or in one known safe zone (the safe cells joined to the cut cell, `zone_discovery.safe_zone_of`), that whole region or zone is uncuttable (A63). A cut goes out with a tool that cuts: the one the weapon-to-arm rule picks for cutting (`equip.weapon_to_arm`, A99); when that is not the armed item, Gather arms it in the same paced queue as the `Use`; with none held it cuts with what is in hand. A weapon it swapped out (`Memory.gather_rearm`) is armed again, once, when no `gather_gems` op is on top and the tool it armed is still armed; food or a potion is left to Heal. A no-effect cut made with an item known not to cut (food, a potion, or one whose known capabilities leave `cut` out: `break_memory.cannot_cut`) marks nothing, neither the cell nor its region (free-play run 3: cuts with a potion armed marked ground uncuttable). An unsourced code still counts, since no read says it cannot cut. Since A61 Gather runs only for the top `gather_gems` op and reads its count from the op. With nothing in reach it walks to the cheaper of the cheapest gem pile (counting 8 steps cheaper, a sure gem) and the cheapest grass, the grass only when no dearer than the nearest unexplored ground by walk and yield while Gather works anywhere (A105), each priced by its walk with the danger on it, on the path and at the target (known reach costly; ground near a known hostile's, and faded posts' ground, adding steps to each cell; A94) (the 16 nearest of each kind tried), skipping barren regions and exhausted cells (cut under 600 ticks ago, not grown back, or a `Use` there had no effect; see A63); with none known while standing on safe ground it heads out to the nearest known field cell, else the nearest frontier; with every known cell to cut held by a hostile it walks on to the nearest frontier with no known hostile within `GATHER_HOSTILE_RADIUS`, by a route clear of reach, kept until a cell to cut or a gem pile it may take comes into sight and a clear route reaches it, or a hostile comes to hold its end; what is in sight but unreachable is tried once (`Memory.gather_clear_seen`) (`walking to ground clear of hostiles`, A82); only with nowhere to head does it ask the planner and hold (A120; its search already went for unexplored ground); a walk to unexplored ground it priced cheaper than the grass it knows is Gather's own (A105). It works a target region first, the one the op names with `x, y` or a better region it leaves a poor one for (A63); while it works only in a target region, it walks to gem piles only there, and takes one in reach wherever it lies (free-play run 5: piles outside the named region drew it to a guard's post). Its last decision (`cutting`, `taking a gem`, `walking to grass`, `walking to a gem pile`, `walking to unexplored ground` (A105), `walking to a target region`, `moving off from a hostile that shadows`, `walking to ground clear of hostiles`, `fighting a hostile that shadows`, `blocked by hostile`, `cuts have no effect here`, `heading out of safe ground`, `no cuttable cell in view`, `region barren`) is the planner's State `gather_status` while a `gather_gems` is on top (A63 runs 1–3); any but `cutting` gets `, no cut for N s` once no cut has taken effect for `STALL_SECONDS` (30) of this spell of Gather (A63 run 3: a walk read as `cutting` for 45 s with no cut). `heading out of safe ground`, moving off, a pile `Take` and a walk to a pile come first; `cuts have no effect here` shows only while Gather stands on ground marked uncuttable, or the latest cut had no effect in its region and none has worked since, within 600 ticks. The backoff to Explore is gone. **Since A88:** the shadowing hostile, field cells before safe ones, the walk out of safe ground, the walk to ground clear of hostiles and the route tries per kind are gone; Gather runs one target search under one ground filter and one price (A88). **Superseded in part by A162:** hostiles keep no post (Manual §11 Hostiles). The post, its reach, its fade, faded-post pricing and `note_came_for_us` are gone. A hostile out of view holds its bar round the cell where it was last seen: a moving one for `SIGHTING_TICKS`, a blocked one (`Sighting.still`) for `BLOCKED_SIGHTING_TICKS`. | A5 |
| A63 | **Gem yield by region, learned from play.** Gem drops from cutting grass vary by area, and some areas drop none (bushes drop berries, not gems, A81); the agent learns where from its own cuts, with no location written in. **Measure** (`gem_yield.py`, `GemYieldTracker` on the runner): every applied `Use` on a block that shows grass or a breakable (bush, tree, rock, mountain, wall) is one record in the world knowledge base, `kb.extra["gem_yield"]["<map_id>"]["cuts"]` (cell, block, tick, `gem`; the latest 500 per map). The drop is tied to the cut by a free `gem` supply not in view at the cut that appears on or next to the cell within `GEM_WINDOW_TICKS` (5), or by the gem counter rising in that window with no gem `Take` (a `gem`, a `gem_cache_*`, or a code not read) applied since; one gem credits one cut, and a gem left on the ground is never credited twice. Cuts pending at a death or a map change are dropped, not filed, so a window cut short never counts as a miss. The store carries `version` (`gem_yield.VERSION`, 2 since A81); one of another version reads as empty and the next cut replaces it, since totals from before A81 counted bush cuts at the old rates and would mark ground barren that Gather never revisits (review on #172). **Summarise:** grass cuts count in 16×16-block regions per map (`regions`: `cuts`, `gems`, `last_tick`). A region with `BARREN_MIN_CUTS` (15) cuts or more and no gem is barren: ground at the manual's lowest grass rate (`PRIOR_YIELD`, 20%) shows no gem in 15 cuts under `BARREN_FALSE_RATE` (4%) of the time (`barren_min_cuts`). Poor and good are judged against the map's own rate (A81): `expected_yield` is the gems a cut dropped over all this map's cuts, with `PRIOR_YIELD` counted as `PRIOR_CUTS` (20) cuts, so it starts at the manual and follows what the character measures; no region is left out for how it came out, which would skew the rate high (review on #172). **Planner:** State carries `gem_yield` (the region size, `expected_yield`, our region once our cuts sampled it, the best three sampled regions at any distance with their yield and `distance` in blocks (A63 run 3: past 3 regions the one productive region dropped out of view), and the barren ones within 3 regions, each named by its corner block), and the progression section tells the planner to hunt where the yield is good, leave a region that stays barren after a fair sample, and explore unsampled regions. **Gather** (A22) skips grass in a barren region unless the `gather_gems` op names it with optional `x, y`. That `x, y` names a **target region**: Gather walks there and cuts only there while it knows a cell to cut there, walks toward the region's middle while no cell of it is known, and works as usual once it knows none there to cut (free-play run 2: 54 cuts for 1 gem in one region while the next one gave 1 gem in 4, and `x, y` could not move the character). Without one, Gather picks its own: a region is **poor** after a fair sample (`FAIR_SAMPLE_CUTS`, 20, with under half of `expected_yield` in gems a cut; a barren region is skipped from 15 cuts, before it is a fair sample), and **better** at `expected_yield` or more over `GOOD_MIN_CUTS` (4) or more; while a better region lies within `BETTER_REGION_BLOCKS` (64), poor regions are skipped like barren ones, and from a poor region the best better one (by yield, then distance) is the target (`gem_yield.poor_regions`, `better_region`). While Gather works only in a target region its `gather_status` ends in ` (region x,y)`, the corner block. A cell counts as exhausted for `REGROW_TICKS` (600) from the moment of the cut, including the gem window while the cut is still pending in `GemYieldTracker`, not only once it is filed, so a stale read still showing grass cannot get the same cell cut and filed twice. A planner `gather_gems` under a pinned one with no higher count repeats it and is dropped, so a reply that differs only in such an op leaves the stack unchanged (A63 run 1). A `Use` on grass that comes back `applied_no_effect` (not Break's capability probe, A28, whose miss says only that one item does not work on that block) cut nothing and is never filed (a false miss would push its region toward barren); `GemYieldTracker` holds that cell out of Gather for `REGROW_TICKS` instead, and `NO_EFFECT_ZONE_CUTS` (3) of them in one region or one known safe zone, repeats on one cell included, mark the whole region or zone uncuttable (`uncuttable`), which is not barren: barren means cuts work and drop no gem. The store keeps at most 500 no-effect cuts and forgets each after `NO_EFFECT_TTL` (6,000 ticks), so a mark lapses and the ground is tried again. State `gather_run` carries this run's `cuts` (that took effect), `no_effect_cuts` and `gems_gained` (rises of the counter), so the planner can tell work from a stall (A63 run 2). Tests: `test_gem_yield.py` (fake maps and cells only). **Since A88:** Gather no longer leaves a poor region for a better one by itself (`better_region` is gone): a region's yield is in the price of its grass, and naming a region is the planner's. | A22, A62 |
| A23 | **Fight.** Group-aware win estimate, `never_attack`, retreat queued behind attacks, conservative until measured, never from a safe zone. Gates Fight on A9's estimate (since A94 through the engagement's one fight-or-flee decision, `engagement.sync_engagement`; `survival.would_lose` is retired): a fight it says we lose goes to **Flee**, and the "threat outclasses us" **Retreat** trigger lands with it, firing only while the group is coming for us (A9). Fight swings only at a hostile (`survival.is_hostile`), never at an NPC of a type not yet shown hostile. Live survive-a-fight runs: [`docs/observations/A23_survive_fight_runs.md`](docs/observations/A23_survive_fight_runs.md). | A5, A6, A8, A9 |
| A24 | **Healing from food and potions.** `Arm` + `Use` self; heal amounts learned per type (retired by A155: heals are the Supplies reference's); re-`Arm` the weapon after a drink (A10 leaves it unarmed). Shipped: a drink is `Arm` + `Use` in one paced queue (`arm_then_use`), since an unpaced pair sends only the `Arm` (free-play run 3: the potion stayed armed from 191 s to the end, and Equip, waiting on the re-arm, finished 145 `equip` ops with nothing to equip). The `Use` targets `{"kind": "self"}` (API Use); a `character` target naming our own id is how a weapon strikes a character, and free-play run 4's drink on it drank nothing. Heal remembers the weapon to put back (`Memory.self_use_rearm`, `equip.weapon_to_rearm`): the weapon armed now, else the last weapon seen armed this run (`Memory.last_weapon`, noted every decision) while still held, else a held weapon; never the potion or tool in the slot (free-play run 4 re-armed a potion left armed by run 3). It sends one `Arm` for it on its next decision, before anything else, the drink done or not, including a drink of a potion already armed; a second potion is then armed again. Heal's guard also holds while that re-arm is due, even at full health or with a hostile in range, and Equip leaves the armed slot alone until then. The re-arm is sent once: a rejected `Arm` or a weapon no longer in hand clears it. A drink's refusal is filed (A80) only when the server rejects its `Arm` or `Use`, once per drink, against the supply Heal decided on (`Memory.self_use`, `Runner._note_supply_refused`), which is forgotten once a queue without the drink replaces it; a drink decided in the held-queue probe and not sent files nothing (free-play run 5: 6 probes wrote both potions off). The `Arm` never goes out without its `Use`: when the cooldown leaves no room for both in one queue, Heal (and Gather's cut) sends nothing that decision (`arm_then_use`). Ground food in sight is always taken, whatever its learned heal: it is free, and skipping it would only spend a potion or a walk to town. While hurt, health before a food `Take` or self-`Use` is compared with the next observation and filed as `heal_amount` (the largest seen) under the item's code; for a self-`Use` the code comes from the `Arm` queued before it (Waits aside), since `armed_code` only updates with the observation. A `Take` also files `heal_on_pickup`. A response that took damage files nothing. Food picked up by walking onto it is not measured. | A10, A20 |
| A25 | **M8 acceptance.** M8 done-when, as a gate: `scripts/smoke_m8_olympuff.py` and `agentrealm_agent/m8_acceptance.py`. It shares `TimedRunHooks` and `FULL_RUN_FRACTION` (`acceptance_run.py`, also used by A16 and A33) and the smoke helpers in `acceptance_smoke.py` (`wake`, `navigation_start`, `run_acceptance_smoke`; A16, A33). The smoke script wakes a sleeping character with one `Wait` (and waits out a respawn), reads the world and position, and plays on the overworld for one hour by default on `characters/olympuff_m8.toml` with `characters/olympuff_m8.directives.toml` (`on_hostile = fight`, pickup on, gather and buy goals). Hooks run from the runner through `AcceptanceHooks`; `before_tick` sees the world the decision saw, and `on_events` sees tick events after the response. The smoke script takes `--character-id` or `--character-name` (A59). A run fails on: any death under `--stop-on-death` (the first one ends the run; by default deaths are counted and reported, not failed, and play goes on after the respawn, A63 run 3); a fight started below the health floor (the first attack `Use` after entering **Fight** sent while the win estimate is at or below the lowest bar the fight-or-flee decision uses, `fight_margin` or break-even whichever is lower (A94), PLAYABLE_AGENT_PLAN Combat; later swings in the same fight are not judged); any API error; and, on a run of at least 95% of the target duration, the M8 milestones. **Milestone rule:** gems must rise at least once; worn armor (a non-weapon, non-consumable worn slot other than accessory), a shop weapon armed (not the starting pocket knife), held plus stowed potions reaching `potion_reserve` at least once; it must take ground food while hurt (our own `SupplyTaken`, which only the taker receives, for a supply the Supplies reference marks food, from any state: **Heal** or a **Detour**, A93) and **Heal** must drink a carried potion: a self-`Use` sent with a potion in hand (an `Arm` just before the `Use` names the potion, since `armed_code` still shows the weapon) whose result applied and after which the potion count dropped, or our own `SupplyUsed` for a potion (A76); at least one lone weak kill: an `NPCDied` with an `NPCDamaged` from our character on that NPC on the same tick (the killing blow, from any state, on any poll), for an NPC that at our first attack on it was its whole combat group and whose type the win estimate prices at most 2 a hit, measured or not (A93). The summary counts every kill as well. Shorter runs still fail on deaths (under `--stop-on-death`), fight-floor violations and API errors. The live pass that clears this gate waits on A58. Since A61 nothing equips or restocks potions on its own: the gear and potion milestones need the AI planner (A35) to emit `equip` and potion `buy` ops, which its progression stage 2 asks for (A62). | A58, A19, A21, A22, A23, A24 |
| A45 | **Use on an NPC by id.** Fight's swings target `{"kind": "npc", "npc_id": N}` (`states/intents.use_npc`), so the server swings at the block the NPC stands on the tick the `Use` runs (server release 1.11, saims B126; [API rules § Use](https://agentrealm.gg/docs/api)) and a queued swing follows an NPC that moved after the plan. An NPC out of sight, dead or unknown that tick is `target_out_of_range`, which drops the rest of the queue and re-plans like any rejection. The item table matches `NPCDamaged` against the block the NPC was last seen on and files the hit only when the one NPC seen there is the target, so a hit after it moved, or on another NPC on that block, is left unattributed. `never_attack` checks the target NPC's type by id and fails closed: with it set, an npc id not in the entity list drops the swing. No `direction` target is used: the agent never aims a `Use` at a neighbour meant relative to where it will stand. | A23 |
| A46 | **Item table capabilities.** `capabilities` on an `items` row: a sorted list of the capabilities that subtype has opened a block with, filed when a break's `BlockChanged` arrives with it armed (A28) and never removed, since a failed break is the block's property, not the item's. Break's tool choice reads it alongside the Manual's Supplies reference, whose capabilities A54 files on the same rows. | A18 |
| A47 | **Loot carry edge cases.** What the agent can do before live play answers them. A life's ground code (retired by A156: a life is any supply of class `life` in the Supplies reference): when `lives` rises in a response whose only applied `Take` of our own queue was one supply (not `gem`), that supply's `supply_subtype_code` is filed as `life_on_pickup: true` on its `items` row in the knowledge base and logged; from then on Loot and Shop score it above gems and take it with a full pack, like `LIFE_SUPPLY_CODES`. Two or more applied `Take`s in that response, or a result from another queue, file nothing. Larger chest: a chest's `Use` on yourself that went through raises carry capacity to its Supplies reference `chest_capacity` when larger (since A97; `middle_chest` 30, `red_chest` 50, `blue_chest` 10 changes nothing); before A108 this was assumed on the `Take`. Not a measurement (GAME_NOTES **Assumed until measured**); `carry_capacity_full` still lowers it and a respawn resets it to 10. Stowed `Drop` is not attempted: Loot sheds held supplies only. Confirming these in live play is A57. Notes: [`docs/observations/A20_live_play.md`](docs/observations/A20_live_play.md), [`docs/observations/A47_live_play.md`](docs/observations/A47_live_play.md). | A20 |
| A57 | **Loot carry facts from live play.** Observe, then code (the life code is the Supplies reference's since A156, and `Drop` takes armed and worn supplies since A154): a life's ground `supply_subtype_code` (cut Olympuff grass and bushes, or sandbox field grass with a bomb, Manual §16, and see what A47 files as `life_on_pickup`) goes into `LIFE_SUPPLY_CODES`; stowed `Drop` (`Drop` a supply id from `inventory.chest` and read the result) lets Loot and Shop shed stowed junk the same way as held if accepted, from a deliberate probe whose result is kept in the knowledge base; larger-chest capacity (carry a shop `middle_chest` and fill until `carry_capacity_full`) replaces A47's Manual §16 default with the measured limit. GAME_NOTES open questions. Session notes: [`docs/observations/A57_live_play.md`](docs/observations/A57_live_play.md). | A47 |
| A54 | **Item table capabilities from the manual.** At the start of a run the agent loads the Manual's Supplies reference (saims B132, `supplies.load_for_run`) and merges each subtype's break capabilities (cut, chop, smash, burn and blast among its `use_effects`) onto that subtype's `items` row through `absorb_supply_entry`, using the same sorted, grow-only rules as break-learned tags (A46). Learning from breaks (A28, A46) stays; the manual only fills a row sooner. The same table replaces the seed item tables (Server gaps, Supplies reference). | A46 |
| A81 | **Game update of 2026-10-10: attack power 2, grass and kills pay gems, bushes do not.** The live game (saims B136, B137, B139; Manual §16) gave every character permanent attack power 2: a swing at a hostile hits on 8 or better (65%) and deals 1 up to 2 plus weapon damage, the pocket knife 1–4. It counts toward hunting-ground strength, and the hunting ground's ceiling rose from 5 to 7. Grass drops a gem 20% of the time in the ring-1 fields and 25% farther out; bushes drop berries, not gems; field work makes about 6 gems a minute. A kill drops a gem at the zone's grass chance plus a per-type bonus (a Snotling in the ring-1 fields 30%, a Chugbug in the hunting ground 40%). The owner sheet's strength no longer counts a held potion or worn non-armor. The agent: **Gather** (A22) cuts only grass (`gem_yield.GATHER_BLOCKS`), never walks to or cuts a bush, and a bush cut is not counted in region yield; bushes stay food for Heal and Loot. **Gem yield** (A63) marks a region barren after 15 cuts with no gem (4% false at the manual's 20%, was 30 at 10%), and judges poor and good against `expected_yield`, the map's rate over all its cuts with the manual's 20% as the prior, instead of fixed 10% rates. Weapon damage is the Supplies reference's `damage`, read with the weapon class (`supplies.weapon_damage`, A54), one list. The `gem_yield` store moves to version 2: a store saved before A81 reads as empty and is replaced on the next cut. **Win estimate** (A23): our damage a swing is `survival.swing_damage`, the published roll at the world's base attack power (`BASE_ATTACK_POWER`, 2: the API serves no attack power, **Server gaps**) and the armed weapon's published damage (`supplies.weapon_damage`), instead of an assumed 1 a swing; a hostile's swing is priced on the same roll (`hostile_swing_damage`: its damage number is its attack power, so one measured at 2 hits 65% of the time; a landed hit is priced at the largest hit seen, a floor on that number, never the mean below it; a type never measured keeps the threat table's conservative guess on every swing, undiscounted; A85 prices it at attack power 2 instead), instead of every swing landing for its full damage (run 8 on the new version: hostiles hit 6 of 8, and the agent never swung because every fight read as lost). The extra fights are intended: at the default `fight_margin` the knife now takes one weak measured hostile at full health and still refuses a pair or a type never measured (A85: a type never measured is now priced at attack power 2, not refused), so kill drops are within reach while the survival reflexes keep their rules. The regen probe's `fight_margin` moves to 2.4 so it still fights an unmeasured hostile at full health and retreats at health 6 from one measured at 2 (by then it has hit, so its type is measured; `test_regen_probe` pins both). The hunting-ground bracket (A27) learns from `over_strength_ceiling` and holds no strength sum, so it needs no change. **Planner:** stage 2 says kills now pay gems and when a weak hostile is worth fighting (a `gather_gems` with `fight` on held ground, or the hunting ground), which gear the hunting ground's ceiling admits, and the new grass rates; stage 3 says a boss is hit 65% of the time. The bundled reference is refreshed, and GAME_NOTES carries every rate. Tests: `test_gem_yield.py` (`DerivedThresholdTest`), `test_survival.py` (`SwingTest`), and the Gather tests that walked to or cut bushes. | A22, A23, A63 |
| A85 | **Free-play run 9: remembered posts fade, and the threat table is kept.** Run 9 was the first to start from a filled knowledge base, and Gather made 1 cut all run: posts saved by earlier runs (A78) never expired, so they held the grass round town for good, and a reach learned from a long chase stretched one far from its post. Every fight was refused too: only `hostile` was saved per type, so each run started with every type unmeasured, and the win estimate refused unmeasured types. **Posts fade** (`Sighting.strength`, `Sighting.fade_post`): 1 while the guard is in view; as built here, halved every `POST_HALF_LIFE_TICKS` of world time out of view (3000 ticks, 5 min at 10 ticks/s) times the spells it was seen on its post (`Sighting.spells`, up to `POST_MAX_SPELLS`, 4: 20 min; a spell counts when the guard is back on its post after `SIGHTING_TICKS` out of view, so one flickering at the edge of sight adds none), and while the post is in sight with nobody on it also every `EMPTY_POST_HALF_LIFE_TICKS` looked at (10 ticks, 1 s; at most one halving a look). A95 removed the world-time half-life: a post now fades only while looked at empty, times its spells, so a post seen recently or again and again stays strong and an empty one goes within a few seconds of looking. Seeing the guard makes it 1 again. At `POST_HOLD_STRENGTH` (0.5) or more it holds ground as before (`hostile_ground.held_by_hostile`); below that it only prices it (`faded_reach`, `Danger.priced`): a cell in it counts up to `FADED_POST_STEPS` (6) steps further for Gather's grass pick (`Danger.price`, in `_nearest` and the path search's `CostGridParams.priced`), scaled by strength, and never bars it, nor counts as a hostile near a cell for Gather's walk to clear ground or Detour's risk gate (A82, `hostiles_within`); below `POST_FORGET_STRENGTH` (0.1) it is forgotten. A post's reach counts at most `POST_REACH_CAP` (6, the planner's danger radius) from it. A sighting that keeps no post is unchanged (`SIGHTING_TICKS`, or its cell in sight with it gone). Strength, the tick it is as of and the spells are saved with the post (`knowledge_base.hostiles`), so the fade carries across runs (on world time until A95; now the strength left is kept as is). **The threat table is kept** in `npc_types` per type: `max_hit` (the largest `Damaged`), and `hits` and `misses` of its swings at us (a `Damaged` is a hit, an `Attacked` with no `Damaged` from the same attacker on its tick a miss; `threat.count_swings`). Loaded at start and saved at exit, adding only what this run counted to the shared file's counts (`ThreatTable.saved_hits`). **Win estimate** (A23): a type's swing is priced on its hit rate (`ThreatTable.hit_rate`: its hits and misses, with the published roll counting as `PRIOR_SWINGS`, 10 swings) at its largest hit; a type never measured swings at the world's base attack power (`UNMEASURED_DEFAULT`, 2; the Manual publishes no per-type damage) on the same roll, and the win estimate (A94: the engagement's decision) no longer refuses an unmeasured type below effective risk 0.5, so the knife takes a lone unmeasured hostile at full health and still refuses a pair or a type measured to hit hard. The retreat threshold and Detour's risk gate price an unmeasured type the same way (`ThreatTable.damage_per_hit`, which no longer takes the hardest type seen), so a type has one price everywhere. A guard's last-seen cell off its post holds by the passer-by rule, whatever its post's strength (A95). Tests: `test_freeplay_run9_hostile_memory.py`. **Superseded in part by A162:** hostiles keep no post (Manual §11 Hostiles). The post, its reach, its fade, faded-post pricing and `note_came_for_us` are gone. A hostile out of view holds its bar round the cell where it was last seen: a moving one for `SIGHTING_TICKS`, a blocked one (`Sighting.still`) for `BLOCKED_SIGHTING_TICKS`. | A22, A23, A78, A81 |
| A93 | **The M8 gate counts kills and food from the events the API documents.** Free-play run 10's gate summary said 0 weak hostile kills and no ground food, while the trace had 2 kills (each dropping a gem) and an apple taken at 206 s. Kills: the gate counted an `NPCDied` only for the NPC it had marked as **Fight**'s target, cleared that mark whenever the decision was not **Fight**, and called a hostile weak only once its damage was measured, while the win estimate the agent fights on prices a type never measured at the base attack power, 2 (A85); a hostile whose swings all missed was never weak to the gate. Food: the gate counted only a `Take` that **Heal** sent, but the apple was taken at the end of Heal's food walk, and walking onto food eaten on pickup takes it with no `Take`; **Detour** also picks food up when hurt. The entity and `is_food` lookup was the agent's own and was not at fault. Now a kill is an `NPCDied` with an `NPCDamaged` from our character (`actor_kind` `character`, our `actor_id`) on that NPC on the same tick, the killing blow, whichever state swung it; it is lone weak when, at our first attack on that NPC from any state since it came into sight, it was its whole combat group and the win estimate's price for its type is at most 2. Food counts on our own `SupplyTaken` (only the taker receives it) for a supply in sight that the Supplies reference marks food, taken while hurt. The summary reports every kill beside the lone weak count. Tests are built from the run's event shapes (`tests/test_m8_acceptance.py`). | A25, A85 |
| A97 | **Game update of 2026-10-11: item capabilities.** The live game (saims B138, #753–#767; Manual §11) describes every item by its capabilities. What changed for the agent: worn armor now also cuts `occupy_damage` (fire and lava deal it less defense and the armor worn; before, only defense), and the docs make plain that armor in hand protects nothing (worn armor already cut hostile and boss hits, adding to the hit target and coming off the damage); weapon damage adds to attack power for the damage roll only, not the roll to hit; water is walkable only with an armed or worn supply (timed effects no longer count); only the largest light radius carried counts, the firefly jar is an always-on light, and goggles, boots of speed, the water-walking sandals and the truth lens grant their effect armed too; the Supplies reference gains a `blue_chest` row (consumable, chest capacity 10, the chest a character starts with), and its `damage` is a weapon's, its `heal` a potion's or food's. The agent: **Hazard steps** cost the `occupy_damage` that gets through the armor worn, never below 0 (`navigation/planner.py` `_Grid.cost`). **Win estimate and danger pricing** (A23, A94), which never counted armor (a rule older than this update the agent had not modelled), now count the defense of the armor worn (`survival.worn_defense`, from the Supplies reference's `defense` over the worn slots only): a hostile's swing hits on the published roll against it and a landed hit is priced at the hostile's number less it, at least 1, since the largest hit seen is only a floor on that number (`survival.hostile_swing_damage`, `hit_damage`); the engagement's decision, Retreat's health floor and Detour's volley all read it, and the ground priced round a hostile follows the decision. `Damaged.amount` is already net of armor, so the threat table files a hit gross, adding back the armor worn when it landed (`threat.absorb_damaged`, `WorldModel.learn_threat`), and armor comes off once, at pricing; a hit armor absorbed whole counts as a hit and files no number. A `max_hit` saved before this is at most the armor low and grows on the next landed hit (`knowledge_base.hostiles`). **Loot** (A47) takes a chest's capacity from the reference's `chest_capacity` (`supplies.chest_capacity`), raising only when larger: the blue chest changes nothing and the red chest is 50, not the 100 `MANUAL_CHEST_CAPACITY` had. The bundled `reference/supplies.json`, the planner reference and GAME_NOTES are refreshed; the planner's prompt says armor counts only worn. Already right, unchanged: Equip never tries `Wear` on a chest or teleport scroll (A95's `equip.never_worn`); our swing (attack power 2, 65%, weapon damage on the damage roll only, A81); Equip arms only weapons and wears armor, so no armor is ever armed for protection; weapon `damage` read only for the weapon class and food and potions told apart by `eaten_on_pickup` and the `heal` use effect (A54). The agent has no water, light or timed-effect rule to change: it walks only the walkable block types (`world.WALKABLE`), so water is always blocked to it, and it buys no raft, sandals or light on its own. Left as is: Loot still assumes the larger chest's capacity on its `Take`, where the Manual raises it on `Use` (A57). Tests: `test_armor_and_capabilities.py`. | A23, A47, A54, A81, A94 |
| A55 | **Equip wear slot by trying.** A held subtype never seen in snapshot `worn` that Equip's candidate filters keep (`_candidates` drops compose fragments and consumables, `wear_slot` skips weapons) gets its slot by trying, from the **Equip** state (no separate probe state). `Wear(supplyId)` takes no slot, so the server picks it. Wear the never-worn subtype, and if it applies, read which slot the next snapshot's `worn` shows it in and record that slot (`WorldModel.worn_slots`), after which A19 scores it like any other worn item. A `not_wearable` rejection is a fact about the subtype: remember it per subtype for the run and never try that subtype again. Any other rejection (`worn_slot_occupied`, which does not say which slot, or another code) is transient: remember it per subtype and clear it when the loadout or inventory changes, as A19's `sync_refusals` does. Equip never `Remove`s a slot to make room for a try. One try per response, and never while a higher-priority state holds. A try needs no `would_strand` guard: it never `Remove`s, and a `Wear` into an occupied slot is rejected, so it cannot take anything off. Since A95 the try is only for a subtype whose slot the Manual's Supplies reference (A54) does not give: listed armor and accessories take the reference's `slot`, and a subtype it lists in any other class (tools, consumables, gems) is never worn. | A19 |

**M9: Navigation and knowledge.**

| ID | Item | Depends on |
|---|---|---|
| A26 | **Door graph and cross-map routing.** Warps recorded in the knowledge base; route over the graph, then A* on each map. | A12, A17 |
| A27 | **Travel.** To entrance marks, town, hunting grounds and shops; strength bracketed by `over_strength_ceiling`. Travel is the executor for the plan's top `travel` op; directives `goals` entries `travel:<to>[:[map_id:]x:y]` become such ops on the one plan stack. It walks with the cost grid and door graph (A26). An op that does not resolve yet (`shop` before any priced supply is seen, `town` with no town or respawn known) asks the planner (`no_way_on`) and holds: unanswered, the op stalls and is moved below after `PLAN_STALL_SECONDS` (A104, A120). A `hunting_ground` with no eligible ceiling known searches for one instead (A23 survive-a-fight run 1: it stalled and dropped with none known): Travel explores the whole map's frontier (`HUNT_SEARCH_AREA`), and while it does, spare windows read zones on a sparse grid, one cell per 4 × 4 square, in widening rings around the character out to 64 cells, nearest first, skipping cells already read or unrevealed (`zone_discovery.hunt_probe`, ahead of the respawn ring and path cells: with an empty knowledge base the ring took all 211 reads of A23 survive-a-fight run 2), since `get_zone` gives `strength_ceiling` only on a hunting cell. The probes stop once a read on the map finds a hunting cell or the search has spent 200 reads (`HUNT_PROBE_BUDGET`), and run while Travel worked on the search in the last 30 s (`HUNT_PROBE_FRESH_SECONDS`). A read that finds one resolves the op and Travel walks there. The search is bounded: the op is dropped once no frontier is left or after `HUNT_SEARCH_SECONDS` (300 s); a search Travel has not worked on for 60 s (Retreat held the round, another op was on top) starts over. A plan `travel` op names `x`, `y` only for a `point`, or for a given `shop` or `entrance`: `town` and `hunting_ground` take none, and a symbolic `to` without them is read as `0, 0`, the nearest known one (`plan._validate_travel`). Arriving finishes the op; with no step plannable Travel asks the planner and holds (A120), or at stuck step 2 yields to Break; while an occupant holds the route's first step, Travel holds the round (not progress, so the op still stalls). A shop is a cell where a supply with a `gem_price` was seen: a priced supply "spends those gems when picked up" ([manual §11](https://agentrealm.gg/docs/manual#11-game-rules)), so a `shop` travel, or one to any cell with an item for sale, arrives next to that cell, never on it (A21). The API names no shops, and a bought-out cell stays listed. Entrance marks come from one `get_minimap` read at startup, so marks on maps revealed later are learned on the next run. The `doors` policy goal is not a route to these marks: it walks known door tiles in view (`doors_goal_path`), while `travel:entrance` ops walk to minimap entrance marks. The bracket keeps only the lower bound (`over_strength_ceiling` ⇒ strength above that ceiling): a successful entry would not change which grounds are candidates until win estimates (M8) choose between ceilings. A loadout change resets it and reopens the cells it closed. Since A61 Travel also walks the built-in planner's `goto` and `doors`. | A5, A26 |
| A28 | **Break and break memory.** Per (block, capability); break costs go live; escalation steps 2 and 4; `Escape` through blocks. The tool for a capability is a supply held or armed, weapons first, the armed one winning a tie, so a break arms once and keeps it (an armed supply is not in `held`: free-play run 1 flipped the arm between two cutting weapons 289 times and never stepped). Break steps toward its block first and arms only once in reach: a decision sends only its first intent, so an `Arm` ahead of the step left the step unsent (A68). Arming is not progress at step 2: `ARM_DECISION_LIMIT` (3) Break decisions that arm with no break `Use` resolving between them (it did nothing or was refused) fail the level (`arm_only`) and escalation carries on. | A5, A15, A17 |
| A29 | **M9 acceptance.** M9 done-when, as a gate: `scripts/smoke_m9_olympuff.py` and `agentrealm_agent/m9_acceptance.py`. The smoke script wakes a sleeping character (same rules as A16), checks the overworld start, and plays on `characters/olympuff_m9.toml` (`doors` then `explore`, pickup on; Investigate, A30, walks to the marks) until every required entrance is recorded and the character stands on town, or the time budget (default two hours) elapses. **Return to town:** when the catalog is first complete, `M9AcceptanceMetrics.on_entrances_done` fires and the script writes `travel:town` into the profile's directives file (`characters/olympuff_m9.directives.toml`, keeping its params, `never_attack` and non-travel goals and dropping other `travel:*` ops); the A8 reload hands it to Travel (A27), which walks to the knowledge-base town cell. The file is restored (or removed, if it was absent) when the run ends. Hooks run through `AcceptanceHooks`; survival gates are shared with A16 in `acceptance_survival.py` (built on `TimedRunHooks`, `acceptance_run.py`), and wake, overworld check and the run loop with A16 and A33 in `acceptance_smoke.py`. **Required marks:** every minimap entrance row on a map that is not a level interior, except a cell the strength bracket closed under `over_strength_ceiling` for the final loadout. **Recorded:** `looked` with a `block_type`; a locked mark also has `needs: key`. Only looks made during the run count: looks loaded from a persisted knowledge base do not. Before the runner starts, the smoke script clears `looked`, `block_type` and `needs` on every entrance row, so Investigate looks again, and then takes the gate's snapshot (`M9AcceptanceMetrics.snapshot`): a mark recorded at that run-start snapshot is `pre_recorded` and never counts toward the catalog. The snapshot is taken at run start, not at the gate's first tick, because the first decision can already record a look. **Town:** the knowledge-base town cell at the last decision of the run; leaving town after a visit clears it. A minimap whose marks the bracket all closed requires none; "no entrance catalog" means no marks were listed. A run fails on: any death under `--stop-on-death`; a tick where `should_retreat` held outside the survival states; an unsafe Recover withdraw; 24 Step-sending decisions in a row at one cell with one reason; any API error; and, on a run of at least 95% of the default time budget, an incomplete entrance catalog or not standing on town at the end. Heal actions are reported, not gated. The live pass that clears every Olympuff mark is after A25 and A58; the gate ships offline first (A29 partial until that run). | A25, A27, A28 |

**M10: Curiosity and clues.**

| ID | Item | Depends on |
|---|---|---|
| A30 | **Interest list and Investigate.** `Read`, `Say`, `get_zone`, walk to look; the curiosity budget. Shipped: `Read` of readable cells in sight and `Say` once to NPCs within 25 blocks, from where the agent stands (free, never charged); an applied one is remembered in the knowledge base, a target refused 3 times is dropped for the run; `get_zone` is A7's spare-window probes (respawn ring first, then the path); door and entrance looks walk next to an A27 minimap mark or a door with no recorded warp and no look yet on any map the knowledge base knows except level interiors (a map whose position read named a positive `level`; Level owns those doors, A37), routing through recorded door warps when the mark is on another map (subject to the curiosity cap), read `block_type` and `locked` from terrain (Manual §9.2), and store them with `looked` on the `kb.entrances` row and the map's door entry; `kb.entrances` is keyed `"<map_id>:<x>,<y>"` (old `"x,y"` rows are rekeyed from their `map_id` once, when the knowledge base loads); `needs` is set only to `key` on a locked door (Manual §9.2, §11), since a block never says whether it breaks and "across water" is the route, not the cell; a look with no route, or one that cannot read the cell from beside it, counts as a refusal; the curiosity cap limits walk-to-look detours and future Break queues to the directive `curiosity` share over the last 600 ticks, while reads and speech from where the agent stands stay free; the window counts game ticks only and is not reset on a map change or death. Scroll reads and subtype probes are A56, nominated with readable cells. Since A61 Investigate runs only for a plan `read` or `say` op; the interest list, door looks and the curiosity budget are removed (`interest_list.py`, `door_look.py`, `curiosity_budget.py`). | A5, A17 |
| A56 | **Scroll reads in Investigate.** Learn which `supply_subtype_code` values are scrolls (log every supply seen, `Read` one of each unseen code once, keep codes that do not answer `nothing_to_read`, GAME_NOTES open questions); store them in the knowledge base and nominate unread carried or in-sight scrolls for free `Read` like readable cells. To observe a code in live play, spot a scroll on the map or in a pack, read its `supply_subtype_code` from `get_entity_tiles` or the snapshot's supplies, and `Read` it once to confirm it is not `nothing_to_read`; note it in [`docs/observations/A30_live_play.md`](docs/observations/A30_live_play.md). Shipped (`scroll_investigation.py`): codes from entity reads and inventory go to `kb.extra` `seen_supply_codes`; one supply per unseen code is probed (`probed_supply_codes`), and a code whose read applies joins `scroll_subtype_codes`; an applied scroll read adds the supply to `read_supplies` and its text to `kb.clues` as a `scroll` row (A32); a known-scroll supply that answers `nothing_to_read` is also added to `read_supplies` and never read again; chest contents are not read. Reads sort signs first, then probes, then scrolls, nearest first. | A30 |
| A31 | **Odd-block detector.** Nominates blocks for `Break`. **M4: Strategist.** Its directives file is A8.

| ID | Item | Depends on |
|---|---|---|
| A34 | **Plan schema and goal stack.** Op table, field validation, param limits; states consume goals; the built-in plan with no model. Since A61 dispatch settles the stack once per decision and runs the stall clock for every executor; `plan_goals.py` is gone and Gather reads its op. `wait` needs `why` and at most 30 s; the built-in `hold` (one-hour wait) and `wander` goals are gone. **Stall clock (A61).** One clock in dispatch: the top op's executor resets it with an outcome that makes progress (a step, a Take, a Use that finishes the op); a `Compose` or `Use` try, or an executor's hold such as Travel waiting on an occupant, is not progress. A reflex holding the round leaves it alone. The one exception is **Break** at stuck step 2 of the owner's own walk (`dispatch.OWNER_WALKS`: Travel `travel:*` and its hunting-ground search's `explore_area` (A27), Explore `explore_area`, Level `level:*`, Boss `boss:*`; Shop's walk to a shop or town and its search round town, `travel:*` and `explore_area` (A120); Investigate and Loot walks give up instead of escalating; an executor that walks under another op's goal label, as Travel's search does, must list that label there too, or Break working its walk lets the op stall and drop): a step toward the block resets the clock and a `Use` try leaves it alone. Break working any other walk (the safe default's `explore`), or only re-arming, does not stop the clock. **Since A120** an executor with its way shut or nothing to work on asks the planner and holds (`way.clear_the_way`), a hold that is not progress, and the safe default never moves under its op (`explore.HOLD_THEIR_WALK`, every executor but Explore), so the clock runs on a character standing still for the planner, never on one walked about for no op. | A5, A8 |
| A35 | **Strategist thread: the AI planner owns the goal stack.** Shipped (`strategist.py`). On by default in every live run: `run`, and every smoke and probe script, plays with it; `--no-planner` (or `AGENTREALM_NO_PLANNER=1`) is a test mode, not the product. With it on and no key, startup fails with one line before any API call. Provider-agnostic behind `complete(messages)` (`LLMClient`): `AGENTREALM_PLANNER_PROVIDER` picks `anthropic` (default; the official `anthropic` SDK, the planner's one optional dependency, model `claude-sonnet-5-5` unless `AGENTREALM_PLANNER_MODEL` says otherwise, `AGENTREALM_PLANNER_EFFORT` default `low`, server-side refusal fallback on) or `openai` (stdlib `urllib`, model required); with no provider set, OpenAI is picked only when an OpenAI key is the only key. Keys come only from the environment: `AGENTREALM_PLANNER_ANTHROPIC_KEY`, else `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`; `AGENTREALM_PLANNER_OPENAI_KEY`, else `OPENAI_API_KEY` (the planner's own names first, since some hosts strip the standard ones). Each window the tick thread moves `Memory.clue_signals`, `nav_stuck.stuck_signals` and `Memory.strategist_signals` (death, goal_done, goal_failed, directives) into the inbox, and raises `map` (every change of map or level, re-entering one included, with its level; it replaces the old `level` trigger), `hurt` (health falls below `AGENTREALM_PLANNER_HURT_FRACTION` of max, default 0.5, once per drop), `idle` (no applied Step for `AGENTREALM_PLANNER_IDLE_MINUTES`, default 10) and `timer` (`AGENTREALM_PLANNER_REPLAN_S`, default 15 s, after the last call ended, A83). Any trigger sends a call as soon as none is in flight, the position is known, self and inventory have each been read once (A100), and the budget allows. Budget is per minute of play, so a session never falls silent partway: `AGENTREALM_PLANNER_CALLS_PER_MIN` (6) and `AGENTREALM_PLANNER_TOKENS_PER_MIN` (uncached input, cache writes included, plus answer as the API reports them, cache reads left out; unset, the larger of 40000 and 1.5 times the cached prefix, about 102k today, A62; a call with no reported usage is charged its uncached prompt at 4 characters a token) over the last 60 s, every attempt counted, failed ones included. The old per-run call and token caps and the 60 s gate are gone. With the planner on, the goal stack starts empty and only the planner or directives `goals` fill it: no stack op comes from anywhere else, and the built-in plan from `policy.goals` is put on the stack only in the test mode. One call at a time goes to a background thread that only sends the prompt and waits; the world, plan, `Memory` and trace are touched only on the tick thread. The prompt is a cached system prefix (the game reference, `docs/GAME_NOTES.md`, the progression stages, the op table `plan.OP_FIELDS`, the contract, and each survival param's meaning and which way tightens it, `plan.PARAM_MEANINGS`; A62) and a user message with the triggers, the state lines (the whole remaining stack among them, each op marked `pinned`, a directives op, or `planner`, `safe_ground` (whether the character stands on a known safe tile, `unknown` until a zone read covers it), `nearest_safe` and `town` (cell, Chebyshev distance and eight-way direction; A23 survive-a-fight run 1: the planner waited "because hostiles cannot hurt in town" 14 cells outside it), `last_reply_rejected` (every op the previous reply had dropped and every param change ignored, with the reason, shown once; three runs asked for `retreat_hits: 1` "to retreat sooner"), `given_up_travel`, the travel destination cells stuck detection gave up on, each with `retry_at_tick`, when its backoff ends (A90), `stall` (once the character has not moved, gained or spent gems, gained or lost an item or cleared a level for `STALL_SECONDS`, 30, how long and its last decision), `level_entrances` (the known entrance marks, nearest first, 8 shown, with what a look filed), and `shop_prices` (every item seen for sale, cheapest first, with the gems held and which they buy now); a `travel` with no x, y shows without the `0, 0` placeholder and with `goes_to`, the cell it resolves to now (free-play run 1: shown at `0, 0`, `travel:town` was re-sent unchanged 22 times)),  every clue in `kb.clues`, and the directives `instructions`; it is logged (`ask`, the prefix as its size and sha256 digest) before the call, and the reply after (`applied`, `kept`, `cleared` or `error`). Before play, `Strategist.check` makes one cheap provider call (the model read); a refused key (HTTP 401 or 403) stops `run` and every smoke script with exit 2 and one line (`PlannerAuthError`), as does any other 4xx but 429, such as a typo'd model (404); rate limits, server errors and the network are left to the run. A failed call (network, HTTP status, refusal) keeps the stack, puts its triggers back, and backs off: the next call waits 2 s, doubling per failure in a row, at most 120 s (`BACKOFF_BASE_S`, `BACKOFF_CAP_S`; live: six failed calls in 1.5 s). A reply that is not a JSON object is handled as a failed call: the stack is kept, its triggers go back, and the backoff applies (A82). Each failure, and each reply whose ops are all invalid, reaches the acceptance hooks as an error (`on_strategist_error`); a reply with at least one valid op, or an explicit empty stack, is an accepted plan (`on_strategist_reply`); one with no `goals` key (notes or params only) is neither. A reply is read as JSON (a code fence around it is fine) and checked by `parse_plan_payload` (A34). Its `params` merge onto `Plan.params` at once, key by key, within the directives floor. A reply with no `goals` key keeps the stack. Its goals are first reconciled with what changed while the call was out (`strategist.Asked`, stamped on each ask with the stack and the supplies held then): one copy of each op that finished or was dropped since (its `goal_done` or `goal_failed`) is taken out, since a reply carries over the stack it was shown, while a further copy, or an op the prompt never showed, is the planner asking for another and stays; and a new `buy` head counts from what was held when the planner asked, plus one per buy of that item finished since (free-play run 7: a reply asked before `buy matches` finished put it back, and the second pair spent the last gems). Gems need no reconciling: `gather_gems` counts an absolute total and `buy` checks the price at the shop. A stack equal to the one left (ops compared without their `why`, and an empty reply on an empty stack included) keeps its progress (stall clock, `wait` start, block snapshot, path), so a 15 s timer reply never restarts an op or the fallback walk; the same op on top (also compared without `why`) keeps its own progress and the op itself, so the path set for it stays owned, and only the ops below change. Otherwise its valid goals become the stack and the current path and every committed walk (`Memory.walks`, A15) are dropped, and no valid goal (an empty list, or only invalid ops) clears it: the planner layer emits nothing and the dispatcher's safe default runs (exploring in safe ground, A61). Directives `goals` (`directive_stack_ops`) override all of this, manual steering: their ops still left (`Plan.directive_ops`) stay on top, a reply's goals go below them (one repeating a directives op does not stack it twice), and once they are done or dropped the planner owns the stack. The system prompt tells the model that pinned ops cannot be removed, so it plans around them and replans only its own part (second live Walk run: unable to see pins, it spent 17 of 21 calls trying to drop the pinned target). A `travel` op of any `to` that resolves to a cell stuck detection gave up on leaves the stack wherever it sits, directives part included (A16), and a reply's `travel` that resolves to such a cell is filtered out in `_settle` before the same-stack check, so a re-send never reaches the stack, raises no `goal_failed` and sets off no new call. A program adds directives goals in memory with `CharacterConfig.pinned_goals`, each pinned once: when its op pops done, is dropped, or stuck detection gives its target up, the runner unpins it (`Runner.unpin_done_goals`) and the op leaves the stack (`Runner.drop_given_up_ops`), so neither a planner reply nor a later directives reload brings it back. The planner's SDK is in `python/requirements.txt` (`make setup`). `wait` needs a `why` and at most 30 s (`MAX_WAIT_SECONDS`). When the planner needs something the states cannot do, add an op to the table and the state that runs it, never a state that starts itself. | A32, A34 |
| A62 | **The planner knows the game.** The planner's system prompt is the game reference, then the measured facts in `docs/GAME_NOTES.md`, then a short progression section (survive and learn; build up loot, gear and supplies; beat levels; beat the world, each with readiness thresholds as guidance the model applies, not code), then the op contract; the model is told to plan from the reference and to trust the measured facts where the two disagree. The reference is `python/agentrealm_adk/reference/agentrealm_reference.md`, the site's docs and guides (every page under `/docs` and `/guides`) as Markdown with its source and fetch date on top, written by `scripts/refresh_planner_reference.py` (standard library) and committed, so play never fetches the website. It is about 72k tokens, so `planner_reference.py` splits it at each page heading: the core sections (manual §1, 3, 6, 8, 9, 11, 14, 15, 16; API principles, tick loop, intents, events) always go in, the rest in file order under about 60k tokens, and history and setup (the changelog and manual §2 quick start, §4 accounts and keys, §13 running locally, and the API page accounts and access, matched by key prefix like the core) only by name; `AGENTREALM_PLANNER_REFERENCE_SECTIONS` picks `all`, `core`, or core plus a comma list, and the prompt names what it left out. The whole system prompt is the same on every call and cached: `cache_control` on Anthropic, a fixed `prompt_cache_key` on OpenAI (which caches long prefixes itself). Triggers, state and stack go in the user message after it. The state lines carry what stage readiness needs: health, gems, `armed`, `worn`, `held` (supply codes with counts), `levels_cleared` (from snapshots) and `level_count` (from GetWorld). **Budget (decided):** one rule for both providers. `AGENTREALM_PLANNER_TOKENS_PER_MIN` counts uncached input plus output, cache writes included (Anthropic `cache_creation_input_tokens`; OpenAI prompt tokens not reported cached). Cache reads are not counted: they cost a tenth of input and `AGENTREALM_PLANNER_CALLS_PER_MIN` bounds them. Unset, the budget is the larger of 40000 and 1.5 times the prefix (`default_tokens_per_min`, from the prompt, about 102k today), so the first call and a cache miss never silence the planner. **Cost estimate** (`claude-sonnet-5-5`: $2 input, $10 output, $0.20 cache read, $2.50 cache write per MTok; prefix about 68k tokens, about 2k dynamic input and an assumed 1k answer per call): about $0.03 a call on a cache hit, so about $0.11 a minute (about $7 an hour) with a call every 15 s (the timer's gap after each reply, A83, makes that an upper bound) and about $0.17 a minute (about $10 an hour) at the 6-calls cap; each cache write adds about $0.17. The trace logs the prefix as its size and digest, not its text. The refresh script stops without writing when a `CORE_SECTIONS` entry matches no section (a renamed site heading), and a test checks every entry against the committed reference. Tests: `test_planner_reference.py` (prompt order, cache marker, dynamic part after the prefix, sections, the refresh script on a fixture page) and the provider tests in `test_strategist.py`. | A35 |
| A36 | **M4 acceptance.** M4 done-when, as an offline fixture: `python/tests/test_m4_acceptance.py` plays an invented test world with the real `Runner` and states against a fake server, with a fake model in place of the LLM, and `agentrealm_agent/m4_acceptance.py` checks it. The world has a sign whose text is the clue, a torch for sale, an entrance behind a wall of hedges. The agent reads the sign (Investigate); the clue trigger reaches the strategist, whose answer is `buy` torch, `travel` to the point before the hedge, `break_block` it with `burn`, and `travel` to the entrance. It passes when at least one clue trigger reached the strategist, no request failed, and each of those ops was planned (an applied answer had it, extra fields allowed), run (the state that owns it sent a new queue that acted on it: `Shop` for `buy`, `Break` for `break_block`, plan pathing for `travel`, in Explore or in Travel's fallback; a round spent on a reflex, another goal or a higher-priority state does not count and is not a failure), and finished (the stack popped it as done). Hooks: `before_tick` gets `acted_op`, the stack op this decision acted on (`Plan.acted`, set by `Plan.note_progress` when the owning state steps toward the head op or sends its Take or Use, and cleared by the runner each round), else None; `on_strategist_trigger` sees each trigger as it moves into the strategist's inbox; `on_strategist_applied` sees each goal stack the strategist applies. Every hook the runner or strategist calls is declared as a no-op on `AcceptanceHooks` (`acceptance.py`) and called unconditionally: no `hasattr` or `getattr` duck typing. The fixture found two gaps, fixed here: plan pathing dropped a `break_block` head ("no Break state yet") when Explore reached it first, and nothing ever finished one. Now Break owns the op: pathing leaves it on the stack and drops it only after the usual 30-second stall, which only `Plan.note_progress` resets (Break calls it only when it steps toward or uses its plan target, never on a reflex or the goto's stuck break), and it is done once the block's type changes from what it was when the op reached the top, as for `use_block`. | A33, A35 |

**M11: Levels.**

| ID | Item | Depends on |
|---|---|---|
| A37 | **Level.** Priority 5, below Travel and above Explore. Active when the current map's `level` is a positive integer; `level` belongs to the map and comes only from its minimap entry (A145; the position read carries none and is never read for one, A164), and a new map has none until a minimap read names it. Walks toward the nearest door whose warp is not yet recorded, else the nearest door, else a frontier tile; fight and pickup reflexes still run first (same door preference as the `doors` goal). Exit rule: a `travel:*` directive that resolves outranks Level, so Travel can walk the agent out (to town, say); Level takes over again once that op is reached or while none resolves. With no door or frontier step it sends nothing without `wait`, so dispatch falls through to Travel's or Explore's goals (A44). Since A61 Level runs only for a plan `enter_level` op: it walks to the op's entrance, then the rooms, and finishes the op when no step is left. | A23, A27 |
| A38 | **Boss.** Plan preconditions, the clock, progress from boss `health`. Shipped: priority 5, below Recover, Loot, Investigate, Solve, Gather and Travel, above Level and Explore, so a `fight_boss` op inside a level is not taken over by Level. Op: `fight_boss` with `x`, `y` of the boss door and optional preconditions `min_health`, `min_potions` (potions held or in the chest), `armed` (weapon code) and `worn` (codes); Boss runs only while they hold, or once a fight is on. Detection: a boss is an NPC that carries `health` on entity reads, the only kind that does (API Reads; GAME_NOTES Combat); Fight never targets one. A fight starts when a boss is seen while `fight_boss` is the top op, and ends on death, when that op is no longer the top (popped, dropped, or the stack reloaded), or when the boss is out of view with no clock running. Defeat is observed, never inferred from absence: that boss read at `health <= 0`, or a `level_clear_ceremony` since it was first seen; Boss then pops the op itself. Clock (retired by A163, which counts it and leaves by the room's exit door): `boss_fight_end_tick`, read only when a round trip carries it (the published docs name no clock field; GAME_NOTES open questions); while it runs the fight counts as on. During a fight Retreat and Flee stand down: Boss walks back toward the door when Retreat's rule fires, unless it commits, which it does when no step toward the door is open or the clock has 10 s or less left. Deferred: retreat-out paths to the op's door on the current map; a boss room with its exit elsewhere needs the room's door from the door graph. | A37 |
| A39 | **Solve.** `Compose`, keys at doors, `use_block`. Priority 4, after Investigate and before Gather; runs while the stack's top op is `compose` or `use_block` (`pathing.plan_step` leaves those to it). `compose` sends `Compose` with one held fragment per slot (lowest id; a duplicate piece stays in the inventory) once no held fragment reports `missing_slots` and the slots held cover `piece_count`; slots may be 0, and a fragment whose `missing_slots` cannot be read is kept and judged by the slots held. It is done when the whole is held. `use_block` `Arm`s the named supply if it is not armed, walks into its `attack_range` of the target (default 1), and `Use`s the block. Once no `compose` or `use_block` op is on top (done or dropped), it `Arm`s once whatever was armed before the first swap, if still in hand; a rejected re-arm is not retried. Only supplies in hand count: one in the carried chest is not withdrawn. It is done when the target's `block_type` differs from what it was when the op reached the top (a broken block shows its destroyed type, GAME_NOTES Breaking blocks), as a `BlockChanged` or terrain read shows; a tile-name guess never finishes it. An op that makes no progress for `PLAN_STALL_SECONDS` (30 s) is dropped with a log line, as A34 drops ops with no path: a step toward the target is progress; sending nothing (pieces or supply missing, target unreachable) or a `Compose`, `Arm` or `Use` that has not finished the op is not. While it sends nothing for its op it clears the way to the target (a block on the way a held item opens) or asks the planner and holds (A120). | A34, A37 |
| A40 | **M11 acceptance.** M11 done-when, as a gate: `scripts/smoke_m11_olympuff.py` and `agentrealm_agent/m11_acceptance.py`. The smoke script wakes a sleeping character with one `Wait` (and waits out a respawn), reads the world and position, and plays on `characters/olympuff_m11.toml` (`doors` then `explore`) until the gate passes or the wall-clock limit (default 7200 s) is reached. Hooks run from the runner through `AcceptanceHooks` (`acceptance.py`, including `on_level_clear` on `level_clear_ceremony`); the smoke scripts take `--character-id` or `--character-name` (A59). `before_tick` sees the world the decision saw, before the response is applied. A run fails on: any death under `--stop-on-death` (the first one ends the run; by default deaths are counted and reported, not failed, and play goes on after the respawn, A63 run 3); a tick where `should_retreat` held outside Sync, Downed, Escape, Retreat, Heal, Flee or Boss (`SURVIVAL_STATES` in `m11_acceptance.py`, set as its `survival_states`); a Recover `WithdrawFromChest` that runs anywhere but a known safe tile; 24 Step-sending decisions in a row at one cell with one reason (a loop); more than 3 oscillation-guard give-ups within 6000 ticks (same rule as A16); any API error; and, on a run of at least 95% of the default limit, the M11 milestone. **Milestone rule:** at least one `level_clear_ceremony` with a `level_number`, then an attempt at the next level: after that clear, the character leaves the level and enters the interior of a level not yet cleared (positive `level` on the position read, not one a clear was seen for), or the plan's top op is `enter_level` or `fight_boss` while not already inside that level. The gate reads the plan's top op from `Plan.current()`, which the runner passes to `before_tick` as `plan_op` (not `Memory.goal_op`, the op the current path was built for, which nothing clears); the op that was on top on the tick a clear arrived never counts, since its fight produced the clear and it stays on top until Boss pops it; it is matched by door (`x`, `y`), so no `enter_level` or `fight_boss` at that door counts either. Left the level means back on the overworld map: any other map counts as still inside, because a new interior map has no `level` until a read names it (A37), and the op names a door, not a level. **Planner required:** a level clears only by killing its boss, Boss runs only on a `fight_boss` op (A38), and on this profile only the AI planner (A35) pushes one, so the smoke script exits 2 with `--no-planner` or without a planner key. Shorter practice runs still fail on deaths, misses, loops, oscillation aborts and API errors; the milestone is judged only on a nearly full limit. M11's metrics build on `TimedRunHooks` (through `SurvivalAcceptanceMetrics`) and the smoke uses `FULL_RUN_FRACTION` (`acceptance_run.py`), shared with A16, A25 and A33. Its metrics subclass `SurvivalAcceptanceMetrics` (`acceptance_survival.py`, with A16 and A29), adding Boss to `survival_states` and using the shared `OscillationAbortTracker`, and the wake, overworld-start (`navigation_start`) and run helpers in `acceptance_smoke.py` with M7 and M10 (A33). The live Olympuff run that passes this gate waits on earlier acceptance live passes (including A58). | A36, A38, A39 |
