**M13: Make it yours.** The point of this repo is that people build their own agents for Agent Realm, and that it is easy and fun. A newcomer should get a character playing in minutes, understand how the agent thinks in one sitting, and add their own idea without reading the whole codebase. Done when someone new can follow the README from clone to a running character, then add a working state of their own using only the guide.

| ID | Item | Depends on |
|---|---|---|
| A50 | **README for newcomers.** Short: what this is, quick start on agentrealm.gg, "how the agent thinks" in about ten lines (dispatcher, states, reflexes, plan), and "make it yours" pointing at the guide and the starter. Per-state and policy detail lives in [`docs/CHARACTER_AND_STATES.md`](docs/CHARACTER_AND_STATES.md); the README links to it and to **Scheduler** / **Reflexes** / **Plan** here. | |
| A51 | **Write-your-own-state guide.** `docs/MAKE_IT_YOURS.md`: how a state works (`guard`, `act`, `StateOutcome`, `wait`, fall-through), where it goes in the dispatcher, how to read the world model and knowledge base, how to send intents within the call budget, how to test it, and how to tune an existing state with directives. Shipped: worked example `ExampleGreetState` (`states/example_greet.py`, about 40 lines) says hello once to each other player in sight, which no shipped state does (Greet and Investigate greet NPCs, A65). It is off by default: not in the shipped `STATES`, and the guide shows the one line that adds it. Its test asserts the shipped tuple leaves it out and drives the real dispatcher with `STATES` patched to include it. | A44 |
| A52 | **Starter agent.** One small, readable file that plays on its own (sync, walk, flee, explore) and that people copy and grow, beside the full reference agent. Runnable with one command from the README; covered by a test. | A50 |
| A53 | **Hackability pass.** Read the code as a newcomer: clear names, a short docstring on each state saying what it does and when it runs, no dead code. Fix what gets in the way of extending it, without changing behavior. | A51 |
| A86 | **`make test` never touches `python/.state`.** The suite wrote test traces, `worlds/sandbox.json` and a re-save of a real world's knowledge base into `python/.state`, which can corrupt a user's saved knowledge and did touch a live runner's state. `config.STATE_DIR` now honors `AGENTREALM_STATE_DIR` (default `python/.state`); `python/tests/__init__.py` points it at a fresh temp dir before the agent is imported, an audit hook there records every file operation the test process makes under `python/.state`, and its `load_tests` appends `StateDirUntouched`, which runs last and fails if `STATE_DIR`, `knowledge_base.WORLDS_DIR` or `supplies.CACHE_PATH` resolves under `python/.state` or the hook recorded anything. It sees only the test process, so a live `run` on the same checkout cannot fail it. | |
| A87 | **Rules, not run patches.** A fix found in a live run changes the agent in one of two ways: it generalizes a rule a state already has (a threshold, a predicate, a cost), or it hands the situational call to the planner (which region, whether a risk is worth it, when to give up). It never adds a branch for the situation the run hit. Code comments state the rule in game terms; the run that found it is cited in the PLAN.md row and `docs/observations`, not in code. Enforced by `tests/test_architecture.py`: the number of run citations in agent source (`run N` or `runs N`, any case, each occurrence counted; `starter_agent.py` included) is a ratchet, set at today's count, that a PR may only lower; `make test` fails a PR that raises it. Each of A88–A91 lowers it by its files' share. AGENTS.md **Rules for agents** carries the rule. Shipped: the rule and the ratchet. | A53 |
| A88 | **Gather and gem yield under A87.** Re-derive `states/gather.py` and `gem_yield.py` from `docs/observations/A63_gem_gathering_runs.md` and `A67_free_play_runs.md` as they would have been written had A87 been in force. Carve-outs to fold into a general rule or hand to the planner: field cells before safe ones (A63 run 2), the shadowing hostile (A63 run 3), piles outside the named region and route tries per kind (run 5), the kept walk's taken first step (run 7), the frontier clear of hostiles (A82). Likely shape: one scored target search over cells to cut, piles, field ground and frontier under one hostile-ground filter, with the planner choosing the region and whether to work near a hostile. Done when every rule in both files is stated in game terms with no run in a comment, the choices that depend on the situation are the planner's, the ratchet falls by both files' share, and the Gather tests test the rule, not the run. Line count and rule count are not targets. **Shipped.** Gather runs one search each decision (`states/gather.py`): `ground` works out where it may work once (`gather_safe.gather_ground` for every target; for cutting, not a barren region unless the op names it, not ground where cuts keep having no effect, not a cell cut too recently), `_targets` lists every place to go for (grass, gem piles, and the edge of unexplored ground not given up by stuck detection), bounded to the op's target region while Gather works one (while a way reaches something there, its middle while no cell of it is known; else it works anywhere, `Ground.lift_region`), and each is priced as its walk (`_price`) plus its work (`GemOdds.work`), the actions there for the gems still needed at the yield there: a cut per gem; a pile one `Take` for a sure gem, then cuts for the rest (so its edge over grass is the cuts its gem saves, 4 steps at 20%, no longer a fixed `GEM_PILE_STEPS`); an edge first pays the steps past it to grass. The cheapest wins, searched once per set of targets of one work (`_cheapest`). What is in reach (a pile beside it, the grass it stands on) costs no walk and is worked unless the walk Gather would take costs less in all (`_in_reach`), so poor grass underfoot is not cut on the way to better ground. No walk crosses a known hostile's reach: the search treats it as a wall (`gather_safe.route_bar`), so it finds the way round instead of trying a few targets per kind. Commitment (A71) and the one repricing rule (A109, `targets.outpriced`) apply to every kept target, edges and the unseen region included. Where the carve-outs went: field cells before safe ones and the walk out of safe ground (A63 run 2) are gone, since safe-zone grass is cut until its no-effect cuts mark the zone uncuttable (GAME_NOTES.md: learned, not assumed) and unexplored ground is always a target; the shadowing hostile (A63 run 3: `SHADOW_SECONDS`, the move-off walk, Gather's own fight) is gone, since a hostile in view already bars the cells near it and prices the ground round it, and fighting it is the engagement's call or the planner's `fight`; piles outside the named region (free-play run 5) follow the one region bound every target has, and a pile beside the character outside the region is left to Pickup; route tries per kind are replaced by the route bar in the search; the kept walk's taken first step (free-play run 7) is A71's hold, kept for every target; the frontier clear of hostiles (A82, kind `clear`) is the ordinary edge target on `gather_ground`; the better region a poor one was left for (A63, free-play run 2: `better_region`, `BETTER_REGION_BLOCKS`, `GOOD_MIN_CUTS`) is the price, since a region's yield is in it, and naming a region is the planner's (State `gem_yield` lists the best); A105's near-frontier-versus-far-grass pricing and A109's ballooned walk are the general price and commitment, no longer cases. `gather_status` drops `moving off from a hostile that shadows`, `fighting a hostile that shadows`, `walking to ground clear of hostiles` and `heading out of safe ground`. Subsumes the Gather parts of A22 (shadow, field before safe, walk out, route tries), A63 (better region), A82 item 1, A105 and A109. The planner contract and the `gather_gems` row of `plan.OP_FIELDS` say the same. The run-citation ratchet falls by its share, 15, to 90 with A90's 4. Tests: `test_gather.py` by rule (`WorkInReachTest`, `TargetSearchTest`, `NoEffectGroundTest`, `HostileNearTest`, `YieldPricingTest`, `TargetRegionTest`, `KeepsClearTest`), taking over the Gather classes of the free-play run 2, 5, 6, 7 and 8 test files. | A87 |
| A89 | **Heal and healing under A87.** The same pass over `states/heal.py` and `healing.py`. Carve-outs: the safe pick around a pack and the re-plan on the reach-priced grid (A63 run 4), food behind cuttable bushes (A16 Walk run 4), the re-armed potion (run 4), refusal tries written off (run 5). Likely shape: Heal picks its target by one cost over safe ground, refusals stay one table from API code to action (A80), and the try-and-wait bookkeeping moves to the executor or runner. The planner decides when healing is worth leaving the op for; Heal only decides how. Done as A88. **Shipped.** **The planner's line:** the health share below which health is low is the planner's new `low_health` param (default 0.5, in 0..1, `plan.PARAM_MEANINGS`; a survival param the planner may only raise past the directives' value, which only the directives file lowers), replacing `LOW_HEALTH_SHARE`; one line decides both when a carried potion is spent (A96, `healing.spend_potion`, State `potion_drunk_at`) and when Heal's rest on safe ground goes ahead of the op on top (A84's "plan op first unless health is low", free-play run 9). Heal decides only how to heal. **One price:** every Heal walk (`_walk_toward`) is priced on Heal's one ground (`_ground`: hazards avoided, a known hostile's reach costly) with each block on the straight way a held item opens costed at its break; when the cheapest way crosses such a block Heal walks that way on its own ground, bounded like any Heal walk, and opens the first block it crosses once in the opener's reach (`break_toward`). Food within `FOOD_REACH` is picked by that price (`_food_target`), food in reach costing none, so food behind a bush a held blade cuts (A16 Walk run 4) is the general price, not a fallback tried when no walk reaches, and the nearest-three-by-straight-line tries (`FOOD_CANDIDATES`) are gone; food no walk reaches has no price and is not a target, so it raises no `stuck` give-up. The safe pick around a pack (A63 run 4) is `pathing.reachable_safe_goal`'s skip of hostile ground, shared with Retreat and Park, and the re-plan of a kept path that a new hostile's reach now crosses is a rule of every Heal walk. **One give-up memory:** Heal's own `Memory.heal_safe_given_up` (until a full heal, or 300 ticks at low health) is gone: a walk to the committed safe cell that is given up, or a cell a check rules out, is given up in A90's memory (`stuck.give_up`, which also raises the `stuck` trigger), and safe ground stays given up while any such give-up on this map backs off (`_safe_given_up`), so cells it cannot reach still never chain; when to send the character back is the planner's (`retry_at_tick`). **One table, in the executor:** what is sent after a refused food `Take` or `Use` on yourself, or a `Use` that did nothing (A80, A76), moved from `healing.py` to `executor/supply_refusals.py` (`refusal_action`, `noop_action`, `can_try`, `must_arm`, `must_stand_on`); the runner files it (`Runner.file_supply_refusals`, never a probe's guess, A77), and states only ask. Names follow the game, not the potion: `Memory.supply_refusals`, `self_use`, `self_use_rearm`, `Refusal`, `NoopUse`, `AppliedSelfUse`. A108's `heals()` gate is one rule (`supply_refusals.effect_measure`): a `Use` on yourself went through once its item is used up or what it raises rose (health, for an item that heals; a chest only shows as used up), and a no-effect hold lifts once what it raises falls below where it was. The re-armed weapon after a `Use` on yourself (run 4) is `equip.weapon_to_rearm`'s rule, sent by `use_item.rearm_after_use`. Tests: `test_heal.py` by rule (`LowHealthLineTest`, `OneSafeCellTest`, `FoodByPriceTest`), taking over `test_freeplay_run9.py`; `test_potion_spend.py` (was `test_freeplay_run11.py`); `test_heal_refusal.py` (`FiledOnceTest`, taking over the Heal classes of `test_freeplay_run5.py`). **Superseded by A130** for resting: `low_health` now decides only when a carried potion is spent; Heal's walk to safe ground, its safe-cell commitment (`pathing.HEAL_TARGET`) and `_safe_given_up` are gone, and `OneSafeCellTest` with them. | A87 |
| A90 | **Stuck escalation under A87.** Shipped: `navigation/stuck.py`, `navigation/oscillation.py` and the runner's give-up expiry, re-derived as they would have been written under A87. **One ladder:** the escalation steps serve every walk, and nothing in `stuck.py` names Gather, Travel, Explore or Break (the Break walk's goal moved to `break_memory.BREAK_GOAL`). **One memory of what failed where:** `NavStuckMemory.given_up`, a `GiveUp` per walk target keyed by goal, map and cell (`goal_key`, so a give-up is its map's only), replaces `backoff_until`, `backoff_power`, `given_up_travel` and `given_up_hubs`. It is aged by time alone: a give-up holds until `BACKOFF_BASE_TICKS * 2**n` (`n` the give-ups of that target before it) has passed, keeps its power after it lapses, and is forgotten when the target is reached or, once lapsed, when more than `GIVE_UPS_KEPT` (64) are kept (lapsed ones go first, oldest first; a give-up still backed off is never forgotten). Every reader asks it: `stuck.is_backed_off` for any walk, `travel.resolve.given_up_travel` for Travel destinations (with when each lapses), `break_memory.given_up_blocks` for blocks. The hub exception (town and shop give-ups lapsing after 1200 ticks or 16 cells, every other Travel give-up held for the run) is gone: every Travel give-up lapses with its backoff, and the planner, told `retry_at_tick` on the `stuck` trigger and in `given_up_travel`, decides whether to send it again. `Runner.note_lapsed_give_ups` traces each lapse once (`stuck.lapsed`). **Pacing gives up every walk that took part, by construction:** `oscillation.note_move` files each move under the target its walk heads for (`walk_key`: the active stuck attempt's when it is that walk's, else the walk's committed target in `Memory.walks`, else its path's end), never under whichever attempt is active, so a Break walk to a block while Explore's attempt is active is still a walk, keyed by its block. When the guard fires, every walk that moved is given up through step 5 (`stuck.give_up`, reason `pacing`), with or without an attempt of its own; then every stuck attempt whose step 2 block was a target given up leaves step 2 (`stuck.escalate`, reason `pacing`). A given-up block is not nominated by a ladder, priced by the cost grid or cut toward by Heal while its backoff holds (`given_up_blocks`, `no_break_reason` `given_up_break`), and a planner `break_block` for it is dropped with the reason (`break_state._op_refusal`). Only Flee- and Retreat-only pacing gives nothing up (their escape is replanned around the cells). **The planner's call:** the top op stalls by A104's rule (`dispatch._stall_paced_op`) only when its executor paced with a step that was no walk, since nothing of its was given up; an executor whose paced moves were all walks keeps off the given-up targets by its own rule, so the state-specific `OWNER_WALKS` test is gone from the pacing rule. The `stalled_ops` record A111 wrote for a block is gone: a block is a walk target, so it lives in the walk-side memory and reaches the planner as a `stuck` trigger. The `oscillation` event lists `walks` (goal, target, states), `goals`, `unwalked` (states that paced with no walk) and `escalated`. Subsumes A103's pacing half and A111. The `RUN_CITATION` ceiling falls 109 → 105. Tests: `test_oscillation.py` (`EveryWalkGivenUpTest`, which alternates a Break walk to a step 2 block with an Explore walk whose attempt is the active one, as in free-play run 18; `PacedOpTest`), `test_give_ups.py`, `test_break_memory.py` (`GivenUpBlockTest`). Observations: `docs/observations/A67_free_play_runs.md` **Run 18**. | A87 |

What is sent after a refused or no-effect `Take` or `Use` on yourself is already the executor's (`executor/supply_refusals.py`, A89); the runner only files results into it, which stays. **M14: Ladder Lorraine.** A second full agent beside Gary, built on the same plumbing, that plays to one number: levels cleared per hour. Evan's picture: a prime directive (beat levels), a tree of sub-goals that serve it, and a readiness check at each node, so the agent asks first "can I beat the next level now?" and descends only as far as it must: hold what the level requires, be ready for its risk, hold the gems for what is missing, gather, stay alive (A139). Rules stay general (A87 from day one); every situational call (which region, whether a risk is worth it, when to give up) is the planner's, asked one question about one node. Lorraine imports the client, world model, pathing, navigation, executor, supplies and knowledge base from the agent ADK (`python/agentrealm_adk/`, A149), and nothing of Gary's. Done when she has played the same free-play script as Gary over at least three hours and her levels per hour, deaths and gems are in `docs/observations/`, whichever way they came out. The agent naming scheme and meta files (A137) ship first so the two can be told apart in every log and PR. This paragraph is M14's scope and done-when; like M13 it has no entry in `docs/PLAYABLE_AGENT_PLAN.md`.

| ID | Item | Depends on |
|---|---|---|
| A137 | **Agent names and meta files.** Shipped: Grinding Gary (`gary`), Ladder Lorraine (`lorraine`), Starter Stan (`stan`). One meta file each under `docs/agents/`, same six fields (name, plays to, how it thinks, temperament, will not, lineage); the scheme in AGENTS.md **Three agents, three names**; the README table. Commits, run notes and observations use the short name. | |
| A138 | **Lorraine's package and the import line.** Shipped: `python/ladder/`, run from `python/` as `python3 -m ladder create|run|status <profile>` on Gary's profile files (`tests/test_ladder_cli.py`); its parser and `status` mirror Gary's until A149 moves them into the ADK. Until her goal tree replaces it (A139–A141), `run` is Stan's loop (`starter_agent.run`: sync, flee, explore), a temporary tie that is her one import of Stan. The plumbing no longer reaches into Gary's planner or states: `oddities` takes `CAPABILITIES` from `supplies`; `GoalOp`, `EXPLORE_ANYWHERE` and `explore_targets` live in `targets`; `hostiles_near` and `is_safe_ish` in `hostile_ground`; `shop` stays Gary's and she does not import it. The line is the import closure: `tests/test_architecture.py` `ImportLineTest` imports `ladder` in a fresh interpreter and fails if `agentrealm_agent.strategist`, `plan`, `brain`, `runner` or any `states.*` module is loaded; everything else in `agentrealm_agent` is plumbing she may use. Since A149 that plumbing is the ADK (`agentrealm_adk`) and the test (`test_ladder_loads_nothing_of_garys`) fails if her closure loads any `agentrealm_agent` module. Her run-citation ceiling is 0 and never moves (`test_lorraine_cites_no_run`). | A137 |
| A139 | **The goal tree and its readiness predicates.** Shipped (Evan's tree): `ladder/goals.py` holds the tree as data, walked from the top every turn: beat the world; beat the next level (the target is the lowest level not in `levels_cleared`, A145); under it, in order, hold what it requires (the entrance's hard need, a look's `needs` or a clue's `clue_needs`, A147: a key, sandals, a bomb) and ready for its risk (heals, weapon, armor against the level's expected danger); under both, hold the gems for what's missing (from either branch, priced from known sellers, A131); under that, gather; under that, stay alive (the risk budget of the goal above). The tree is data (`goals.tree()`): each node carries its readiness predicate over `WorldModel` and the knowledge base (`Check`: ready, and why in game terms), the op that works it in Gary's op shape, the ops that can (what A140's planner may answer with) and the goal it serves (`subject`); predicates and ops read only their node and the walk's `Reading` (each check, and what is missing, read once a walk), so `walk` works on any tree. The walk: from the root into the first child not ready, children in order; only a node whose children are all ready is ever the turn's goal (`goals.walk`, on a fresh `tree()` unless given one). "Gather" is a node; where and how safely to gather is the planner's answer at it (A140). **One risk function**, `ladder/readiness.py` `risk_budget(goal, world, kb)`: health plus held heals (`healing.held_heal`, A132) against the goal's expected cost, the damage its hostiles are expected to deal while she kills them one by one, Gary's win estimate reused (`survival.expected_damage` over `ticks_to_kill_them`, the threat table and the published rolls, armor as worn, `survival.worn_defense`; a boss scaled by its served health). The value-to-risk rule: an ordinary goal may cost half of health plus heals, a valuable one (a level, which ends in its boss; a gem cache) three quarters (`VALUE_RISK_SHARE`). A level no hostile has been seen in is assumed to hold `LEVEL_PRIOR_FOES` (3) unmeasured hostiles until known hostiles remembered on its maps replace them; gathering is priced against the known hostiles in view or remembered within `survival.FIGHT_REACH_RADIUS`. A foe is only ever a known hostile (`survival.known_hostile`, the test Gary's engagement uses): `WorldModel.sightings` keeps every NPC and character seen, helpers, townsfolk and other players too, so it is never read as a list of hostiles. An item lying in sight that meets a need is one test, `entrance_needs.lying_in_sight` (which `in_sight` now asks). "Ready for its risk" is the function at level scale; "stay alive" is it on the goal being worked with `now`, counting only the heals usable now (up to max health), and its op restores margin with the first move that makes it fit (eat food in sight, buy a potion, `readiness.margin_after`), else back to town; a carried heal is never drunk for it, since it counted already and a drink leaves the margin as it was. What is missing (`readiness.missing`): the entrance's need unless it lies in sight, then the gear and heals with the most slack gained per gem until the level's risk fits; one with no known seller is named, not priced; a shop cell prices only the very item it was seen selling (the seller test's any-potion-for-a-potion, A21, would value a large potion at a small one's shelf). Ready for its risk arms and wears the best gear owned first (`equip`). The reflexes (A141) read the same function (`readiness.margin_now`). **Blocked nodes:** `goals.Blocks` marks a node `cannot` with its reason and an expiry (`BLOCK_TICKS`, A140 sets it, and lifts it early when the state its reason named changes); the walk never makes a blocked node the goal, records it as skipped with its reason, and descends into its children, taking the first with a node not ready under it (a ready node has nothing to do, so it is never the goal, whatever block above sent the walk into it); a child not ready whose whole way down is blocked blocks its parent too, with its reason, and the walk tries the parent's next child not ready (with none the parent gives no goal), so the level is never entered with a need unmet. Gather's `count` is the gem total what is missing costs, and with none short gather has no op. `ladder status` prints the self line, health, and the tree's readiness (`goals.dump`, without the goal) from two reads that change nothing, self and world, through the world model's own sync (`WorldModel.apply_self` now reads the self read's health, as a snapshot does, for Gary's and Stan's runners too; `test_world.SelfHealthTest`), and the knowledge base. It makes no round trip, which would take the events a live run is owed, so what only a round trip serves (what is carried, gems, levels cleared) reads as none there and the turn's goal is not shown; its reads still spend the character's call budget, so it is not run beside a live run on the same character. Nothing here asks the planner (A140) or acts (A141). Tests: `tests/test_ladder_goals.py`. | A138 |
| A140 | **One planner question.** Shipped: `ladder/ask.py`. The planner is given one node, the state that node's predicate reads (`ask.node_state`: its readiness check, where she is, health, gems, what is carried; nothing of any other node), the ops that can work it, the tree's own op as an example of the shape, and, as the cached prefix, the game reference and the measured facts; and asked one thing: `{"op": {...}}` to run now, or `{"cannot": why, "until": a state line}`. No stack, no stage prose (`ask.question`, `ask.CONTRACT`). A reply that is neither, or an op the node cannot run, is a failed call (`ask.read_reply`). A `cannot` marks the node blocked in A139's `Blocks` with its reason for `BLOCK_TICKS` (`ask.answer`), so the next walk descends past it. Its `until` names what would let the node be worked, and only a line that changes when the situation does (`ask.UNTIL_LINES`, read by `ask.watch_lines`): `held` (what is carried), `levels_cleared`, `sellers` (the items a seller is known for), `entrance_needs` (the next entrance's unmet need); `gems N`, lifted once N gems are held; or `ticks N`, a shorter expiry (at most `BLOCK_TICKS`). Where she stands, health and the readiness text change every step or hit and are never watched; any other `until` is dropped and the block holds to its expiry. An `until` already true when the `cannot` comes (`gems N` with N or more held, `ask.already_met`) is no wait: the node is blocked to its expiry and the planner is asked once more about it, with a note that the condition is already met; an op then lifts the block, a `cannot` waiting on something not yet true replaces it, and another already-met one leaves the plain block. A block lifts as soon as what it waits on comes (`ask.lift_changed`, run before every walk), and the next walk asks afresh about the goal it reaches. The call budget, backoff and reply parsing moved out of Gary's `strategist` into the ADK, `agentrealm_adk/llm.py`, with the providers and their settings unchanged (`ProviderConfig`, `connect`, `check_client`, `CallBudget`, `Backoff`, `parse_reply` with the caller's accept test, `default_tokens_per_min`): Gary's strategist and Lorraine's `ask.Planner` both import it directly (no re-export from `strategist`), and she never imports his prompt; Gary's behaviour is unchanged. `python3 -m ladder run` now plays the tree (`ladder/play.py`): each tick window it lifts blocks whose awaited state came and walks the tree; it asks the question only when the goal node or the watched state (`watch_lines`) changes, or, after an ask that got no answer, once the backoff and budget allow, never every tick (with no answer the tree's own op runs; `--no-planner` or `AGENTREALM_NO_PLANNER` for the test mode). An op whose x, y is the cell she goes to (`play.CELL_OPS`: `travel`, `fetch_item`, `enter_level`) is walked to over known ground on this map and is done once she stands there, never walked back to; an `explore_area` or `gather_gems` x, y names a region, not a cell. That execution was minimal (Stan's sync, wake and flee, the question asked on the tick thread, so a call that hung stalled play up to the provider client's timeout, 600 s in the `anthropic` SDK) until A141 replaced it with her own loop, executor and reflexes, the call off the tick thread. Tests: `tests/test_ladder_ask.py`; the A138 import line now loads every `ladder` module. | A139 |
| A141 | **Executor and reflexes.** Shipped: `python3 -m ladder run` plays on her own loop over the ADK (`ladder/play.py`), with no tie to Stan's: one call a window (`play.choose_call`: self, position, terrain, minimap, entities, else a round trip), and each round trip her reflexes first, then the walk under way, then the op for the tree's goal. **The executor:** `ladder/act.py` maps each op to its walk and intents (`act.OPS`, one table: `travel`, `enter_level`, `fetch_item`, `buy`, `equip`, `gather_gems`, `explore_area`, `fight`, `fight_boss`; every op the tree may answer with has its entry). Every walk is the ADK's `pathing.guided_step` (the A90 stuck ladder, give-ups and backoff, A125's net progress), sent as a paced `Step` queue (`executor.build_paced_walk_queue`, cut off the path as it is queued, never past a door) and held while it runs and the op is unchanged; a swing is paced by the armed weapon's cooldown (`executor.build_attack_queue`, A153), a drink or an `Arm` + `Use` by `arm_then_use`. An op's walk keeps off every known hostile's ground (`hostile_ground.walk_reach`, A128) but the ones in view a fight with which fits her risk budget now (`readiness.fits_now`), and a fight's own target. Each op reports when it is over (carried out, or nothing left it can do: a seller searched without the item, a walk given up, nothing left to explore); an op over is not run again for that goal and the tree's op stands in, and once that one is over too the goal is blocked with why to `BLOCK_TICKS`, as a `cannot` blocks it, so the walk moves on. Every swing arms the weapon to fight with first when another is in hand (`act.swings`, `equip.weapon_to_arm`, A99). Every send, a reflex's or an op's, goes through the one water gate Gary's do (`fording.ford_gate`, A133: `LadderRunner.gate`, no rearm in front of flee or retreat). An op she cannot carry out as given (`act.refuse`: not an op she runs, or a field it needs missing, such as an `enter_level` with no x, y) is over at once with why, and a planner reply naming one is a failed call with that why logged, so the tree's op runs. What replaces a running queue is checked by the ADK's held-queue rules (`held_queue.unconfirmed_send`, `open_step` under attack, A116, A128). Lorraine imports nothing of Gary's states: the few intent shapes she sends are hers (`act.take`, `arm`, `wear`, `remove`, `use_on`, `use_block`, `use_self`). **Reflexes** (`ladder/reflexes.py`, in order, the first that fires holds the round): sync (asleep, a `Wait` wakes her; unplaced, she holds), downed, flee (a hostile in range and the margin short: `pathing.flee_step` toward the refuge, then `flee_run`; cornered, a drink, else a swing at the hitter), retreat (hostiles that can reach her, none in range, the margin short: a walk to the refuge). Flee and retreat share one refuge, `pathing.retreat_safe_goal`: the nearest safe block she gets to before any hostile, each priced as a chaser (A162); her walks keep off no hostile ground on a known safe block (`hostile_ground.off_safe`), heal (in a fight, health alone no longer pays for it but the heals carried do: the heal that wastes least), and fight back (hit from her weapon's reach with the margin paying for it: she swings rather than walking on taking hits). Only sync and downed hold the round with nothing to send, and their hold stops a queue another sent; any other reflex with nothing to send, or cut to nothing by the water gate, yields to the next and then to the op, so she never stands taking hits for want of a move (A114). Every one reads one number, her margin now (`readiness.margin_now` on `readiness.Here`, the hostiles that can reach her, `readiness.foes_here`, valued as the goal she works), never a threshold of its own. A reflex never sets a goal: it leaves the op, the blocks and the last ask as they were, and the next walk of the tree decides. **The planner off the tick thread:** the one question (A140) runs on its own thread (`ask.Planner.start`, `Call`, `Planner.finish`): the messages are built and the budget charged on the tick thread, only the provider's call runs off it, and the reply is taken up once it is back; until then the tree's own op runs, and one about a goal the walk has left is let go. A slow or hung call never holds up a reflex (the 600 s worst case A140 noted is gone); a hung one only keeps a second question from going out. The A138 import line now also fails if her closure loads `starter_agent`, and checks it loads `agentrealm_adk.executor` and `ladder.reflexes`. Tests: `tests/test_ladder_act.py` (each op's walk and intents; a reflex preempts and hands back without setting a goal; the reflexes read `risk_budget`; a hung planner call does not block a flee; a walk onto water arms the ford first, and a reflex the water gate cuts to nothing yields; a retreat with no step yields; a planner op she cannot carry out is refused with why), `tests/test_ladder_ask.py` `PlayTest` (the ask off the tick thread). | A139, A140 |

A finding from a run goes into a predicate or into the planner's question (A87); never a branch. | A141 |

| A144 | **A travel op to an entrance finishes when he goes through it.** In a free-play run Travel walked the character through the lake door into a level, and the `travel` op to that entrance stayed on top: on the new map Travel asked the planner `no_way_on` (`travel:entrance …: no step toward it`) again and again, because the entrance's cell is on the map he had just left. Rule (A87), one test for every op that walks to a door: (1) the runner records each door gone through when the position read shows the warp landed (`WorldModel.passage`: the door's map and cell, the map it led to, the tick), with or without a knowledge base; (2) an op that walks to an entrance (`passage.entrance_of`: a `travel` to an `entrance`, `0, 0` matching any door; a `travel` to a `point` on a door cell; `enter_level`'s door) is done once that door was gone through since the op reached the top (`Plan.top_since_tick`) and the character is still on the map it led to (`passage.went_through`); `plan.goal_done` pops a `travel`, and Level, which walks the level it entered, finishes `enter_level` when the entrance led onto no level. Going through some other door is no arrival; (3) a cell on a map the character is not on is not answered with `no_way_on` while a way off this map is left to try: every walk that plans across maps (`navigation.walk_toward`, Travel's and Shop's walk) takes the known route (`route_first_leg`), else the way off this map (`navigation.way_off_map`: a door whose warp is not known yet, else the nearest frontier, leaving out doors (`level:door`) and frontier the stuck ladder gave up and is still backed off from, A90; without a knowledge base no warp is recorded, so no door is taken), and the stuck ladder and way-clearing run on that leg as on any other. It is bounded: a door gone through has its warp recorded and is never the way off again, and the frontier runs out, so a map no door leads to ends in the usual yield, one `no_way_on`, and the A34 stall that drops the op; (4) an op that names no map (a `point` with no `map_id`, `enter_level`) means the map the character was on when it reached the top (`Plan.top_map_id`, head progress beside `top_since_tick`), so the same cell on another map is no arrival. Tests: `test_entrance_passage` (through the op's door onto its map finishes it, through another door, from the same cell on another map, before the op reached the top, or off the map the door led to does not; an op naming no map means the map it reached the top on; the nearest entrance is done through any door and a point on a door through it; in the sim, Travel through the entrance pops the op with no `no_way_on`; through another door the op is kept and Travel steps toward the door off the map it landed on, asking nothing; `way_off_map` takes a door not yet gone through first, leaves out a given-up door and given-up frontier and then finds no way, and takes no door without a knowledge base; `enter_level` through an entrance onto no level is finished); `test_navigation_stuck` (a walk to a map no known warp reaches heads for a door not yet gone through; with only a door whose warp is known it explores the ground left, never through that door, then asks once and the op is dropped). | A26, A27, A114, A120 |
| A146 | **A walk inside a level crosses water exactly as on the overworld; afloat with only the ford in hand the engagement never says fight and Fight never takes to the water; a hostile's reach that is the only way is passed when health covers it.** In a level map that was almost all water with sand (dirt) islands, the character stood on water with the raft armed; a planner `travel point x,y` to an island cell came back `no_way_on` "no step toward it", and a hostile hit him there while he never swung: the raft holds the armed slot, the sword cannot be armed on water (`would_strand`), and the engagement priced the raft-in-hand as the starting knife, so it said fight. Rule (A87): (1) whether water is ground is one test, `WorldModel.walkable`, which honours `fords` (A133) on any map, a level's or a room's as much as the overworld's; every route search, grid and step check asks it: the cost grid (`planner._Grid`), `known_prefix` (now over the world), the stuck ladder's blocking types, the held queue's lone Step (`now=True`: water only `afloat`), Recover's approach on another map (`map_id`), Gather's ground count; `CostGridParams.fords` is gone, and `door_graph`'s scratch worlds carry the character's loadout (held, armed, worn) so their `walkable` fords as his does; the worn codes also mean the cost grid's hazard price on another map now takes off the armor worn, as it does here (`occupy_damage` less `worn_armor_defense`); a search or loop over many cells takes `WorldModel.walkable_test` once, so whether water is ground is worked out once per search, not per cell; `walkable_block` is used only inside `world.py`, and a map view's `walkable` and `frontier` take `fords` as a required keyword, so any call of them elsewhere shows. (2) Standing on water with only a fording supply in hand (`fording.swings_nothing`: Ashore's afloat test `on_water`, a would_strand-marked cell included, and `stranded_unarmed`) the win estimate counts no swing (ratio 0), so the engagement's decision is no fight, and a fight decided ashore does not hold there (`engagement.fight_holds`); Ashore makes for land (A133), Flee or Retreat act if it finds none, and once ashore the engagement decides again and Fight arms the weapon (A133). Tests: `test_fording` (`LevelWalkTest`: a level-map walk over water with the raft armed takes a step, the same walk with no ford asks `no_way_on`; `AfloatEngagementTest`: afloat with the raft and a hostile adjacent the engagement is not fight and dispatch neither fights nor swings; ashore the same hostile is fought; a fight decided ashore does not hold once afloat); `test_fording.WaterCellsOnlyTest` (a cell known as water only from a refusal counts as afloat to the engagement); `test_architecture.OneWaterTestTest` (outside `world.py` no module uses `walkable_block` or `WALKABLE`, passes `fords`, which every map-view call must, or passes its own water test to `walkable`). The reproduced failure is the live trace (map 44), a hostile's reach shutting the only route: on the sand under the door he armed the sword, then Fight armed the raft again to close on a bogmaw standing on a sand islet and drifted onto open water, took a hit, and from there Travel to the door's sand asked `no step toward it`, blocked by that bogmaw: its reach (A128) shut the only water route back. So also: (3) Fight and Boss close only over ground they can swing from (`fight.close_step`, `off_safe_step`, `WorldModel.walkable` `swing`: water only while a fording supply is worn), so no fording supply is armed to walk in on a hostile; (4) a hostile's reach that is the only thing between a walk on an op's behalf and its goal may be passed (`pathing.HostileReach`, the typed reach `op_walk_sets` returns, asked by `enters_reach` with the walk's own goal: `guided_step`'s leg target at every level, a reveal step included, and `bounded_step`'s target; a walk that names no goal never passes): when it is proven that no plan that keeps out of the reach gets to that goal (`HostileReach.only_way` through `navigation.shut_off`: either end's ground outside the reach closes within `FINE_NODE_BUDGET` without the other; a search the budget cuts off proves nothing, so the walk asks; the goal left out of the reach so a goal inside it is still reached round; one search per position and goal), and `covers_pass` holds. Flee and Retreat run as now: a pass happens only on a walk the engagement does not say to flee from, so none while the engagement says flee and none that comes within `hostile_range` of a hostile it passes (there the engagement, which would not fight it, says flee: a pass through the range would have Flee step back out and Travel step in again until the A136 guard benched one); the hits the hostiles in view are expected to deal on the pass at our movement speed (`executor.movement.ticks_per_step`, `survival.expected_damage`) must leave health above Retreat's floor against them (`survival.health_floor`); a remembered hostile's ground, which cannot be weighed, is never passed, and otherwise the walk asks, as before. (5) The level's 140 `no known way` asks over 1550 s, from open water to island cells 1 to 6 cells off while only long straight water lines ever moved him, were no water test at all: the level is dark, sight there is perception scaled by brightness, so a terrain read spans the perception window with `?` for every cell not yet revealed, and `WorldModel.apply_terrain` recorded every `?` in the window as VOID, no ground, known and never walkable; only the cells revealed round the lines he had walked were ground. The API says a `?` is unknown, never empty ground: a `?` is now VOID only past the map's edge (the read's `map_width` and `map_height`, else the size a minimap or an earlier read gave, `WorldModel.map_sizes`, else below x 0 and y 0), and one inside the map stays unknown, so a walk plans through it at fog cost and Explore goes to see it. Replayed through real terrain reads of the live map (`test_fording.LiveLevelMapTest`: lit and dark, level read and not, from (13, 1) to (7, 1) and to (8, 10)), the dark cases fail before this change and step after it; `test_brain` (clouds inside the map stay unknown; past the edge no ground). More tests: `test_fording.FightClosesOnlyOnLandTest` (the raft held, the sword armed, a hostile across water with no way by land: no walk in, no Fight, and the raft armed only by an escape); `test_fight_flee_no_flip` (Fight's walk in crosses water only with the raft worn); `test_hostile_reach.OnlyWayPassTest` (a covered pass through the margin of a hostile's reach is walked to the end with no Flee between; health that does not cover it asks; a pass that would come within the hostile's range asks; with a way round it never passes; a goal inside the reach is no only way while a way round reaches it; a remembered hostile's ground is never passed; no pass while the engagement says flee) and `CoversPassTest` (a cell held by no hostile in view, every hostile crossed counted, health left exactly at the floor refused). | A87, A94, A128, A133 |
| A147 | **Free-play run: a level's clue names the item it needs; he gets it first, and a worn ford beats an armed one.** He entered a water level with only a raft. The raft holds the armed slot, so on the water he could not swing and took hits. The knowledge base held the clues "The lake door wants dry feet or wet ones. A raft gets you there. Sandals get you through." and "Folk say there are sandals too, made somewhere hot, that walk on water." (the pond fisher's line, `docs/observations/A67_free_play_runs.md`), and he went in anyway. Water-walking sandals are worn, and they also work armed (GAME_NOTES **While-equipped pieces work armed too**); worn, they leave the weapon armed. Rules (A87): (1) **a level's needs come from its clues.** Every clue already reaches the planner (A32, A35); its reply's new top-level `entrance_needs` key (`[{x, y, map_id, item, why}]`, `item` a code or a capability such as `fords`, `null` to clear) records the need on that known entrance's `kb.entrances` row as `clue_needs`, with the planner's reason as `clue_needs_why` (`entrance_needs.record_needs`; its own field, so a look's `needs`, which only a look writes and M9's `clear_entrance_looks` clears, is never overwritten or erased by the planner, and a look's clear or refile leaves the clue's need, applied in `Strategist._settle` before the goals, so the same reply's `enter_level` meets it); one on no known entrance is refused with why. No regex reads the clue: the contract tells the planner to record a need when a clue ties a level or its door to an item or capability. State `level_entrances` shows `needs`, `clue_needs`, `clue_needs_why` and `need_met`. (2) **Get the need first.** An entrance where either recorded need is one nothing held, stowed, armed or worn meets (`entrance_needs.unmet_need`: the item, any potion for a potion, or a supply with the capability) is not entered. A reply's `enter_level`, or `travel` to that entrance by x, y, is left out with why (`entrance_needs.refuse_unmet`, said through `last_reply_rejected`), except an `enter_level` whose item lies in sight, which Level takes first (its A71 prerequisite stop, which now serves any recorded need, a look's `key` on a locked door as well as a clue's item); Level drops an `enter_level` that reaches the top with the need unmet and no stop for it, and while a stop waits its walk stops short of the door; Travel has no stop, so a `travel` to such an entrance is refused even with the item in sight, and Travel drops a `travel` whose destination is one (by x, y, or the nearest door it picks for `0, 0`) or whose next step lands on one. The why and the contract tell the planner to get the item: buy it from a known seller (A131, State `sellers`), follow a clue to a seller or to where it is made ("made somewhere hot" points to hot ground: lava or fire on the map, a region named for fire or a volcano), or explore for one. (3) **A worn ford beats an armed one** (`equip.ford_to_put_on`): before the first water step, `fording.ford_queue` wears a held fording supply with a worn slot (a `Remove` first when the slot is taken), so the weapon stays armed and Fight runs on water as on land; one that goes in the armed slot (the raft) is armed only when no wearable one is held. The planner's `equip` op naming the raft wears the wearable one instead (`equip.worn_ford`), and Equip's own upgrade wears it into an empty slot. Tests: `test_entrance_needs` (a clue's need set by the reply lands on the entrance and in State, in its own field; a clue's need keeps M9's `entrance_row_recorded` passing on a locked door, survives `clear_entrance_looks` with its why, and is still read after a look refiles `needs`; the planner's `null` never erases a look's key, and a new need with no why drops the old why; a `travel` is refused even with the item in sight, and one on the stack is dropped once the need is recorded, for a named entrance, the nearest door picked for `0, 0`, and a point walk whose step lands on the door; a need on no known entrance is refused; null clears one; `enter_level` and `travel` to the entrance are refused while the need is unmet and allowed once it is held; the reply's own need refuses its own `enter_level`; Level drops the op, walks once the item is held, and takes it on the way when it lies in sight; with sandals and a raft held the queue and the `equip` op wear the sandals and keep the sword armed; on water with the sandals worn the sword may be armed; with only the raft it is armed as before); `test_commit_target` (a locked door's key stop given up no longer walks on to the door). | A32, A35, A71, A87, A131, A133 |
| A145 | **Levels are taken in order** (Evan: he does not try level N+1 until he has beaten level N). The minimap marks entrances with no level number, so a number is learned only by going in. The level comes only from the minimap: `GET position` carries none (GAME_NOTES Levels), so `WorldModel.map_level` is the current map's `level` from its minimap entry (`WorldModel.apply_minimap`, 0 for a map outside every level); the position read is never read for one (A164), and A37's level test reads this since A145. The minimap is read at startup and once a door step lands on a map whose level is unread (`level_order.minimap_due`, one call in that window, at most `MINIMAP_TRIES` (2) per map, then given up: the map's level stays unknown); the planner's `map` trigger waits for that read (`level_pending`), so it fires once, with the level. The step is held (`Memory.level_entry`, `note_door_step`) until the level is read; a step in from outside every level then records it on the entrance's `kb.entrances` row (`level`, `settle_entry`). The target is the lowest level not in `levels_cleared` (`level_order.target_level`; a clear ceremony's `level_number` counts at once). Entrances are tried in one order (`level_order.entrance_rank`): one known to be the target, then the unknown ones nearest first, then cleared ones; one known to be a higher uncleared level is never chosen while a lower uncleared level exists. Inside a level above the target the character leaves at once: `level_order.sync_leave` puts `leave_level` on top of the stack, the agent's own op (`plan.INTERNAL_OPS`, never in the planner's op table nor taken from a reply), and never pushes it again while one is on the stack or while it is remembered as stalled, so the A104 stall rule moves or drops it like any op. Level walks it to `level_order.way_out`: a door seen whose known warp lands on a map read as outside every level; else, on the map a known entrance's warp landed on (`landing_cell`, from the warps the knowledge base keeps, so it holds across restarts), the door nearest that landing not known to lead deeper into a level (a warp onto a map whose level is unread is unknown, not out, so it is taken only here, when no door is known to lead out), or the landing cell while none is seen; with neither it asks the planner and holds (`way.clear_the_way`), never guessing by proximity alone. It is done once the map's level is read as 0 (`goal_done`): an unread level is unknown, not out. Every map level a minimap read names is kept in the knowledge base (`knowledge_maps.record_map_levels`), so it holds across runs. No exploring, loot or boss on the way; the reflexes act above it as always. The order reaches the planner as State `level_target` and each `level_entrances` row's `level` and `order` (target, unknown, cleared, barred), one line in the arc, and a reply's `enter_level` or `travel` to a barred entrance is left out with why (`level_order.refuse_barred`, `last_reply_rejected`); Level drops a barred `enter_level` that reaches the top. The fallback that picks a door (`door_graph.doors_goal_path`) takes the known target first, then an unvisited door, then any, never a barred one. Tests: `test_level_order.py` (leaving, level 4 entered with nothing cleared, a repeated or stalled leave, the way out with and without a known way in, the order, the barred `enter_level` drop, refusals), `test_door_graph.RunnerWarpRecordingTest` (the minimap names the level a door step entered; `MINIMAP_TRIES`), `test_strategist.TriggerTest` (the map trigger waits for the level). | A37, A104, A107 |
| A148 | **The planner reads x and y from State, not from the map ruler.** In a free-play run on a 16×12 level map the planner sent `travel point 17,1` for a door at x 7: the State map's ruler wrote each x's digits down its column, three rows deep, and two-digit columns that share digits across rows were misread. The walk then hit a wall. Shipped (A87: rules, not a patch for that door): (1) every cell the planner may target is in a State list with its `x`, `y` outright. New: `doors_seen` (`strategist.door_lines`), every door seen on this map nearest first, the one under the character included (`here`, as on a level's door on arrival), each with `block`, `locked`, `cells`, `dir` and `step_from`, the ground beside it a walk steps onto it from; with no cap, since doors are few; and `on_the_ground` (`strategist.ground_lines`), the nearest `GROUND_SHOWN` (8) items, gems, items for sale and chests in view, with `of=` counting all in view, as the contract says. NPCs (hostiles among them), signs, level entrances, sellers, food, oddities and death chests already were. (2) The contract tells the planner to take every x, y it targets from those lists, never by counting columns in the drawing. (3) The ruler gives every fifth column's whole x on one line, from that column rightward, with a `|` under its first digit (`strategist.ruler_lines`); a label that would run into the one before is left out, never cut short. Each row already carries its whole y. The map stops at the map's edge once a terrain read has given its size (`WorldModel.map_size`, A146's `map_sizes`, filled by minimap and terrain reads), and its head gives the whole map's range. (4) One check on every reply, before its ops reach a state (`cells.refuse_bad_cells`): an op whose cell on the current map is off its edge, or a wall where a `travel` or `fetch_item` would stand, is left out with why, said through `last_reply_rejected`. A map of unknown size refuses nothing as off it. Only the ops that run on the current map are judged: those before the first that may leave it (`cells.leaves_map`: any op not in `cells.STAYS_ON_MAP_OPS`, so `enter_level`, `leave_level`, `fight_boss` and `buy`, and a travel to another map, a town, a hunting ground or a door; judged last, after every other refusal; the pinned ops ahead counted, and the `leave_level` that goes on top by itself inside a level above the target); the ops after it pass, and their walk finds out there. Tests (`tests/test_planner_cells.py`): the ruler's labels on a 16-wide and an 800-wide window, a level door (under the character too) in `doors_seen` with x, y, every door listed past eight, the map size taken from a terrain read (and kept by one without it), and off-map and wall targets refused with the reason. | A114, A72 |
| A149 | **The agent ADK.** Shipped: the shared plumbing moved (with `git mv`) out of `python/agentrealm_agent/` into `python/agentrealm_adk/`: the API client, `WorldModel`, the knowledge base, pathing and navigation, the executor, travel, the reflex helpers, the reference data (`reference/`), and every other module the A138 import line marked as plumbing. Gary keeps his planner (`strategist`, `plan`, `brain`, `cells`, which reads `plan`), `runner`, `states`, `shop`, his `__main__` and his milestone acceptance gates (`acceptance*`, `m*_acceptance`). No re-export stubs: Gary (`agentrealm_agent`), Lorraine (`ladder`) and Stan import from `agentrealm_adk` directly. The command line both agents shared moved into `agentrealm_adk.cli`: the server flags and `create` (`parser`), the profile operand and character flags (`profile_parser`), and `status`'s self line (`print_self`, `status`); each agent's `__main__` adds its own `run` and `status`. `Memory.detour` no longer names Gary's `Detour` type, so no ADK module imports an agent even for a type checker. The A138 architecture test gained `tests/test_architecture.py` `ImportLineTest.test_the_adk_imports_neither_agent`, and its Lorraine check now fails on any `agentrealm_agent` module in her closure (`test_ladder_loads_nothing_of_garys`): every ADK module imported in a fresh interpreter loads no `agentrealm_agent`, `ladder` or `starter_agent` module, and no ADK source has an import naming one. The run-citation count (A87) and the one-water-test check (A146) scan both packages, and the A91 executor check scans `agentrealm_adk/executor/` and fails if it finds no source. Behaviour unchanged; `make test` runs 2774 tests, `main`'s 2773 plus the ADK check. | A138 |
| A150 | **ADK interfaces.** Once Gary and Lorraine both exist and have been measured (A142), extract what both have grown into the ADK (`python/agentrealm_adk/`): a state tree (Gary's states, Lorraine's goal tree), a shared blackboard (what each decision reads and writes), a planner hook (the one place a situational call is asked) and a reflex tier (survival reflexes above every goal, reading one risk function). Each agent then implements the interfaces instead of carrying its own version. The blackboard also takes `Memory.detour`: the ADK's `Memory` holds Gary's Detour find typed `object | None` since A149, so the ADK names no agent; give it a real type there (the record on the agent's side, or a `Protocol` in the ADK). | A142 |
| A151 | **A hostile's ground that blocks every way is passed or cleared, seen or remembered.** In a dark level that was mostly water (map 44, 16 × 12, all 192 cells known, none `?`), he stood on water with the raft armed at 10/10; one bogmaw sat on an island in the middle. Every walk to either exit door, and to the tile cells by them, came back `no step toward it`, blocked_by "no known way: walls, no ground or unseen ground", tried "a way round", the internal `leave_level` walk ("no path out of the level") included; only water legs away from the bogmaw moved him, and every blocked target lay within about 5–6 cells of it. Cause, reproduced on the live layout (`test_blocking_hostile_ground`, failing before this change): the bogmaw's ground (A128 `walk_reach`) was its walk reach plus the post it keeps, grown by the hits it dealt from off its post (`Sighting.reach`, up to `POST_REACH_CAP`), and that post ground covered every way to the door (7, 0). A146's pass never fired: `covers_pass` accepted only cells within `hostile_range` plus the margin of a hostile in view, so post ground beyond that, seen or not, and all of a remembered hostile's ground were never passed; and `ask.what_blocks` named a hostile in view only by its range and a remembered one only out of view, so a hostile in view whose post shut the way was named by nothing. Rules (A87, A120): (1) **the ask names the blocker**: `blocked_by` names each known hostile whose ground (`hostile_ground.walk_ground`, the one per-hostile picture `walk_reach` is built from) the way crosses, with its cell: `hostile <code> <id> at x,y` in view, `remembered hostile <code> <id>, last seen at x,y, out of view, holding ground round x,y` out of it, never the generic "no known way"; (2) **the way is cleared**: when that ground is the only way (`HostileReach.only_way`), every op walk, the internal `leave_level` walk included, (a) passes it when health and the heals carried cover the hits expected on the pass and leave it above Retreat's floor (`covers_pass`, A146's bar; held heals count toward a pass only where they can be used, `pathing.heal_on_pass`: none on a pass with a water cell unless the fording supply is worn, since an `Arm` there would strand (A133), each costing its `Arm` and its `Use`'s cooldown in ticks of hits, and never above the health the pass starts with; priced on the same `walk_ground` the reach was built from), the ground of a remembered hostile counted as a seen one's, priced from the threat table (its largest hit known, else the published roll), and never within `hostile_range` of where a hostile crossed is known to stand (in view where it stands; out of view where it was last seen and its post); (b) else, when the one hostile shutting the way is in view, not forbidden, and the engagement would fight it from a land cell within its range (`engagement.would_fight` with `at`, that cell, so the decision it makes on landing is fight; the landings tried nearest first, and searched only while the way is not known shut, `Memory.way_shut`), reached with no step on water within that range, the executor's way-clearing walks there (`way.land_to_fight`, its own `bounded_step` walk under `LAND_GOAL`), and ashore the engagement takes it, Fight arming the weapon (A133); only with neither does it ask, the hostile named; (3) **a remembered hostile's ground still bars ordinary routing**: it stays in the reach and priced costly, and a walk with a way round keeps to it; only the sole-blocker case changes. Tests: `test_blocking_hostile_ground` on the live layout (doors (7, 0) and (7, 11), islands at (4–6, 3–5), (11–12, 4–6) and (6–8, 8–10), tile at (7, 1), raft armed, 10/10, bogmaw at (6, 5) holding a post it came 4 out from, he at (11, 10)): seen or remembered, the walk to either door gets there; `leave_level` reaches the door; at low health it asks, naming the bogmaw; heals carried on water with the raft only armed do not count; a pass not covered heads for land beside the bogmaw with no water step within its range, and landed, Fight arms the sword; `land_to_fight` gives none for a forbidden hostile, a remembered one, two crossed, or no land in range; with neither it asks; `what_blocks` names a hostile the engagement would fight when its range reaches the way, and a remembered one with where it was last seen; with a way round, remembered ground is kept out of; `test_hostile_reach` (a remembered post is never passed within its range; blocked_by names a remembered hostile with where it was last seen; `CoversPassTest`: a cell no known hostile holds is never covered; held heals count only where they can be used). Observations: `docs/observations/A67_free_play_runs.md`. | A87, A120, A128, A133, A146 |
| A152 | **Sync the game notes and reference to the published manual.** The Manual now states the core play rules (2026-10-12): where a supply works (armed, worn, carried; the raft armed only), what waits on a cooldown, food and potion heals, no natural regeneration, the chest on death, levels in any order, boss timers and clear rewards, hostiles, safe zones, regrowth and oddities, shops. (1) `docs/GAME_NOTES.md` cites every fact the Manual states (section and sub-heading) instead of Obs; observations stay only where the docs are silent, and say so; what the Manual contradicted is corrected (hostiles do follow, a miss shows `NPCAttacked`, food is never `Use`d, a dropped priced item costs its price on any cell, oddities drop unless a cache lies there); the open questions it answered move to an answered table. (2) The bundled reference is refreshed (`supplies.json` unchanged); `scripts/refresh_planner_reference.py` keeps a paragraph that opens a list item on its marker's line. (3) [`docs/observations/A152_manual_sync.md`](docs/observations/A152_manual_sync.md) lists every place the code contradicts or works around a stated rule, with file:line and the Manual's line. (4) Fixed here, mechanical only: the planner contract's health sources (a boss clear refills; no carried food), the death line (the kit is kept, the chest resets to 10), the drop line (a priced item costs its price again wherever it lies), the oddity line (any of the five; no drop while a cache lies there); docstrings on the armed supply never in `held`, `Drop`, pacing, oddities, regrowth and the opener table. Behaviour changes are filed as A153–A164 (the follow-ups F1–F12 in [`docs/observations/A152_manual_sync.md`](docs/observations/A152_manual_sync.md), in order), not done here. Tests: the existing suite; `test_planner_reference.py`'s sentinel no longer collides with the Manual's text. | A54, A62 |
| A153 | **Only a weapon swing waits on the attack cooldown.** Shipped. Manual §11 What waits on a cooldown: only a weapon `Use` that rolls against a character or NPC, or breaks a block, waits; every `Use` of a consumable, tool or trap, and `Arm`, run with no cooldown; a weapon `Use` that changed nothing spends nothing. Rule: a `Use` is paced by the armed weapon's cooldown only when it is a weapon's swing or break (`executor.pacing.is_weapon_swing`: a `Use` not on yourself with a weapon in hand, nothing in hand or an unlisted code counted as a weapon; `use_cooldown_ticks` gives its Waits, 0 for any other `Use`): the runner's single `Use` and `states/intents.arm_and_use` (a drink, a chest, Gather's cutter) take it, so a drink, a chest, a tool or trap `Use` and the `Arm` before it go out on the next tick; Fight's and Boss's attack queues are swings and keep the weapon's cooldown. `last_use_tick` moves only on an `applied` weapon `Use`, judged with the code in hand when it ran (`supply_refusals.code_in_hand`): never on a drink, a tool's `Use` or a swing that came back `applied_no_effect`. A refused self `Use` retries on its own constant (`supply_refusals.REFUSAL_WAIT_TICKS`, 10 ticks), not a weapon cooldown. A break costs its `Use`'s tick (`break_memory.BREAK_BASE_COST`, 1) plus the weapon's cooldown for a weapon (`break_step_cost`), so a tool's break costs no cooldown. A held heal on a pass costs its `Arm` and its `Use`, two ticks of hits, no cooldown (`pathing.heal_on_pass`). Tests: `test_executor_pacing.py` (`WhatWaitsOnACooldownTest`), `test_runner.py` (a `Use` that is no swing goes out on the next tick; only an applied weapon `Use` starts the cooldown), `test_heal.py` (a drink right after a swing), `test_break_memory.py` (`BreakStepCostTest`), `test_hostile_reach.py` (a drink on the pass costs two ticks). Findings 9. Manual sync, [`A152_manual_sync.md`](docs/observations/A152_manual_sync.md). | A123, A152 |
| A154 | **Each supply is in one place.** Shipped. Manual §11 Where your supplies are: each supply is in exactly one of `armed`, `worn`, `held`; `Drop` works on any held, armed or worn supply. Rule: retire the A76 checks for the armed supply in `held` (`healing.potion_count`, `supply_refusals.in_hand`, and `break_memory.held_supplies`, whose code dedup hides the armed supply when a held one shares its code); `loot.droppable_supplies` offers armed and worn supplies too; `fording._loadout_after` models a `Drop` of a worn one. Done: `healing.potion_count` counts the armed potion beside the held ones, `supply_refusals.in_hand` is held plus armed (Lorraine's `heal_now` reads it), and `break_memory.held_supplies` always lists the armed supply (id -1) beside a held one of its code, which `pick_supply_for_capability` prefers (`s.id < 0`). The snapshot's worn ids are kept (`WorldModel.worn_ids`, `item_table.worn_ids_from_inventory`), and `WorldModel.loadout_supplies` lists the armed and worn supplies with ids; `droppable_supplies` and `pack.reserved_supplies` take them in, so the plan's `fight_boss` `armed` is never offered as a drop. `fording._loadout_after` drops a worn one from its slot (and puts a removed one back in hand), so a `Drop` of the worn fording supply on water is cut. Tests: `test_supply_places.py`. Findings 10, 12. Manual sync, [`A152_manual_sync.md`](docs/observations/A152_manual_sync.md). | A76, A102, A152 |
| A155 | **Food is eaten on pickup, never carried.** Shipped. Manual §11 Food and potions: food heals at once on pickup, even at full health, and never takes room in the chest; a potion is used up even at full health. Rule: Heal's carried-food branch, food in `healing.held_heal` and the A24 heal learning retire, heals coming from the Supplies reference; food takes no pack room (`pack.make_room`); a `fetch_item` of food is done when taken; Pickup and the contract leave food lying until the character is hurt by at least its heal; no potion is sent at full health. Done: `healing.carried_heal` returns only a potion; `held_heal` and `heals_carried` count only potions, so Gary's `readiness` and Lorraine's `ladder/readiness.py` (which reads `held_heal`) agree; `note_heal_pending`, `absorb_heal_pending`, `Memory.heal_pending` and `item_table.merge_heal` are gone, and saved `heal_amount` and `heal_on_pickup` item keys are dropped at load (`knowledge_base.RETIRED_ITEM_KEYS`). One test of when food is worth taking, `healing.worth_eating` (hurt by at least its `supplies.heal_of`), read by `food_to_take` and `food_blocks` (so Heal, State `food_near` and `readiness` `eat`), Pickup (`pickup_outcome`) and Detour (`valuable`, `allowance`); A135's order stands: out of combat food near first, potions for fights and levels, and food is now "walk to it when hurt by at least its heal". `pack.make_room` takes anything eaten on pickup with no drop. The runner tells the plan of each `Take` that went through (`Plan.note_taken`), and a head `fetch_item` of food eaten on pickup is done once one did (`Plan.eaten`, head progress). `healing.spend_potion` never drinks at full health, so neither do Heal, Flee cornered, Retreat or the planner's `use_item` (`use_item.potion_refused` names why). The contract's `POTION_RULE`, `food_near`, `health_at_bar` and stage 2 lines say the same. Tests: `test_food_on_pickup.py`. Findings 13–16. Manual sync, [`A152_manual_sync.md`](docs/observations/A152_manual_sync.md). | A24, A102, A132, A152 |
| A156 | **Hearts come from the Supplies reference.** Shipped. Supplies reference: `heart`, class `life`, eaten on pickup. Rule: a supply of class `life` is a life (`loot.LIFE_SUPPLY_CODES` from the reference); `learn_life_code` and `life_on_pickup` retire; Detour's and Pickup's scores are checked for hearts on sight. Done: `supplies.is_life` (class `life` in the reference the run loaded) is the one life test, read by `loot.is_life_supply`; the `LIFE_SUPPLY_CODES` constant retires with `learn_life_code`, since the reference is reloaded at run start and a constant would freeze the bundled copy; saved `life_on_pickup` rows are dropped at load (`knowledge_base.RETIRED_ITEM_KEYS`). Scores checked: a heart scores `LIFE_SCORE`, above gems, so Pickup takes it first, with a full pack too (a counter, no slot), and Detour goes for one in view within `LIFE_STEPS`. | A157 | **A death resets the carried chest.** Shipped. Manual §11 How big your chest is, Death: every death resets capacity to the starting chest; a chest upgrade's `Use` is `applied` when it made the chest bigger and `applied_no_effect` when not. Rule: the applied chest clears on every death (`WorldModel.reset_carried_chest`): on `Died` (and on `Respawned`, for a run that saw only the respawn); and for a death the agent did not see, at load (`loot.load_carried_chest`): the record keeps the lives it was saved at (`kb.extra` `carried_chests`: `{"code", "lives"}`; a bare code saved before A157 loads with none), and the first lives read below them is a death since (`WorldModel.chest_lives`, checked once); a served `death_chest` the agent never knew of (not in `death_chests` or `death_chests_gone`) is one too. A chest `Use` on yourself sets it only when its result is `applied` (`Runner._note_applied_self_use`, `loot.learn_chest_upgrade`), never from the chest leaving the pack or a capacity compare; `applied_no_effect` is filed as a `Use` that did nothing (A76). Tests: `test_carry_capacity.py` (a death resets it; a served death chest never known; fewer lives than saved, from a self read or the first snapshot; the lives saved keep it; a pre-A157 row), `test_loot.py` (`RunnerLootLearningTest`: an applied chest `Use` raises capacity until a death, one `applied_no_effect` raises nothing). Findings 20–22. Manual sync, [`A152_manual_sync.md`](docs/observations/A152_manual_sync.md). | A108, A115, A152 |
| A158 | **A dropped priced supply costs its price again, anywhere.** Shipped. Manual §11 A price belongs to the supply: a priced subtype costs its `gem_price` wherever a copy lies on the ground, our own drop included. Rule: the pack rule prices a drop of a priced subtype as forfeiting its price on any cell (`pack.forfeit`: the drop's why and each choice in the planner's ask name the price given up); `pack.drop_makes_stock`, `pack.drop_spot`, `MOVE` and the step off a shop cell retire; a priced supply the character dropped is not recorded as a shop (`WorldModel.our_drops`, fed by each applied `Drop`, `is_our_drop`, kept across runs per character in `kb.extra` `our_drops`, the latest `OUR_DROPS_KEPT`, `travel.knowledge.load_our_drops` and `save_our_drops`, read by both callers of `travel.knowledge.record_shop_cell`: Gary's runner and Lorraine's `learn_shops`). Lorraine never sends a `Drop` or calls `note_sold_out`: her `learn_shops` guard and her buy's sold-out test read the facts Gary writes to the world's knowledge base; `LadderRunner` loads and saves `our_drops` through the same helpers, so a supply the character dropped under Gary is no shop to her. Tests: `test_pack_room`, `test_shop` `RunnerLearnsWhatAShopSellsTest`. Finding 24. Manual sync, [`A152_manual_sync.md`](docs/observations/A152_manual_sync.md). | A102, A131, A152 |
| A159 | **Shop stock returns.** Shipped. Manual §11 Regrowth, drops, and returns: shop stock returns on its home block 2 minutes after it is bought, hidden while another supply lies there. Rule: a shop cell seen without the item is sold out for that time, not forgotten (`travel.knowledge.forget_sale` becomes `note_sold_out`: the cell's `sold_out` keeps, per item, `back`, the sighting plus `RESTOCK_SECONDS`, 120, and `misses`); a sighting before `back` is the same sell-out, and one at or after it is a miss that starts a new window, since the stock could have returned and been bought again; the sale lapses only after `SALE_LAPSE_MISSES` (3) misses in a row, and a cell with another supply on it is hidden, which counts no miss; a sighting selling it clears `sold_out` (`record_shop_cell`). A sold-out shop stays a known seller (`sellers.Seller.back`, `in_stock`; State `sellers` shows `sold_out_until_tick`, and `no_seller_known` never lists its item), but no buy walks to it until it is back (Shop's `_sellers_to_walk`, Lorraine's `buy`), it is ordered after every seller in stock, and a buy whose every seller is sold out is refused (`refuse_unsold`) or dropped by Shop with the tick it is back (`sellers.sold_out_until`). A131's `not_sold` for a clue's or a buy's place is unchanged. Tests: `test_shop` `ShopStockReturnsTest`, `StaleSellerTest`. Finding 25. Manual sync, [`A152_manual_sync.md`](docs/observations/A152_manual_sync.md). | A110, A131, A152 |
| A160 | **An odd block may need any of the five capabilities.** Shipped. Rule: Break refuses a `break_block`'s capability for its type's known openers only on a block State `oddities` does not list (`states/break_state.py`); an oddity's `may_break_with` is every capability held; the contract's oddity line drops its caveat. Shipped: `oddities.is_oddity` (a cell State `oddities` draws from) lifts Break's known-openers refusal in `_op_refusal`, and `oddity_row` offers every held capability not failed there.
