| A161 | **Gather fells trees.** Manual §16 How to make money: a felled wild tree drops a gem at least as often as grass on the same ground (B148). Rule: with a chop capability held, a wild tree is a Gather target under the one price (A105), its yield at least grass's on that ground (`gem_yield`); else the contract stops saying "fell trees". Finding 32. Manual sync, [`A152_manual_sync.md`](docs/observations/A152_manual_sync.md). **Done:** while anything carried chops (`equip.CHOP`, picked by the one weapon rule, `equip.weapon_to_arm`; any sword, never the starting knife), `gather._targets` lists each wild tree (`gem_yield.FELL_BLOCKS`) on Gather's ground with a cell within the chopping item's reach to stand on (`Ground.stands`, `reach.supply_reach`, A116); the walk ends there (`Ground.goals`, `Ground.fell_reach`), and the fell is a `Use` on the tree with the chopping item armed in the same queue (`_cut` with `CHOP`). Its work is `GemOdds.work` at `gem_yield.fell_yield`: the map's felled trees and their gems (`kb.extra["gem_yield"]["<map_id>"]["fells"]`, counted by `record_cut` from the same gem attribution as a grass cut, `gem_yield.fell_totals`) pulled toward grass's yield on that ground as a region's cuts are toward the map's rate, and never below it (the Manual's floor), so trees and grass are weighed by the one price. A fell counts in the stall clock and `gather_run` like a cut (`gem_yield.WORK_BLOCKS`), is filed as a cut but never in a region's grass totals, and one with no effect holds its cell out unless made with an item known not to chop (`break_memory.cannot_cut` with `capability`). Status `felling a tree`, `walking to a tree`; `blocked by hostile` counts a tree a hostile bars as it does grass. The contract says "fell trees" only for when an item that chops is held. Tests: `test_gather.py` (`GatherFellsTreesTest`), `test_gem_yield.py` (`FellYieldTest`, the runner's chop no-effect path), `test_break_memory.py` (`cannot_cut` with `capability`), `test_equip.py` (`CHOP`). | A63, A105, A152 |
| A162 | **Hostiles are chasers and patrollers, not guards.** Manual §11 Hostiles, Combat: a hostile goes for the nearest living character on its map with no sight or distance limit; a chaser steps greedily and stops where no step closes in; a zone spawn never leaves its zone; a hostile never heals and never walks back to spawn; safety is judged on the exact block, where nothing hurts. Rule: the post-and-reach `Sighting` model gives way to the published one (a still hostile is blocked, not guarding); a hostile's damage taken is kept until it dies or respawns (`survival.UNKILLED_HOSTILE_HEALTH` only for one never hit); hazard and danger costs are zero on a known safe block (`hostile_ground.is_safe_ish`, `pathing`, `navigation/planner.py`). Findings 33–36. Manual sync, [`A152_manual_sync.md`](docs/observations/A152_manual_sync.md). Shipped: `Sighting` keeps where a hostile was last seen and whether it was blocked there (`still`: stood still `STILL_TICKS` in view, not next to us, with no step that would close in on us, `WorldModel.blocked`; merely standing still, or standing beside us swinging, is not blocked); `home`, `post`, `reach`, `strength` and `spells`, the post fade and `note_came_for_us` are gone, and an older agent's post row loads at its last-seen cell, not blocked. Out of view a hostile holds its bar round that cell: a moving one for `SIGHTING_TICKS`, a blocked one for `BLOCKED_SIGHTING_TICKS` (5 min), either forgotten once the cell is seen empty (`hostile_ground.held_by_hostile`); faded-post pricing is gone, and ground near a hostile is still priced (`near_reach`). Every hostile is priced as a chaser free to follow at our pace, since the agent sees neither its kind, its zone nor its speed (`hostile_ground.chase`, `chaser_step`, on `WorldModel.chaser_ground`: walkable or unseen, never water). A128/A146: `covers_pass` chases each crossed hostile along the walk; no pass where it comes within `hostile_range` (Flee's ground), and hits are counted while it is within its ground's radius, none on a safe block; so a chaser in the open is never passed, a blocked one is. A151: `land_to_fight` takes a landing only when the chase first brings the hostile into range on land, and the engagement would fight from that cell (`way._meeting`); a chaser that meets the walk on water is never landed for. Retreat's refuge pick: every hostile, not only those it runs from, holds only the cells it gets to before us. Kept damage: `WorldModel.npc_damage` sums every `NPCDamaged`, dropped on `NPCDied`, this run only; `survival.health_left` and `ticks_to_kill_them(…, taken)` price it in the engagement, Lorraine's `fight_cost`, M8's gate and State `nearby_npcs` `health_left`; a boss is priced as before (Gary at `UNKILLED_HOSTILE_HEALTH`, Lorraine scaled by its served health), its served health in Gary's estimate left to A163. Exact block: `is_safe_ish` is a known safe block, nothing more; the planner charges no hazard, danger or priced cost there, `plan_sets` avoids no hazard there, and `walk_ground`, `reach_cells` and Retreat's costly ground leave safe blocks out (`hostile_ground.off_safe`). Tests: `test_hostile_chase.py`; `test_blocking_hostile_ground.py` rebuilt on two maps (a wall keeps the bogmaw from the channel: passed; the land runs round to the channel: it lands where they meet), A146's island kept as the chaser that meets the walk on water; post tests in runs 5, 6, 7 and 9, `test_engagement`, `test_gather` and `test_hostile_reach` rewritten to the still model. | A94, A128, A152 |
| A163 | **The boss clock is counted, and a key is spent on every pass.** Manual §11 Bosses and levels: no read serves the boss fight's limit or time left (5 minutes when the level sets none, 90 s to 10 minutes in Olympuff); count from the tick the step through the door applied; leave through the room's exit door, and the boss keeps its damage; a locked door uses up its key on every pass. Rule: Boss's clock starts on the boss-door step against the level's limit kept in the knowledge base, and near it Boss leaves through the room's exit door; `boss_fight_end_tick` retires; `level_order.way_out` prefers a way out that spends no key, and a locked level the plan means to come back to reserves a spare. Findings 7, 37, 38. Manual sync, [`A152_manual_sync.md`](docs/observations/A152_manual_sync.md). Shipped: the clock lives in the ADK (`boss_clock`, `Memory.boss_clock`, set and cleared only by `states/boss.sync_boss`): it starts on a step through the current `fight_boss` op's door (`WorldModel.passage`, which now keeps the landing cell, `to_pos`) and runs while the character is alive and on the room's map and a `fight_boss` op is current (cleared on a defeat too); it keeps the room's doors seen (`note_doors`), so a dark room keeps its way out. The limit is the level's `kb.levels[<n>].boss_limit_seconds`, else `DEFAULT_LIMIT_SECONDS` (300); a death the clock explains is the limit's (`note_death`, from the runner): the character stood on the room's map before the round trip (the `Died` event's `map_id` is where the chest landed, outside the level), its `Damaged` hits, each with its amount, do not account for it, and it came within `MAX_LIMIT_SECONDS` (600) of the door step and no sooner than a limit already kept; its seconds since the door step are kept as that level's limit, and any other death leaves it unchanged. Boss leaves by the room's exit door (`exit_door`: a door of the room seen since the step in or kept in the knowledge base, never a locked one, one whose known warp leads back first, nearest the landing; with none known, back to the landing, `way_out`) when less is left than the walk there, priced by its path (`exit_steps`: an open walk kept on its inputs, position, target, blocked and occupied cells, the tiles' revision and fording, and reused only while the clock is past twice the leave margin; no open step is never kept), and `LEAVE_MARGIN_SECONDS` (10), and on Retreat's rule, both while a step toward it is open, else it fights on; with the clock running and no boss in view it leaves near the limit and otherwise holds. `boss_fight_end_tick`, `WorldModel.in_boss_fight` and `boss_fight_ticks_left` and `CLOCK_COMMIT_TICKS` are gone; `boss_fight_on` (Retreat and Flee stand down only while a counted clock has time left) and Heal's `in_combat` read the counted clock. A fight seen with no step through the op's door (a restart, the room entered by another door) assumes its clock started then, against the shortest limit (`MIN_LIMIT_SECONDS`, 90 s, or a lower one kept; `boss_clock.assume`), and learns no limit from a death. The boss keeps its damage: `survival.health_left` prices a boss at its served `health` (A162 left it to here), so Gary's estimate, Lorraine's `fight_cost` (no longer scaled by `max_health`) and a return after leaving all count it. Keys: `level_order.way_out` ranks a door that spends no key (locked in the knowledge base or in view) first; `pack.reserved_supplies` reserves a key (a supply that meets the entrance's recorded need) for every `enter_level` or `travel` through a locked entrance, so a plan that comes back through one keeps a spare (`locked_entrance_needs`); the contract's pack and boss lines say so. Tests: `test_boss.py` `BossClockTest` (the clock starts on the door step, the kept limit, a limit death learned and a boss's killing hit not, off the room's map it stops, a return restarts it and the boss keeps its damage, the op's end and a defeat stop it, a death the clock does not explain leaves the limit, a dark room keeps its doors, no door known walks back to the landing, the walk priced by its path and again when its inputs change, a fight with no door step assumes the shortest limit), `test_runner.py` (the runner hands a death to `note_death`), `test_ladder_goals.py` (Lorraine's `fight_cost` on a hurt boss), `RetreatCommitTest` (leaves by the exit door near the limit, the room's door not the op's), `test_level_order.py` (a way out that spends no key), `test_pack_room.py` (a key reserved per pass), `test_hostile_chase.py` (a boss at its served health). | A38, A145, A152 |
| A164 | **Retire the position read's level.** Shipped. Manual §5.3, §11 Bosses and levels: the position read and the snapshot's `position` carry no level; the minimap is the only read that numbers the map. Rule: `WorldModel.apply_position` stops reading a `level` (A37's branch). Done: `apply_position` reads no `level`; a map's level is its minimap entry's alone (`apply_minimap`). Tests: `test_level.py` (a level on a position read is never read), the level tests set levels by a minimap read. Finding 5. Manual sync, [`A152_manual_sync.md`](docs/observations/A152_manual_sync.md). | A145, A152 |
| A167 | **A death chest holds what he carried when he died.** Manual §11 Death, lives, and ending: a death drops everything held, armed, worn or stowed into a chest where the character fell, all but gems and the non-transferable starting kit, which are kept. Free-play run 28 ([`A67_free_play_runs.md`](docs/observations/A67_free_play_runs.md) **Run 28**, defect 5): State showed his death chest with `contents: "unknown"` on every call, so the planner never took it for a source and spent all 15 gems on a bronze sword the chest held. Rules (A87), in the ADK so Gary and Lorraine both get them: (1) on `Died` the chest's contents are what was carried then (`WorldModel.carried_at_death`: held, stowed, armed and worn, less the starting kit, `item_table.NON_TRANSFERABLE`, and any id a `Drop` was refused for; with `dropped_supply_ids`, a known id counts only when named there), marked expected (`DeathChest.seen` False); once it is seen in reach, what it holds replaces them (`seen` True). The knowledge base keeps `seen` (a row saved before it was seen). (2) Gary's State `death_chests` shows each item with its value: its gem price where one is seen for sale (`chest_sources.known_prices`) and `needed` with why (a buy on the stack, an upgrade over the best owned for its slot, potions below the stage 2 bar; `strategist.chest_item_need`), and `contents_are` expected or seen. (3) A death chest on this map holding an item is its source ahead of buying while the walk there takes no longer than earning the item's price, at the gather's measured rate, else `chest_sources.FIELD_GEMS_PER_MIN` (6; `chest_sources.source_for`). Gary: a reply's `buy` of it is left out with why while the chest is out of reach (`chest_sources.refuse_chest_held`), and the contract tells the planner to weigh the chest against buying. Lorraine: `readiness.missing` lists such an item under `chests` at no gems, for her entrance's need and for the level's risk, and "hold what it requires" and "ready for its risk" take it back with a `fetch_item` naming the chest (`chest_id`: walk next to it, `WithdrawFromChest`); she loads and saves the death chests across runs as Gary does. Tests: `test_death_chest_contents.py` (Died records the expected contents, kit excluded, `dropped_supply_ids` filtering; seen replaces expected; kept across runs; the State line; a buy refused while the chest is nearer than earning the gems, kept when not, in reach or on another map; the reply refusal in the strategist; Lorraine's node picks the chest, and her fetch withdraws). | A122, A131, A139 |

**Fight** (A23) swings at NPCs and characters when the engagement's decision is to fight (A94: the win estimate against `fight_margin`, the decision Flee and Retreat read too), using `Use` on the NPC by id (the server finds its block on the tick the swing runs, A45), with retreat steps queued behind the attack. Out of weapon reach it steps closer; with no open step closer it lets go and **Flee** runs.

The playable plan's strategist (M4) replaces the planner sketched in **Planner** above. Once M6 and M7 land, the playable plan's executor and state machine supersede **Scheduler**, **Reflexes** and **Plan** above, and the remaining one-intent queues in **Real time**; until then those sections describe the shipped agent. Since A5, `states.dispatch` picks the intents; `brain.decide` stays only as a shim over it that keeps the first intent as a `Decision`, until the runner sends a state's whole queue. The call budget is unchanged: one request per character per tick, burst 3.

## Tests

Standard library `unittest`, no server. They cover what the agent decides, not the wire: pathing, frontier choice, reflex order, and the scheduler's choice of call. `tests/test_starter_agent.py` covers the starter agent (A52) the same way: its call choice, Sync/Flee/Explore order, pathing around walls, resync, error handling, and the `create`/`run` commands. Run with `python -m unittest` from `python`. The suite runs against a temp state dir (`AGENTREALM_STATE_DIR`, set in `tests/__init__.py`) and fails if any test opened, wrote or removed anything under `python/.state` (A86).
